# Before release

Things in the tree that are for testing, or unfinished, or should not ship. Tick as you go.

## Test binds and commands — remove

- [ ] **Wind test.** `WindTest.cs` (whole file: `WindTest` + `/wind`). `PADKeybinds.Wind` and its `Load`/`Unload` lines. In `PisPlayer.ProcessTriggers` the `PADKeybinds.Wind` guard and `StepWind()`. `Keybinds.Wind.DisplayName` in the hjson.
- [ ] **Lightning bind (P).** `PADKeybinds.Lightning` and its lines; `PisPlayer.ProcessTriggers` guard and `ThrowLightning()`; `Keybinds.Lightning.DisplayName` in the hjson. The lightning *effect* and Rage Of A Thunder stay; only the free bind goes.
- [ ] **`/mors`** — `MorsCommand.cs`. Includes the `again` reset and the graveyard diagnostic line. Mors spawns himself (`MorsPresence`); the command is only for placing him.
- [ ] **`/megashark`** — `MegasharkCommand.cs`. The real summon is the Minishark (`MegasharkCall.cs`).
- [ ] **`/legends`** — `LegendsCommand.cs`. Legends is switched on by the Orb.
- [ ] **`/struct`** — `StructCommand.cs`. Worldgen structures place themselves.
- [ ] **`/magicarsenal`** — `MagicArsenalCommand.cs`, plus `MagicArsenal.Maxed`, `Max()`, `Normal()` and the `"maxed"` save key. Slots should only open by being earned.
- [ ] **`/dona`** — `DonaQuinnCommand.cs`, plus `DonaQuinnEncounter.Summon`, `Reset` and `Describe`. She comes by herself, once a world, on the Cleared Ground.
- [ ] **`/guideintro`** — `GuideIntroCommand.cs`, plus `GuideIntro.Again` and `Describe`. The Guide's first words play by themselves, once, in a world made with them.
- [ ] **`/abomanation`** — `AbomanationCommand.cs` (the call, and the words that make it do one move next: rush, lunge, puke, ash, vent, spasm, whip), plus `AbomanationHead.Force`, `forcedMove` and `forcedRush`. It is called by letting ten animals go on the Mile.
- [ ] **`/parry`** — `ParryCommand.cs`. Says what a parry makes of the enemy nearest the cursor and why; `/parry list` writes every kind of enemy and every solid shot to the log. Not a cheat: it changes nothing. It is the one way to see why the rules class another mod's enemy as they do, so it may be worth keeping for whoever fills in the config's lists. Keep or cut.
- [ ] **`/subworld`** — `SubworldCommand.cs`, plus what it alone sets: `SubworldKeeper.RulesLifted` (read by `SubworldRules.Kept`), `SubworldKeeper.Remake` (read by `SubworldPassage.Saved`) and `SubworldDoor.TakeUp`. A placed Unmarked Door can be taken up by nothing else and the item has no recipe: both want an answer before the command goes (PADWorkFlow §16.35). Since §16.79 also `gate` (where the waygate is), `gate raise` (`BeachWaygate.Raise`, which the world also runs by itself) and `ungate`, with `Waygate.TakeUp`: nothing else takes a waygate up (PADWorkFlow §16.79).
- [ ] **`/foigh`** — `FoighCommand.cs`, plus `Foigh.Summon`, `Foigh.ToSecond`, `Foigh.ToThird` and `Foigh.ToEnd`. Puts Foigh down 46 tiles ahead of whoever types it (nearer, or behind them, where there is no room); `/foigh second` leaves him two thirds of his life less one, so that the passage into his second phase begins on his next tick; `/foigh third` leaves him three tenths less one, for the passage into his third (from his first phase his second's passage is passed over); and `/foigh gone` takes him and his sword and knives away (what he has let go of in the air since §16.49 it leaves: that thins away by itself in a quarter of a second once he is gone). There is no word for one move of his: to see his third phase's three cuts, `/foigh third` and wait for them. Nothing else puts him anywhere yet (the entry on that, under Provisional): the command goes when something does Since §16.50 no more than 8 thousandths of his life can be taken from him in a second by anything that hits him; `/foigh second` and `/foigh third` set his life themselves and are not held to that. Since §16.55 `/foigh end` begins his end as the blow that would kill him begins it, from wherever he is in the fight (not on the road, and not between two phases: it says so), so that it can be seen without fighting him through (PADWorkFlow §16.43, §16.44, §16.48, §16.49, §16.50, §16.55).
- [ ] **`/skeletron`** — `SkeletronCommand.cs`, plus `LegendsSkeletron.Ask`, `Describe`, `MoveNames`, `Possible` and `forced`. What the nearest Skeletron is doing; `/skeletron <move>` makes that move of his the next (Legends; a hand move while he has the hands for it, one of his own once both are gone); `/skeletron hands` kills both his hands; `/skeletron red` puts the red hat on him or takes it off. His moves come at random without it.
- [ ] `Description` strings on any command that survives should not say "for testing".

## Should not be packed — `build.txt`

Add a `buildIgnore` so these stay in the repo but out of the `.tmod`:

```
buildIgnore = Claude outputs\*, Reference\*, *.md, *.py, *.aseprite, *.fx, Effects\Compiler\*, .git\*, .vs\*
```

Then check the built size dropped.

- [ ] `Claude outputs/` (previews, PDFs, reference gifs; an `.ogg` there is packed as a sound, and two files of one name and different kinds there stop the mod loading: §16.86)
- [ ] `spritecheck.py` in the root
- [ ] `PADWorkFlow_1.md`, `PAD_Lore_Planning.md`, `Terraria_Mod_Lore_Bible_ver_1.1.md`, this file
- [ ] `Content/NPCs/Mors/RoughIdea.png`, `Mors_LitPass.png`, `Sprite-0004.aseprite` — sources, not sprites the game loads
- [ ] `Effects/*.fx` and `Effects/Compiler/` — only the `.xnb` is read
- [ ] `.gitignore`: the `*.exe` / `*.msi` lines, so an installer never lands in a commit again
- [ ] `Content/Items/Armors/ArmorHelper.pdb`, `ArmorHelper_v1.zip`, `Terraria Sprite Transformer v1 - mac.jar` — tools beside the armour (seen §16.78); the `buildIgnore` in `build.txt` leaves out their `.exe`s and not these
- [ ] `Content/Sounds/Ambiance/SurfSample.ogg`, `WindSample.ogg` and `Hums/` (about 9.9 MB) — the recordings the Vast Ocean's pieces were cut from; only `Pieces/` is played (§16.85)

- [x] **The Guide's hut line** — `Hints.GuideHut`, said in his first words too, sends the player "seawards", but the Blood Shrine was built at the nearer end of the spawn's forest: in a Grand world the snow side, with the sea the other way (and the Piece of Paper says so). The shrine now goes on the sea's side (PADWorkFlow §16.21). Worlds made before keep it where it was.
- [ ] **The Guide's first words: numbers** — put down 15 tiles off, stops 3.5 blocks away, walks at 1.4 px/tick, "..." to move each line on. Mine, not asked for: confirm in play.
- [ ] **Amalgaermis's moves: numbers** — all mine but the ones asked for (5 to 8 bones, 2 skulls of 3 bones, 2 hands, 0.7 s then 0.3 s between heaves, 500 back on expert Legends). A move every 4.5 to 6.5 s of chase (3 to 4.5 in the second half); rush 5, puke 3, ash 3 by weight; 68% of rushes through you; a spasm every 5 to 8 s, 1.2 to 1.8 s long; it lies down 26 tiles off and stops 12 short; what things hit for (bone 26/44/66, skull 32/56/84, hand 30/52/78, ash 22/40/60, steam 26/46/68). All in `Abomanation.cs`, each with what it does. Confirm in play (PADWorkFlow §16.23).

Keep or cut each.
- [ ] **Amalgaermis: the puke is slower to arrive than it could be** — about 2.5 s from the move beginning to the tell, most of it the body following the head under the ground (so no tail is left hanging in the air). If the fight drags, shorten `MoveGateLeast`/`Most`, not that.
- [ ] **The chopped forest: numbers** — the 5 blocks of a living tree's stump are yours; the rest are mine. All in `ClearedGroundPass.cs`, each with what it does. Run on Far Tract of Potions' terrain outside the game, never seen in a generated world (PADWorkFlow §16.24).
- [ ] **The chopped forest: added without being asked** — the Mile's four signs now stand on `Layout.Ground` (in Far Tract of Potions three of them are on the sky island over the way in); `GraceCleanupPass` no longer empties the inside of a living tree (it was taking the platforms, the doors, the chest and the loom from the room under it). Change the list to taste.

Mine: she comes when a player is within 1350 px of that place (it used to be thirty tiles onto the Cleared Ground, from either end); flat ground keeps within 6 rows, steps 2 at most, has 3 rows of air over it and no water, a living tree is passed over for 40 columns, and the place is kept 114 tiles in from either end; she is put down within 6 tiles of where she was meant to be; the camera settles against a wall, and comes away, over 40 ticks; a player more than 320 px past a wall is let go. Confirm in play (PADWorkFlow §16.25). Since then: in a world made with her ground she is put down at the middle of it, not where the ground is flattest, and flat ground steps over a small hole (§16.28).
- [ ] **'Dame' Quilt's box: added without being asked** — it has no top and no bottom (two walls); it holds any player who is inside it or walks in, not only the one she stopped; nothing inside it can be blown up (`BossArenaTiles`, `BossArenaWalls`: the buff cannot stop that itself); `/dona` says where the flattest place is and `/dona reset` forgets it. Keep or cut each.
- [ ] **'Dame' Quilt is not held by her own box** — nothing in her fight was changed, so a charge still rides on 300 px past the player: with the player's back to a wall she leaves their screen for about two seconds and comes back. The other choice is that she pulls up at the wall, and then a player with their back to one is out of a charge's reach. Decide in play.
- [ ] **Where the box falls** — its middle is halfway between the player and her, as asked, so it is not kept inside the Cleared Ground: in Far Tract of Potions, coming from the jungle, it runs x 9303 to 9748 and 137 of its 445 tiles are before the Cleared Ground's start, because the flat ground is at that end. Keeping it inside would move its middle off that halfway point: say if it should. A world made with her ground does not have this: her place is the middle of the Cleared Ground, and the box lies on her ground (PADWorkFlow §16.28), its ends on the edges of it, which are vanilla's now (§16.29).
- [ ] **Her ground: numbers** — yours: this part flat and undisturbed, with a few holes and none deeper than 5; natural highs and lows, calmer; hills, ponds, cracks or terrain generation at the edges of the patch, the middle flat. Mine: her ground is the middle 560 tiles of the Cleared Ground (her box, 445, and the 56 either way its middle can fall); the flat is the middle 280 of those, half (`Layout.FlatHalf` 140); past either end of the flat the ground eases into vanilla's over 70 tiles (`Layout.FlatEase`), each column put a share of the way to the flat's height that falls smoothly from all to none; the last 70 tiles of each edge are not touched. Of the flat, as before: its line follows the lie of the land (averaged 30 columns either way, twice) and keeps within 6 rows of its level; ground within a row of the line is left as it lies; a hole is 8 wide at the most, there are 4 at the most, 60 apart; no step of more than a row (the Grace plain's rule), and a row's step is poured to a half block; nothing hollow in its first 4 rows; nothing is filled deeper than 120 rows; its top 2 rows are soil and its wall starts at the third; 40 rows over its level and 30 under are claimed in the structure map. All in `ClearedGroundPass.cs` and `Layout`, each with what it does. Run on three worlds' terrain outside the game, never seen in a generated world (PADWorkFlow §16.28, §16.29).
- [ ] **Her ground: added without being asked** — no water is left on the flat: its ponds are filled, its small holes are dry, and water that would run onto it is taken, from as far as 300 tiles past either end (you asked to keep the two ponds in the middle once, before this: say if a small hole should hold water); a pond in the easing keeps its water, as much of its depth as its bed keeps and none higher than it stood; what is hollow under the easing is left hollow, and a cave that a cut goes through into is filled its share of the way like a pit; no living tree roots on the flat, on its easing or within 45 tiles of them (255 either side of the middle), so a giant's stump can stand in the first or last 190 tiles of the Cleared Ground, the outer 25 of her ground at each end among them; the structure map is told of the flat only, so vanilla may dig in her edges; she is met at the middle of her ground and not at the flattest place; in a world made before, flat ground steps over a small hole too; the world remembers how much of her ground is flat (`PADWorld.CalmFlat`), and `/dona` says where her ground and its flat are, or that the world has none. Keep or cut each.
- [ ] **Her box reaches the edges now** — the box is 445 tiles and the flat is 281. With the fight begun at her place, 82 tiles at each end of the box are edge (the easing, and 12 tiles nothing touched); with the box as far off her place as it can fall, 138 at one end. Hills, water and pits deeper than 5 can be inside the box again, at its ends: that is what was asked for, and it is also what the pit by `/dona`'s place was. Triunfo jumps 10, leaps 14 and swims. If the fight should stay on the flat, or the edges should begin nearer, `Layout.FlatHalf` and `Layout.FlatEase` are the two numbers. Decide in play (PADWorkFlow §16.29).
- [ ] **A pond at an edge can lose water** — one whose rim the flat or the easing brings down under it keeps what stands under the rim that is left, and one whose rim was on the flat keeps a row. On 204 stretches of the three worlds water was taken from past the easing in 13, 270 tiles at the most. Look at the ponds beside the flat in a new world and say if that is too much (PADWorkFlow §16.29).
- [ ] **A world made with the build before this one** — it has 560 calm tiles and none of the edges, and `/dona` there says her ground is made flat from end to end. Two faults of that build are in it and are put right for worlds made from now on: where ground was filled in over an old top that vanilla had sloped or poured to half, the shaped block was left under the fill, a small gap inside the ground she rides on; and past either end of her ground a pit with a lip could be left with a pillar of soil one column wide down it. Make a new world to see this build (PADWorkFlow §16.29).
- [ ] **A world already made keeps the ground it has** — her ground is made when a world is. The Run of Bladders keeps its pit (x 2699 to 2715), and `/dona` there says no ground was made for her in that world. It could be put into `/struct update`; that would cut and fill 700 tiles of a world that may have been built on, so it was not. Say if it should be.
- [ ] **Deep cuts and fills under her ground** — with 280 flat tiles they are smaller than they were: the deepest cut and the deepest fill are 26 and 32 rows in The Run of Bladders, 10 and 36 in Far Tract of Potions, 14 and 29 in Life. The fill is all dirt, with no stone in it. Under the ground, not on it. Look once in a new world and say if the fill should have rock in it (PADWorkFlow §16.29).
- [ ] **The Blood Shrine's ground test: numbers** — mine: a tile is part of something floating when 40 open tiles or more in a row begin within 100 tiles under it (`BloodShrinePass.IslandGap`, `IslandThick`), and the pass now says in the log where it built. A world made before keeps its shrine where it is: The Run of Bladders' is on the sky island at x 7973 to 7991, y 200 to 212, 1,680 tiles east of the spawn (PADWorkFlow §16.27).
- [ ] **The graveyard has the ground test the shrine had** — `GraveyardPass.Grounded` is the same 18 solid of 40, and passes a sky island the same way. Not changed: nobody asked, and in The Run of Bladders the graveyard is on the ground. Say if it should have the shrine's test.
- [ ] **The Iron Flow: numbers** — yours: 25 damage; three cuts, down, up, all the way round; carried 1 block, half a block, 1.5 blocks; it hangs pointing down when held; right click parries; the parry's hitbox the arc of your sketch, not drawn; a small window (what it parries, and what that does, is the universal parry's entries below). Elden Ring's: the parry's 6, 8 and 28 ticks (its curved sword). Mine: 24 ticks a cut and 36 for the third; knockback 5; 12 bars; Blue; 20 silver; the arc 48 px from the player's middle and ahead of a line 8 behind it; a flying weapon sent off at 8 px a tick; the angles it hangs at (40 and 60 below level) and every angle and share of every move (`IronFlowMoves`); a cut hits along a line 12 to 66 px from the hand, 38 wide; the cuts follow each other within 40 ticks; raised after the cut up it is held 8 ticks and lowered over 18. Confirm in play (PADWorkFlow §16.26).
- [ ] **The Iron Flow: added without being asked** — what is already inside the arc when it comes up is parried too, and so is what reaches your body from the front in those ticks (the arc alone would let a late press be hit through a spinning blade); a parried NPC cannot touch you for 45 ticks; the screen shakes a little; a cut knocks things away from you rather than the way you face (the circle goes behind you); a parry starts the cuts over; any hostile projectile with vanilla's `arrow` flag and any NPC flown by the flying-weapon AI counts, not only the five named; `Parry.AddProjectile` and `Parry.AddNPC` for more. Keep or cut each.
- [ ] **25 damage from iron** — the answer before was iron tier at about 15; the 25 is yours, and the 12 bars at an anvil stayed. In 1.4.4 vanilla's Iron Broadsword is 12 and its Platinum Broadsword 16. Leave it, or move where it is made.
- [ ] **How big the Iron Flow is in the hand** — the sprite is 64 px, on a player 42 tall, and is drawn at its own size, so hanging down as sketched its hook lies about 30 px below the feet, over the ground. At that size nothing pointing down or level clears the ground: the guard alone reaches 35 px either side of the blade, and the hand is 13 px over the ground. Say if it should hang flatter, or be drawn smaller.
- [ ] **The parry: when to press** — measured outside the game: 6 to 17 ticks before an arrow at 11 px a tick would land (a fifth of a second), 6 to 20 for a flying weapon. To tighten it: fewer active ticks (`ParryTiming.CurvedSword`) or a nearer arc (`ParryPlayer.Reach`).
- [ ] **The universal parry: readings to confirm** — (1) *Giants, "cannot be parried but primary hit still do damage"*: read as a giant's hit is not turned aside and lands on you, it is not pushed or staggered, and the parry's own blow still lands on it, once a parry. (2) *"Like base damage"*: the damage of the weapon that parried as you have it (`Player.GetWeaponDamage`), less the enemy's defence, with no critical hit, none of the game's variation and no knockback of its own. (3) *"Parry wont deal damage anymore"*: a staggered enemy cannot be parried again at all. (4) *"Their AI will be changed to staggered, must remember their normal AI"*: the AI is stopped, not swapped; nothing of the enemy's is changed, so it goes on from where it stopped. (5) *"Quad knock back"*: four times the game's knockback for that hit, so on an enemy the game does not let be knocked back it is four times nothing. (6) *'Dame' Quilt, "all her attacks (joust, thrown lance and horse charge)"*: those three; her swing, which wears the yellow star, is not parried. Parrying the thrown lance lands no blow on her and does not count toward her stagger. (7) Every other boss gets nothing from a parry, not the blow either. Say which of these meant something else (PADWorkFlow §16.30).
- [ ] **The universal parry: numbers** — yours: the blow is the weapon's damage; the riposte is the first hit only, 3 times the damage, 4 times the knockback, no crit, and wakes it; the stagger 2 seconds, on every second parry of a flying enemy and every third of one on the ground; every parry pushes back; solid shots (arrows, javelins, knives, bones), vanilla's bullets, and other bullets at `penetrate` 1; 'Dame' Quilt's three attacks. Mine: a giant is wider than 60 px or taller than 84 (`ParryRules.GiantWidth`, `GiantHeight`); light or heavy is whether the game lets it be knocked back (`knockBackResist` above 0); flying means it has no weight at that parry (`NPC.noGravity`), so a flying weapon is two and whatever walks, hops or is carried is three; the count never runs down between parries and starts over at each stagger (Elden Ring's way); the push is 96 px over 24 ticks, each step nine tenths of the last (`Parry.PushPixels`, `PADStaggerNPC.PushTicks`, `PushDecay`); a staggered thing loses 14% of its speed a tick and a flyer falls; 'Dame' Quilt is out of it for 40 ticks when turned aside and staggered on every third parry (counted as on the ground, though Triunfo carries her: `flies: false`), Triunfo thrown back at 5.9 px a tick with a hop of 3, up on his hind legs for the first 30 ticks of a stagger; her lance knocked away at 5 across and 9.5 up, three turns in 30 ticks; for the one who lands a riposte the camera leans in to 1.05 for 7 ticks, a shake of 6, a flash of 0.06. Confirm in play (PADWorkFlow §16.30, §16.31).
- [ ] **The universal parry: added without being asked** — an enemy has to be attacking to be parried (able to hit you now and coming at you; on your body, moving at all), so one walked into is not; a staggered enemy slumps, and a flyer falls to the ground; a config, the mod's first (`ParryConfig`: five lists); five `Mod.Call` words; `/parry`; a line of counts in the log at load; the camera and the shake for a riposte; Amalgaermis's bones and skulls break on a parry (a skull spills its three bones where it was met); The Hungry is counted a part of the Wall, and the Etherian Goblin Bomber a bomb. Keep or cut each. (The seven lines I wrote for the Iron Flow's tooltip are gone: you rewrote it to "Right click to parry", and it is left as you have it.)
- [ ] **The universal parry: the lists made by hand** — which of vanilla's shots are solid (26 of its enemies' and 8 of the player's thrown things, in `ParryRules.SolidShots`; among them the snowball, the Harpy's feather, the Rock Golem's rock, Plantera's seeds, the Queen Bee's stinger and the two traps' darts), the 25 parts of bosses (`BossLimbs`; the Golem's two fists are on it, but the mod speaks for those itself: `GolemFistParry`), the 8 bombs (`Bombs`). `/parry list` writes what the rules make of every enemy and shot to the log: read it once and say what is in the wrong place.
- [ ] **An enemy whose AI does not take being stopped for 2 seconds** — none is known, and none was looked for one by one: 377 kinds of vanilla's can be staggered. Worms and anything many-parted never are. If one comes back wrong (stuck in a state, teleported, despawned), it goes in `PADStaggerNPC.NeverStagger`, or the config's "never staggered" list without a build.
- [ ] **The riposte throws hard** — four times the knockback is the game's own knockback at four times: a Zombie hit with a knockback of 5 leaves at 9.8 px a tick and 7 up. That is what was asked for; say if it should be capped.
- [ ] **A minion takes the riposte** — the first hit on a staggered enemy is the riposte whoever's it is: a summoner's minions, a sentry or a trap will use it up at their own damage. Say if only the player's own swing or shot should count.
- [ ] **The Golem's fist parried: readings to confirm** — (1) *"golem's fist"*: both fists, and only the punch in flight; at the Golem's side, winding up or going home by itself it cannot be parried. (2) *"the core"*: the Golem's body. It takes its twenty-first even while its head is on, when the game lets nothing else hurt it. (3) The damage is at the landing, not at the parry, and is flat: no armour taken off. (4) *"turn 180 degrees"*: drawn half a turn from how the game points it while it flies home; it goes home the game's own way, at the game's own speed. (5) *"no stagger"*: never, and so never the riposte; not pushed either. (6) The parry's own blow still lands on the fist at the parry. Say which of these meant something else (PADWorkFlow §16.33).
- [ ] **The Golem's fist parried: numbers** — yours: a seventh of the fist's full life to the fist, a twenty-first of the Golem's to the Golem (1428 and 714 in an ordinary world; of what they have in Expert and Master). So seven parries break a fist, and both fists broken that way cost the Golem fourteen twenty-firsts. Mine: the wait before its next punch starts from nothing after a landing; whoever parried it is marked for the Golem's loot; a shake of 7 within 1400 px of the landing (`GolemFistParry`). Confirm in play.
- [ ] **A parried fist and the Golem's pace** — the game makes a Golem below full life faster (fists home at 22 instead of 14, punching at 22 instead of 12, 6 ticks between punches instead of 60, its jump count at 2 a tick instead of 1), and in vanilla that cannot start before the head is off. By your choice, while the head is on the Golem and its fists are shown a Golem at full life by their own AIs (`GolemPace`), so parried fists cost it life without hurrying it. Once the head is off it is as fast as the life it has left makes it, which is faster than vanilla would be at that moment: say if the second half should not be hurried by them either (PADWorkFlow §16.34).

Bosses proper get nothing. Say if these should get nothing too.

- [ ] **The loading screen's pictures are a folder** — `Content/Subworlds/LoadingArts`: every picture in it, whatever it is called, is in the lot, and one is drawn each passage, never the one before (PADWorkFlow §16.37). Seven are in it now: two stand-ins of mine (`LoadingArt_Rainbow`, `LoadingArt_Blank`: PlaceholderTextures.md); `LoadingArt_FlameInFog`, my stand-in with your figure painted over it; two of yours, unfinished (`LoadingArt.png`, `LoadingArt_EpicClash.png`); and the two in `LoadingArts\New folder`, which are my flame as it was (`LoadingArt_EsteemedFlame`) and a second copy of the rainbow, and are in the lot because folders inside the folder count (so the rainbow comes up twice as often). All seven show until one is taken out of the folder. A picture may be any size (1920 by 1080 is what it is mostly seen at), opaque, and is drawn to cover the window. Keep what matters in it left of the middle: the frame goes dark low on the right, where the tip is read, and along the bottom. One is always loaded ahead and held for the next door tried (as a world is entered, and as one is left: §16.40), so a picture put in the folder shows after a build like any other, and none is loaded on the frame a door is tried.

- [ ] **The loading bar goes by the clock** — each of the game's eleven steps has a stretch of the bar as long as the step is expected to take: what it took the last time the same passage was made, this game, and for the first passage each way a reckoning from the two worlds' sizes (`SubworldLoading.Reckon`). The reckoning is your machine's, off client.log (PADWorkFlow §16.39). With tModLoader's backup of the player left out of a passage (below), the player's step is reckoned at a twentieth of a second and tModLoader's backup of the world left (the twentieth is put, not measured: §16.40); were that detour ever not on, at your player's 3.9 s, which is as long as the maps that player has. On another machine the first passage each way will run ahead or hang back, and the next will not. Nothing is kept between one run of the game and the next: say if it should be. Not seen in the game.
- [ ] **tModLoader's backup of the player is left out of a door's passage** — asked for (PADWorkFlow §16.40). Each time a player is saved, tModLoader writes the day's backup zip of the player's file and every map they have over again: about 4 s each way through a door for your player, with nothing on the screen for it. A passage's own save goes without it. The player's file, the game's `.bak` of it, the maps, the world and tModLoader's backup of the *world* are written as ever, and the player's zip is made again by the next autosave or Save and Exit; until then the newest zip of the player in `Players\Backups` is from before the passage. It is a detour on tModLoader's own `BackupIO.RunArchiving`, which is not public and is found by name: if a tModLoader update moves it, client.log has a warning at the mod's load (`Subworlds: tModLoader's backup of a player is not where it was`, or `no detour could be put on`), passages are those 4 s longer again, and nothing else changes. Every passage's line in client.log ends by saying whether the backup was left out. **Offered, not done:** tModLoader's backup of the world left out as well (0.5 to 0.9 s leaving a Grand world). Not seen in the game.
- [ ] **A door's passage is otherwise as long as the game's own Save and Exit and world entry** — with the player's backup left out, reckoned at about 10.5 s out of your Grand world and 3 to 6.5 back (PADWorkFlow §16.39 has every step, with the backup still in). The longest of what is left are the game's writing of the player's map of a great world (4.7 s) and tModLoader's writing of what mods keep in one (3.3 to 3.9 s). Neither is the mod's.

For those 24 ticks the bar is drawn empty and E turns no tip, the world still running underneath. The arrival is as it was: to black over 15 ticks, the world up over 30. Not seen in the game. - [ ] **The Meat Hook: numbers** — yours: a shot fired while the hook holds does double; 5 seconds' wait after a throw that caught nothing and 3 after one that did, from the hook's coming back; 50 tiles; a tile turns it back; it glows red; the harpoon's sound; small is by knockback, hitbox, not a boss and not a part of one; the hook its own projectile, the gun its mother. Mine: 22 damage a pellet and 8 pellets a shell (176; the mod's Onyx Blaster is 144 a blast); 28 ticks a shot, 2 shells, 72 ticks to reload (211 a second to the Onyx Blaster's 247, and about 290 with both shells put into something the hook holds, which is the mod's Tactical Shotgun and a little over); 9 degrees of spread either side with 2 pellets within 2.5, each pellet at 0.82 to 1.12 of the shot's speed; knockback 7; light red, 25 gold; the hook flies out at 24 px a tick and home at 20 rising to 44; its own hit is 1.5 of the gun's damage; it bites 5 ticks before anything moves, holds 48 once what it drags is there and 150 at the most; an enemy is dragged at 20 px a tick to hang 30 px off, the wielder at 16 to stop 56 px short; it gives up after 24 ticks of coming no nearer or with the chain at 1.15 of its reach; a small enemy is no wider than 60 px and no taller than 84 (the parry's giant). All constants in `MeatHook.cs`, `MeatHook_Hook.cs` and `MeatHookRules`, each with what it does. The pellet's 22 is the one number to turn. Confirm in play (PADWorkFlow §16.41).
- [ ] **The Meat Hook: readings to confirm** — (1) *"During hook"*: while the hook is in an enemy, not while it is in the air: a throw that catches nothing doubles nothing. (2) *"2x more damage"*: double, on every pellet of the shell, at whatever it hits and not only at what the hook holds. (3) *"Since the hook returns"*: from the tick the hook ends, which is its coming home, or its going with the gun when the gun is put away. (4) *"Small"*, the knockback part: whether the game lets it be knocked back at all (`knockBackResist` above 0), so a Paladin, a Target Dummy and every NPC of the mod's own drag you to them. Of vanilla's 542 kinds that are not friendly, 378 are dragged in and 164 drag you. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The Meat Hook: added without being asked** — the gun is in the hands whenever it is the item held, and its wielder faces the cursor; it kicks with a shot and is broken open to reload; a shot fired while the hook holds kicks harder, flashes bigger and has a thump under it; the hook does a hit of its own; an enemy on the hook has its AI stopped and touches nobody, and let down is thrown a little way off; the wielder on the chain takes no fall damage from it, has their jumps back, and is taken off a mount and off any grappling hook; a second right click lets go (once it has held 12 ticks), and so does a jump when it is you being dragged; the hook can be thrown while the gun fires, reloads or is empty (it is read off the right button, not through the item's use); frozen, stoned or webbed, the hook is gone and its wait begun; the bar beside the player, "x2" while the hook holds, the note when the wait ends, the hook lying dark on the gun while it waits. Keep or cut each.

- [ ] **Skeletron's hands and the Queen Slime's core parried: readings to confirm** — (1) *"skeleton hands"*: Skeletron's two hands; not Skeletron Prime's four arms. (2) *"1/5 max hand HP"*: a fifth of that hand's own full life, flat, whatever its armour, on top of the parry's blow; taken from the hand, not from his head. (3) *When a hand can be parried* is mine: only while it is swung at you (vanilla's swing down and its sweep, and for as long after as the hand is still flying; in Legends the clap as the hands shut and the smash as they come down). A hand hanging by him hurts by touch and cannot be parried: five parries of one that only hangs there would break it in the time of one swing. Say if any touch of a hand should be parried. (4) *"crystal core boomerang"*: the core she throws in Legends, anywhere on its way out or back; *"returning it to her"*: straight back from where it was parried, hurting nobody on the way. (5) *"shredding her by 1/10 hp"*: a tenth of her full life, not of what she has left; flat, whatever her armour; once, as it gets into her (PADWorkFlow §16.42).
- [ ] **Skeletron's hands and the Queen Slime's core parried: numbers** — yours: a fifth of the hand's full life; a tenth of hers. In your Expert world, alone: 312 off a hand of 1560 (468 of 2340 in Legends), and 3456 off her 34560 in Legends. Each a constant in `SkeletronHandParry.cs` or `QueenSlimeCore.cs`.
- [ ] **Skeletron's hands and the Queen Slime's core parried: added without being asked** — in the clap and the smash the other hand is thrown back with the one parried, loses nothing, and cannot touch you; the bang, the dust and the shake of a clap (and a smash's landing) are left out of a blow a parry has broken; a hand is never staggered or pushed, it is thrown back; the core on its way back hurts nobody, the town's people included; whoever parried is marked as having fought him or her; the tenth can bring her wings or begin her death; `/parry` on a hand says what a parry does to it, or that it is not in a swing. From there the sword is its sharp side (a stand-in picture: PlaceholderTextures.md) and does the later phases' damage (150 / 200 / 230), which it already did from 66 down. Since §16.46 he fights differently in it, by your list: whatever is sent at him he answers two times in three; from far off he mostly walks, leaps onto you more often and from further, and seldom throws a knife; up close he waits a second or two between attacks, the sword's four moves are a quarter faster and carry him a third further, and he has the move with the hand (the sword up, a run at you, the blow down and the cut up, down on a knee, a leap ten tiles forward and five up in half a second with the hand out; with you by the throat the camera comes in, you are pulled against him, the sword goes slowly through you and is raised with you on it, and you are flung off). Since §16.47 it has four more moves of its own, asked for so that it is less his first phase over again: the late cut (sunk low, a pale line from him through you; he is gone along it and standing past you with his back turned; a moment later the whole line opens), the mill (the sword levelled at you, three turns coming on with it out, and a blow down out of the last), the cut up out of the ground (a run at you with the sword dragged in sparks, the blade torn forward through the ground and up, and whoever it lands on thrown into the air, where he is before they are, to strike them down) and in the air (a flash step to beside anyone off the ground or over his head, a moment hanging there with no weight, and a blow that sends them down); and what he picks up close changed so that his first phase's four moves are under a third of it, where they were two thirds. What of that is yours and what is mine is in the entries on readings, numbers and what was added, below. Since §16.48, at 30 in a hundred, by your list: the picture fades and comes back on him bent over his sword, shaking; he coughs blood, says a line (it waits for your answer), raises the sword, takes it by the blade and puts it through himself, tears it out with the blood leaving along its swing, says your line over the bloodied sword and one more, steps behind you, pulls on your wings and cuts them off (nobody flies from then until he is gone), says your other line, and the picture fades back on a sword that burns black and red. In his third phase every blow of the sword leaves a wound (Ultra Bleeding: three at the most, 12 life a second each, no life coming back, five seconds from the last blow). Since §16.49 it has the one move of its own that was asked for ("he can also throw a flying slash projectile too for one of the moves but make it a move set tho"; "remember the slash is multi directional so if ur on the ceiling it also hits you"): three cuts where he stands, each let go of as a crescent of the black and red that goes on through the air at you wherever you are, the ceiling with it; half the sword's blow and its wound each; jumped, or turned aside by a parry. Everything else he fought with there was his second phase's, until §16.52 (below). Since §16.50, the first of three deliveries of what was asked on the 7th ("he can do something so shit dont just shred him ... zenith and last prism lk shredding him tf out"): in every phase no more than 8 thousandths of his life can be taken from him in a second, and at that he turns everything aside; he has a posture that what he turns aside wears at and that breaks him for three seconds (no bar: "keep posture ui hidden"); from his second phase what keeps coming at him is answered whole, a second at a time, two times in three, in whatever move he is in, and held at that for two and a half seconds he runs at whoever it is behind his sword, and losing life near as fast as he can he flash-steps behind them and cuts; in his third phase his defence is doubled and he answers four times in five. Since §16.51, the second: from his second phase he has a hook on a chain in his bottom hand (taken out in the passage with nothing said of it; thrown, it pulls him to one it bites on the ground and pulls one who is off the ground in to him, and the sword comes down as they meet); his leap goes as high as whoever it is for, up to forty-six tiles; his second phase has two cuts of pale wind, let go at them wherever they are; his knives are thrown oftener, and five at once in a fan; any hit of his lets go a grapple (none is thrown for a second) and, off the ground, shuts wings and rocket boots for two seconds; and he reads four things a player does (a swing that missed, something held on him, a reload, a grapple) and answers them. Since §16.52, the third, and with it everything asked on the 7th: in his third phase the sword's moves on his feet are half as fast again, and he waits half a second to a second between moves; a cut of his that ends with you out of its reach is let go at you through the air; he has the wheel (down, then up off the ground about eleven tiles, turning, and three of the great cuts let go at you wherever you are); the leap that comes down on nobody drives the sword deeper, and eleven cuts go out of the ground as far as ten tiles, level both ways and round over the top; now and then the sword stays up longer than it should, or does not come down at all and he is behind you (the blade flares first, either way); and what he has counted of you since his second phase (how much you parry, how much you are off the ground, how much you keep out of his reach) weighs what he picks. Since §16.53, as asked on the 8th: the cuts out of the ground are straight cuts (fourteen across the ten tiles about the blade, each a blow), and in all three phases what fights for you is answered: when it has taken too much from him in three seconds he curls up in the air, nothing landing on him, and bursts into sixteen straight cuts across the twenty tiles about him. Since §16.54, as asked on the 8th: what fights for you is turned aside two times in three (four in five in his third phase) and takes no more than half of what the cap allows in a second, and the ball's straight cuts cut it down four times in nine, the Stardust Dragon whole. Since §16.55, as asked on the 8th: the ball is white in his first two phases and red and black in his third, its cuts reach 18, 23 and 27 tiles and cut what fights for you down four, six and nine times in nine, and a roar is drawn in to him, red and black, before it; and his end is built as your script has it, Finish or Spare (the entry on it, next). **Not built**: nothing is dropped but the sword his end gives, nothing is said in the chat, no flag is kept that he was beaten, and he is not in Boss Checklist or the bestiary (PADWorkFlow §16.43 to §16.55).

- [ ] **Foigh's Rust Side Sword: readings to confirm** — (1) *"Cooldown for 2 mins"*: counted from the blood's end (Dried Blood), so two minutes with it and two without, not two from its start. (2) *"clear All debuffs"*: every one the Nurse would take, so Potion Sickness, hunger, a campfire's, a heart lantern's, honey's and the rest of the eighteen the game keeps from her stay. (3) *"Holding Enter"*: standing on the ground, not on a mount or a hook, nothing under way; while the sword is in hand and could be drenched, Enter is the sword's and does not open the chat (drenched, waiting, or with anything else in hand, it is the chat's). (4) *"every speed is 1.25x"*: using any item (weapons, tools, blocks), running and the jump; not flight, mounts, swimming or falling. (5) *"life max hp is 3/5"*: of all of it, what is worn included. (6) *"all slashes"*: the three in the air and the drive's straight cuts; the chain's cuts are the blade, and hit each enemy once a cut. One blow in it, as the sword goes through, at the sword's damage. (85) "Learns you": three things counted from his second phase and used in his third: parried, off the ground, out of reach. Since §16.53: (86) *"cannot parry wind or blood slashes"*: not broken but unanswerable by eye (a parry had to be pressed while a slash was 4 to 13 tiles off); now a parry already begun when one of his flying cuts reaches you turns it aside, its wind-up and all. His sword in his hand, his knives and his hook keep the old rule. (87) *"the straight slashes stuff ... like this plunge"*: straight cuts across the place he stands in, at every slant, one after another, each shown a moment before it opens. (88) *"try to make it again but do damage"*: the plunge's ring of §16.52 made again as those, each a blow; the ring is gone. (89) *"he curl up into a ball and explode into 20 tile radius slashes aoe"*: in all three phases; "too much" and "a time frame" are mine: half of what the cap lets him lose, in three seconds. (90) *"slashes aoe must always be straight cuts"*: every slash over a place: the plunge's and the ball's. His flying slashes (the three cuts', the wheel's, a cut let go, the wind) go at you through the air and are as they were. (91) *"they dont start and end at the same time, they have variations ranges and different duration, start and ends"*: each cut its own length, its own place along its line, its own ticks shown and hanging, let out at uneven ticks, no two opening on one tick. **The other reading, not built**: that each grows along its length from one end to the other. (92) *"should not go thru a same point"*: the ball's cuts each through a point of its own about him, and those at you through a point of your body of their own. (93) *"it tips dont load right away when i pressed E"*: the next tip up at once, and faint words drawn faint, not dark. Since §16.54: (94) *"minion: 4/9 chance for 3"*: each of the ball's straight cuts cuts down what it lies through of what fights for you, four times in nine: minions and sentries, not what they shoot; each tried once by each cut; the cuts out of the ground cut none. (95) *"the other applies"*: the other three answers of §16.53, all of them: what fights for you turned aside at his usual rate, in all three phases and in every move but when he answers nothing, wearing nothing off his posture; a cap of its own, half of his, in any sixty ticks; and one cooldown for the whole Stardust Dragon, which the game already had (§16.53 was wrong to say each segment hits on its own): what was mended is his turning a segment aside, after which the whole dragon now waits as when it hits. (96) *"killing 1 stardust dragon means killing the whole dragon"*: any part of it cut down by the ball's cuts, all of it, head to tail. (97) *"ultrableeding have blood particle spewing down"*: each wound a spew of its own out of you, spurting with the heart and running between, down to the ground and no further. Since §16.55: (98) *"radius"*: how far from him the ball's cuts reach (§16.53's twenty tiles), the points they go through kept within three fifths of it; of your two sets of numbers the second (18, 23, 27) was built. (99) *"its white, slashes not red black blood"*: the ball white all through in his first two phases: the air drawn in, a gleam on the blade, a white flash and a ring of steel, white cuts with sparks of steel; "3x phase 1" is every time. (100) *"a reverse roar effect in red and black"*: the mod's roar drawn in to him, rimmed red, in all three phases, silent, from his leap to his burst. (101) *"his roar red and black too"*: his roar at his end. (102) *"the 4th columm at the end of the hall"*: the fourth pilaster from the end wall away from the door, the half one against the wall the first (the one in your picture); he is gone there from where he was struck down, a ghost left behind; anywhere but the hall he roars where he is. (103) *"except the ones in a streak above him"*: the five torches up the pilaster he stands under. (104) *"in front of him"*: the way he faces, down the hall. (105) *"cough blood range 2-4 times"*: what he does, not words: he coughs blood two to four times, and "I knew it" waits through them. (106) *"(...)"*: an answer, "...", to go on with. (107) *"look up"*: at the sky. (108) *"bottom hand"*: your back arm, on his shoulder; the other holds the sword by its blade, which is longer than you are tall. (109) *"cast the body"*: shoved over onto his back. (110) *"check guide fade"*: smoke and then ash, as the Guide's sacrifice in Legends, lying in a heap before a wind takes it. (111) *"walks up"*, in Spare: the walk up already made. (112) *"throw it behind him"*: behind you. (113) *"confused"*: a look after you, his head shaken, a look at his hands. (114) *"Player exit subworld"*: out by the hall's passage, its loading screen and all, to the world and the door you came in by. Since §16.56: (115) *"the thin transparent hollow knight roar that every other boss have, instead of just black its black and red"*: the roar every boss has, thin and seen through, each ring's fainter band red and its dark band black, for both of his roars; §16.55 drew the red over the whole square of each ring, and a red light along the dark band: both gone. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **Foigh: numbers** — yours: his life (150,000 / 240,000 / 360,000, half as much again in Legends); the sword (100 / 130 / 150, then 150 / 200 / 230, half as much again in Legends); the knife's 100; all of it through armour; the stages at 66 and 30; the walk up to 20 tiles; the four lines; 2 in 5 hard shots struck and half of those sent back; 3 in 5 of anything else stepped out of the way of; 3 in 5 swings met; what is in each of the four sword moves; two seconds with the sword in the ground; twice the damage for the blow down; a second knocked out by the thrown sword; three knives 20 ticks apart; half a second behind them before the cut up. Mine: defence 40; nothing by touch; his walk (1.15 px a tick, to rest about 54 px off them) and the 26 to 70 ticks of it between moves (18 to 34 after a parry); the sword's reach for choosing (150 px, and no more than 84 px over him or 70 under); what he picks and how often (in reach the first move 3, the third 3, the leap 2, the fourth 2; over his head knives 4, the fourth move 2.5, the leap 2; elsewhere knives 5.5, the step behind 2.5, the leap 2; the move he did last a quarter as likely); every tick of every move (the first 150, the third 138, the fourth about 160, a knife 30, three 70, behind 52 and two steps of 7); the leap (chosen up to 30 tiles off, aimed a third of the way ahead of them, its feet 40 px short of them; how high is yours since §16.44, from the height that was to three times it by distance, and mine in what those come to: 75 px beside him to 225 px at 30 tiles, 33 to 57 ticks in the air, and at least 24 px over the feet of a player who is over him); the jump of ten tiles and the sword thrown at 24 px a tick; his run after it (8.6 px a tick); a knife's 17.5 px a tick; 22 throws in 100 are three, and after three he leaps 40 times in 100; how far ahead a shot is rolled for (5 ticks for one he strikes, 14 for one he steps out of the way of); in front of them three times in four when there is room; a shot sent back at 9 to 16 px a tick; a potion seen within 100 tiles; the hand's reach (26 px); the ticks of the grab (10, 40, 16, 30, 40) and the fling of about 28 tiles; how far each blow throws (a cut about 7 tiles, the thrust 13, the blow down 12, a knife 4, the thrown sword 13); parried, a reel of 44 ticks and the knee on every third (the universal parry's two seconds and its riposte); the road's fades, the walk's pace, the 54 ticks of the camera's way over, the turn's 112, the sword's 108, the 3.5 seconds from Ready to his first step, how fast he talks; the server's waits for a player who never answers (25 s, 90 s, 90 s); his end (2.5 s). The passage into his second phase (§16.44), yours: at 66 in a hundred; 70 tiles; four spits a second, 0.4 s and 0.3 s apart; the three lines and which of them are answered. Mine: every tick of it (26 to stop and go black; black until tick 16 of the knee and the picture back by 46; the camera off to him at 58 and on him at 154; blood at 180, 240, 264 and 282; his head up from 328 to 354; the first line at 364; 160 ticks from his hands tightening on the sword to the stance he fights from (150 before §16.45): 10 to set himself, 16 heaving, the sword coming 9 px up its own line, 8 tearing it out through the ground, its point out 98 px ahead of him, the sword at the sky from 46 to 64 and the stone on the blade from 88 to 100; on his knee the middle of the sword's handle 36 px over the ground and 19 px ahead of him, 52 of the blade's 72 px under it; the third line 12 ticks in and down 80 after it has all arrived; 60 back to the player); the lean on him (1.3, then 1.36); eight drops a spit and thirteen the last; no nearer than 30 tiles when there is no room for 70; the server's waits (90 s for the two answers, 15 s for the third line). His second phase (§16.46), yours: two times in three for each of the three answers; mostly walking from far off, the leap more often, the knife more rarely; a second to two between attacks up close; what is in the hand's move; ten tiles forward, five up, half a second. Mine: what he picks (in reach, which is 200 px now: the hand 3.5, the first move 2, the third 2, the leap 1.5, the fourth 1.5; over his head knives 1.5, the fourth 2.5, the leap 3; elsewhere walking on 6, the leap 3 within 40 tiles, the step behind 1.2, a knife 0.8) and 40 to 70 ticks of walking on; a quarter faster and a third further; the leap back at 7.6 px a tick and 5.7 up; the run after the sword at 10.6; the run with the sword up (9.5 px a tick, the blow begun within 84 px, a second at the longest); the ticks of the hand's move (16, the run, 42, 18, up to 30 in the air, 78, 46, 40; a miss 40); the leap's aim (seven tenths of where you are going, his feet 20 px short, at least 40 px long and 30 high, 20 px over you if you are over him) and the hand's reach (26 px); pulled in over 5 ticks; the camera at 1.4; the sword pointing 23 degrees up as it goes through you and 50 raised, you a fifth of the way along it; one blow in it at the sword's damage. Four more moves (§16.47), yours: that there are three and one in the air; that he steps up to you, hangs with no weight and strikes you down; that they are his second phase's; that it should be less of his first. Mine, every one: the late cut (26 ticks sunk low, 7 gone, the line opening 20 ticks after he has stopped, 66 in all past you; 96 px past you and 720 px at the furthest; the line 24 px longer than the way he went, 24 px over the ground and 18 px thick; none with under 48 px of ground ahead); the mill (22 ticks levelling, three turns of 16, 7.2 px a tick, begun within 380 px, six cuts and a blow at the sword's damage each, the last blow 50 ticks, off the ground at 5.2 up and 3.2 on); the cut up out of the ground (a run at 9 px a tick for 10 to 60 ticks, stopped 40 px short, the cut begun within 72 px and 46 ticks long, or 20 if it lands; thrown up at 12.5 and 1.5; yours to be struck for 90 ticks at the most); in the air (off the ground at 64 px; within 720 px along and up; 46 px to your side and 6 px under, or level, 10 px over or 16 px under where there is no room for that; 14 ticks hanging, 6 after his own cut; the blow 17; sent down at 15 and 2 and down 50 ticks; 76 px of air or it is the mill's last blow in its place; landing 24); that the cut up and the blow after it are two blows at the sword's damage each (300 / 400 / 460 by the world for the two); and what he picks (in reach the hand 3, the mill 3, the cut up 2, the late cut 1.5, in the air 3 if you are off the ground, the first move and the third 1.2 each, the leap and the fourth 0.8 each; over his head in the air 4.5, the leap 2.5, the fourth 2, a knife 1; elsewhere walking on 9.5, the leap 4.5, the late cut 1.5 within 39 tiles, the mill 1.2 within 380 px, in the air 2.5 if you are off the ground, the step behind 0.8, a knife 0.7). His third phase (§16.48), yours: the beats of the passage and their order; your two lines; Ultra Bleeding on the sword; the cut and flight gone from it; black and red; and by your answers a wound a blow, three at the most, 12 life a second each, five seconds from the last blow, no life coming back, flight gone for everything that flies until he is gone, and the four lines' order. Mine, every one: three tenths of his life; the player stood 16 tiles off (never under 8); 26 ticks to black, the picture back by tick 46; coughs at ticks 76 and 104; his first line at 156; the sword out of the ground at 30, at the sky from 46 to 72, by the blade at 92, through him at 111 (162 ticks in all, not a word); out again by 17 and flicked down by 31; your line at 82, staying 70 ticks, mine no sooner than 298, staying 60; 42 px behind you, the hand on your wings at tick 10, a pull of 4 px and 11 degrees, the cut at 40, 24 px away after it; "Futile devices" at 66, 45 ticks, and the fade; 60 ticks back. About 21 seconds as played. The server waits 90 seconds for your answer, 15 and 10 for the lines that go by themselves. The lean of the picture: 1.3, 1.42 with the sword in him, 1.5 behind you. That the wound is five seconds in every difficulty, and that it is three buffs with I, II and III in their names. His third phase's move (§16.49), yours: that he throws a flying slash in one of his moves; that the move is a set; that a slash goes any way and finds one on the ceiling. Mine, every one: three cuts, and which (down, up, down in both hands); 160 ticks; let go of at 30, 75 and 122; he looks for you at 10, 44 and 89; steps of 20, 14 and 22 px; 40 to 62 ticks after it; how often (1.5 beside the 13.5 of his other moves up close; 3.5 from further off, within 800 px, which is about one go in six along the ground and one in four at one over his head); 68 px from tip to tip, 12 px a tick, 80 ticks (59 tiles); the third 88, 9 and 100 (56 tiles); hurting from its tick 2 to its tick 73 (93); **half the sword's blow** (64 to 86 in Classic, about 100 in Expert, 115 in Master, half as much again in Legends) **and a wound**; a throw of 6.3 tiles; that it is parried and jumped, and that a parry of it does nothing to him; that half of where you are going is allowed for; 135 degrees round from the way he faces and no further. What keeps him standing (§16.50), yours: 8 thousandths of his life a second; five seconds' worth kept and spent while he is broken; about three seconds broken; two in three in his second phase and four in five in his third; two and a half seconds; a fifth of a second; the star on the break-out; his third phase's defence doubled; the cap in all three phases and the rest from the second; no bar. Mine, every one of the rest: two seconds' worth at once; shut at nothing until half a second's worth can be taken; a burn out of the same; posture 100, 9 a second from shots, 4 for a shot struck by itself, 6 for a swing met, 25 for his sword parried, a second and a half and then 12 a second back, a second after a break with nothing wearing at it; three hits no more than 15 ticks apart as a stream; one roll a second; one shot sent back in a second held; the server told every 8 ticks; half a second's gap ending the count toward the run; the run at 10.6 px a tick, cutting from 92 px, three seconds at the most, and what it ends in; the break-out at 5 thousandths in a second and no oftener than every six seconds. What reaches them (§16.51), yours: the hook from his second phase, taken out in the passage and not spoken of; both ways for it, by where they are; pale wind cuts in his second phase; knife volleys; a grapple let go by a hit and none thrown for a second; wings and rocket boots shut for two seconds by a hit in the air; that he reads and punishes. Mine, every one of the rest: the hook's 22 px a tick and 36 ticks of chain, thrown from 140 to 732 px, its blow of 50 that leaves no grace, 15 px a tick along the chain either way, the blow that ends it his sword's whole and open to a parry; forty-six tiles for the leap; the wind's 14 px a tick for 66 ticks, half the sword's blow, 45 ticks between its two; 38 fans in a hundred throws, five knives twelve degrees apart, over one who stands; that any hit counts, and that the Soaring Insignia, a flying carpet and a mount that flies are shut with wings and boots; a swing read from 240 px, a thing held for three quarters of a second from 200 px or more (and read again for as long as it is held), one thing told in two and a half seconds; and how often each is picked. His third phase, wilder (§16.52), yours: half as fast again; half a second to a second between moves; a move where he leaps up, turns and throws big slashes; the sword plunged deeper and slashes out to ten tiles all round; delays and feints; that he learns the player. Mine, every one of the rest: which moves are quicker; that no wait is longer than a second and the short ones stay short; walking on at 5 (it is 9.5 in his second phase) for 20 to 36 ticks; a cut let go from 150 px, ahead of him, no oftener than every 30 ticks; the wheel's 17 ticks down, 179 px up, a turn every 16 ticks, three great cuts, 130 px of room over his head, 800 px, and its 1.2 up close, 2.5 from further off and 3.5 at one over his head; the sword 44 ticks in the ground (it was 120), driven in at tick 24, eleven cuts of 48 px at 12 px a tick that hurt for 13 ticks; a linger 15 times in a hundred for 20 to 34 ticks, a feint 8 in a hundred, the blade flaring as he begins either and again 20 ticks before a lingering sword comes down; and the three things he learns, each thing seen counting for 15 parts in a hundred, coming at the most to two and a half times (what goes up after one in the air), twice (what closes with one who keeps off, what no parry turns), and 60 lingers and 35 feints in a hundred. Since §16.53, yours: that his slashes can be parried; straight slashes for the plunge, doing damage; the curl and the burst on too much minion damage in a time frame, and twenty tiles for it; every slash over a place straight cuts, each with its own times, length and place, none through one point. Mine, every one of the rest: that a parry's wind-up counts against his flying cuts and straight cuts and nothing else of his; a straight cut shown 12 to 22 ticks, open 3, hanging 6 to 28, 14 px thick, from 45 in a hundred of its line across its circle to all of it, a tile and a half of it kept either side of the point it goes through, half his sword's blow, the wound in his third phase only; the plunge's fourteen within 40 ticks, through points within seven of the ten tiles, every third through you, the sword torn out five ticks after the last has cut (92 ticks at the latest; it was 44); the ball after half of the cap's three seconds (1,800 of 150,000 in a Classic world), no oftener than every ten seconds, a machine's word no oftener than every second; 8 ticks down, 16 up at up to 9 px a tick, the burst at 52, the fall from 58; its sixteen cuts let out from 22 ticks before the burst, through points within twelve of the twenty tiles, every fourth through you. Since §16.54, yours: four in nine; two in three and four in five; half of the cap; the dragon whole; blood spewing down. Mine: the turning aside in all three phases and in every move; no posture worn by it, nor at the cap; its cap over any sixty ticks, counted for each player (600 in a Classic world, 960 Expert, 1,440 Master); the ball at nine tenths of that over three seconds (1,620; it was 1,800 of anything that fights for you); sentries cut down as minions are; the spew's places on you, its beat (36 ticks), spurts (5 ticks of two drops), runs (one tick in three), a third of a beat between wounds, and stains (a spurt's drop one time in three, 150 to 230 ticks). Since §16.55, yours: white, white, red and black; 18, 23 and 27 tiles; four, six and nine in nine; the roar drawn in, and his own, red and black; the fourth pilaster; twenty tiles and five; two to four coughs; thirty degrees; ten seconds. Mine: the ball's points within three fifths of its reach (173, 221, 259 px); the roar drawn in over its 44 ticks, at a boss's strength, silent; his roar's breath 22, voice 150 and 36 after, at -0.45 and one and a half times a boss's; the dark along the hall at 40 px a tick; the fade's 24, 16 and 30, the camera's 96 at 1.3; the fall's ticks (knees 46, over 20, hands 18); the sword lying 38 and 82 px ahead of his feet; a walk of 1.6 px a tick; a cough of 18 ticks every 30; ninety seconds before a line goes on by itself; the thrust's, the cast's (432 in all) and the spare's ticks; the throw (6.2 px a tick back, 5.2 up); fifteen seconds before he goes on without the walk up; the sword's numbers.
