- [ ] **Foigh: added without being asked** — his cuts can be parried (not the thrown sword, not the hand), and a knife and a shot sent back are broken by a parry; the leap back at the end of the first move cuts; before the thrust he turns to face them again; the leap is also used from the middle distance, after three knives, and from over them out of a step up; what a minion or a sentry does lands on him always; a second shot while his sword is still up is struck with the same guard; the hand can be got out of the way of, by a run in good boots or a jump (walking away is not enough), and he is 40 ticks getting over a hand that closed on nothing; a player held is kept out of walls; the pale ones of himself he leaves along a step; his name and face on the dialogue from his first line; if the player he waits for dies or leaves on the road he is gone, and in the fight with nobody left alive he stands 2.5 seconds and goes; `Dialogue.Aside`. Since §16.44: the two stand-in pictures (his sword's sharp side and the stone: the passage asks for both and neither had one); `/foigh second`; the server's waits in the passage, so a player who never answers does not stop the fight for good; a player who becomes his target after the picture is back is not moved; a player put down by a cut is stood up if they had been thrown (`RagdollPlayer.StandNow`: the road's walk up as well). Since §16.45: how the upright sword comes out (the heave, then torn out forward through the ground, the passage ten ticks longer for it); the floor's tiles heaved up one after another along its way, with sparks, dust and two shakes; the screen shaken for the player who watches by the camera (the passage's one shake had never reached them: 70 tiles is further than the fight's shake goes). Since §16.46: flung off the sword once it has been raised with you on it; the blow down and the cut up of the hand's move can be parried and the hand cannot; he does not turn once he is down on the knee; a hand that closes on nothing costs him 40 ticks; in his second phase a step's distance past the fastest he goes is carried into the ticks after (without it the thrust went no further than before); a swing of yours that he meets does not cut the second or two short (in his first phase, and in any other wait, it still brings his next move on within 26 ticks); a step out of the way of a spell and into the sword counts as a move from up close; in reach at 200 px and the leap tried from 40 tiles; the step behind kept among what he does from far off; a line for a death on the slow sword. Since §16.47 (the choice was given, so this is only what goes past three moves and one in the air): the late cut and the mill can be parried, and the cut up out of the ground and the blow in the air cannot (they wear the star: PAD_NPC_Plan §2.7; the cut up in the hand's move is another, and still can be); the late cut can be parried whichever way you face; the blow after the cut up lands whatever grace the cut left you; too near the ground for the blow in the air it is the mill's last blow, and so one who only jumped is met where they come down; he goes up to one stood on a ledge or a platform over his head as well; a hook in something or a rope is taken to hold you where you are, and with no room beside you it is a knife; one who falls slowly, or whose weight is turned over, is worked out as the game works them out, and beside one whose head is against a ceiling he comes in lower; at least 10 ticks of the run before the cut up, and he stops short of you; he does not turn in the mill, nor once the late cut's step is taken; the late cut throws you the way he went; his first phase's four made rarer up close, and from far off walking on 9.5, the leap 4.5, the step behind 0.8 and a knife 0.7, so that the shares of §16.46 hold with two more things to pick from; two lines for a death (by the late cut, and sent down); the names of the eleven moves, which are nowhere in the game. Since §16.48: that **everybody** loses flight while he is there with wings cut, not only the one he cut (one flag, as the mod's own lock is); that cut wings do not glide or quicken the air and take the booster's rockets and jump with them; that one with no wings on has the cut made all the same; that knives leave no wound; two coughs of blood; a light up the blade at the sky; his hands bleeding on it; the sword left standing behind you; seven cuts hanging in the air as the picture comes back; `/foigh third`; and his sum of where a player in the air will be made the game's own for everything a player flies or falls by. Since §16.49: that what he lets go of can be parried (it is gone, and he does not reel) and can be jumped; that it is half the sword's blow and leaves a wound; that the blade itself cuts one who stands near enough in each of the three, and can be parried there; that the blade flares before the first; that he looks for you again before each cut and comes round; that one behind him and not over him is not cut at; that half of where you are going is allowed for; that it goes through platforms, is gone against the first solid thing, and goes when he does; and its line for one it kills ("... was cut by a sword that was nowhere near."). Since §16.50: that what burns or bleeds him comes out of the same cap; that at the cap he stays shut until half a second's worth can be taken; that the first blow on him broken is a riposte, and that he is thrown back a step as he breaks; that one shot of a stream held off goes back at whoever sent it each second; that nothing of theirs lands while he runs at them; that he goes up after one who hangs over his head, from the run and from the break-out; that he begins his next move out of his guard when a stream keeps him in it; and that what lands on him while he is down does not count toward the break-out. Since §16.51: that any hit of his lets go a grapple and shuts wings, not the sword's alone; that the Soaring Insignia, a flying carpet, a mount that flies and gliding are shut with wings and boots, and that what wings and boots had left is given back after; that the hook's bite leaves no grace against the blow it brings; that he is beside them in the air if they jump while he comes along his chain; that he does not break out with his hook out; that the fan goes over one who stands; that a reload and a grapple are read, beside the swing and the thing held; that a parry is not read as a swing; that the up-close wait follows his hook; and that his knives are picked oftener in his third phase as in his second. Since §16.52: that a cut of his sword is let go at one out of its reach; that he walks on less in his third phase and thinks again sooner; that no wait there is longer than a second; the blade flaring before a sword that lingers or a feint, and his eye; that he looks for you again when the sword has stayed up; that the hand's blow lingers too; that the wheel needs room over his head, and that its cuts go straight down at one under it; that with a wall where the blade went in the cuts go out from over his feet; that the more you parry the more he picks what no parry turns; and that the more you keep off the less he walks. Since §16.53: that his flying cuts and straight cuts take a parry begun before they reach you, and his sword, knives and hook do not; that the straight cuts open on ticks none of the others does; that the ones at you each go through a point of your body of its own; that the sword stays in the ground until five ticks after the last has cut; that nothing lands on him while he is curled up; that he finishes the move he is in first, faces whoever it is for, and looks for them again just before he bursts; that one owed as a passage begins is forgotten; the red drawn in to him, the shaking, the flash, the ring of red out to twenty tiles and the ground shaking as he bursts; and that the ball's cuts wound only in his third phase. Since §16.54: that what fights for you is turned aside in his first phase too, and in every move of his; that it wears nothing off his posture and does not set him running at you, at his rate or at the cap (at the cap it wore his posture before); that its cap is counted for each player apart; that sentries are cut down by the ball's cuts as minions are; that one turned aside is seen and heard turned (sparks, steel); that the dragon turned aside waits as when it hits; that the ball now comes at nine tenths of the minions' own cap; that one cut down shows the cut across it; that the blood comes down to the ground under you and lies there a while. Since §16.55: the white's look (the air drawn in, the gleam, the flash and the ring of steel, the sparks); the roar drawn in in all three phases, and silent; the step to the pilaster, a ghost left where he stood; his roar's black smoke, and a wisp off each flame put out; the flames lit again under the last black, when he is gone, and on every load; the camera's lean; his sway before he falls; the sword lying between you, its grip under your hand; your walk up being walked for you; the blood from his mouth; his laugh; the sword carried on your shoulder and held by its blade; the shove; the heap of ash, and a wind of its own; the throw's flight; the look of the ten seconds; the passage's own loading screen; a line never answered going on by itself after a minute and a half, the question then "No you weren't" and the choice Spare; and with nobody to see it, down on his face and gone. Keep or cut each.
- [ ] **Foigh's music** — none on the road; in the fight the game's other soundtrack's second boss theme (`MusicID.OtherworldlyBoss2`: `FightMusic` in `Foigh.Fight.cs`, one line). Picked by what it is, not by ear. Say what it should be.
- [ ] **The Empty Hall is an arena now: readings and numbers** — yours: a wall of Gray Brick 5 tiles thick at both edges; a ceiling 36 thick; gray stone wall behind; no platform; torches and Tiki Torches; well lit; flat; and since §16.44, 14 wall segments long and 2 high, the door's height counted in, with the ground, the walls and the ceiling as thick as they were. Mine: "5 thick" and "36 thick" are of what can be seen (the brick runs on past every edge, so a wide screen sees no end to it); a "segment" is one cell of the masonry, twelve columns by twenty-four rows, which makes the hall 171 tiles long and 48 high in a place 264 by 207 (it was 307 by 161 in one 400 by 320); "gray stone wall" is Stone Wall, laid as masonry so it is not one flat sheet (a dado of Gray Brick three rows high, a rail of Stone Slab, pilasters of Stone Slab three wide every twelve columns, the first and the last against the end walls, a frieze, one course of Stone Slab half way up, and a fifth of the Stone patched with brick); the lights on a lattice (a torch on every pilaster every twelve rows from three rows over a head, a column of them up the middle of every bay six rows out of step, one more of each at the head of the wall, and a Tiki Torch on the floor in the middle of every bay, the two end ones too since §16.44): 131 torches and 14 Tiki Torches, nowhere further than six tiles from a flame by the game's count of light but in the one column against each end wall (seven), and nowhere on the floor further than four. Its size is five numbers at the head of `EmptyHall.cs` (`Bays`, `Panels`, and the three thicknesses); two panels and nine bays are the least, because a place has to be 200 tiles each way (`SubworldKeeper.Least`: under that a Teleportation Potion drunk there makes the game throw). The layout number is 5, so a world's next passage makes the hall afresh. Drawn outside the game with the game's own pictures and looked at; this size not seen in play (PADWorkFlow §16.43, §16.44).
- [ ] **Foigh's higher leap is easier to walk out from under** — asked for: the leap from the height that was to three times it, by distance (§16.44). His weight is one number, so a higher leap is a longer one: 47 ticks in the air from 15 tiles where it was 34, and 50 from 19. Against the rig's player, who gets out of the way of nothing on purpose, it landed 38 times of 96; before, 73 of 101. Onto a player standing still it still lands every time. If it should be as high as now and as hard to dodge as before, the way is a faster fall on the way down, a plunge: one number in `Foigh.Fight.cs`, not put in. Say. In his second phase the leap is tried from 40 tiles off, and is no higher from there than from 30 (§16.46).
- [ ] **After the passage Foigh is 70 tiles off, and he mostly walks it** — 70 tiles is where you asked the player to be put; and from far off, by your list for his second phase (§16.46), he mostly walks, at 1.15 px a tick. Standing still you have about seven seconds before he is within the 40 tiles he leaps from, with a step behind you or a knife about one time in four that he thinks (every 40 to 70 ticks). Say if he should close the distance some other way first, or walk faster in his second phase (`WalkSpeed` in `Foigh.Fight.cs`, one number). And in the hall 70 tiles from him may be against a wall or at the door: wherever it falls. Since §16.47: beyond the 40 tiles he leaps from he walks on 9.5 times in 11 that he thinks (a step behind you or a knife about one time in seven); the late cut closes up to 45 tiles in one go, but is only picked from within 39.
- [ ] **Foigh's leap with the hand out can be run from** — ten tiles in half a second, as asked, is 5.3 px a tick, and a player in any boots runs faster than that. In the rig it has a player who stands anywhere within 12 tiles of him, one who walks at him from 16, and one 60 px over the ground; one who runs from the moment he goes down is 20 tiles off when he lands, and one who only walks away from 150 px, without boots, gets clear narrowly. He wears the yellow star for 18 ticks on his knee before he leaves the ground; the two cuts before it throw whoever they land on about seven tiles, which is inside the leap. If it should be harder to get away from: further, or the same ten tiles in less time (`SpringFar` and `SpringTicks` in `FoighMoves.cs`). Say (PADWorkFlow §16.46).
- [ ] **Held by Foigh, the player is drawn over him** — the game draws any player over any enemy. Pulled in against him you hide a good part of him, and the sword is seen going in on one side of you and coming out on the other, not across you. The same holds on the sword of the one who drank. To be looked at in play: if he should be drawn over you while he has you, that is a change to how a held player is drawn, and it is not put in (PADWorkFlow §16.46).
- [ ] **Foigh's cut up out of the ground and the blow after it are two blows that cannot be parried** — the cut up of §16.47 throws whoever it lands on about 12 tiles into the air whatever shield they have; he is beside them up there before they are; and the blow that sends them down lands whatever grace the cut left them. The second is certain once the first has landed, and the two are twice the sword (300 / 400 / 460 by the world): what his third move's blow down does in one. Both wear the yellow star, by the mod's own rule (PAD_NPC_Plan §2.7), so neither can be parried. Against the rig's player, who gets out of the way of nothing on purpose, the cut landed 17 times of 19 in three fights of four minutes. It can be got out of the way of: a run from the moment he sets off (he runs at 9 px a tick, for 10 ticks at the least, with the star on him), a jump over him at a run, or a moment in which nothing can hurt you; standing, walking off, a jump where you stand, or a run begun once the blade is in the ground does not get out of it. If it should be softer: the second blow for less than the sword, or landing only on one who can be hurt by then (`Taken` in `Foigh.Fight.cs`), or the cut picked less often (its weight in `Choose`, 2). Say (PADWorkFlow §16.47).
- [ ] **Foigh's late cut leaves you beside him** — the line throws whoever it cuts the way he went, which is toward where he now stands, 96 px past where you were: you end about where he is, and his next move is picked from up close, after the second or two of §16.46. Turned aside, he reels as he does from any parry, which is backward, and his back is to you: he comes back toward you with the sword thrown up over him. Both follow from rules that were already his (a cut throws the way it goes; a parry sends him back). Not seen in play. If the line should throw you away from him, or he should reel away from you, each is one sign for this one move. Say (PADWorkFlow §16.47).
- [ ] **Where Foigh takes a player in the air to be going: sideways is not the game's sum** — where he steps up to is where you will be as his sword comes down, 30 ticks ahead at the most. Since §16.48 the way up and down of that is the game's own sum, read in its assembly and held against the game itself (its whole `Player.Update` run on a player with keys held: 227 flights, a quarter of a million askings, out by 0.003 px at the worst): a jump, every one of the game's 46 pairs of wings flying, hovering and gliding, rocket boots, a flying carpet, a slow fall, weight turned over, one who jumps again as they land, platforms, ceilings, and one he threw. §16.47 said here that wings and flying mounts are off in this mod: wrong. They are off until the Empress of Light is down; and his sum had flying on wings wrong (about 8 px by the time his sword comes down), which is mended. **Still not the game's**: how you go sideways. He takes your speed along the ground to stay as it is; the game slows one who lets go of the keys and quickens one who holds them, on wings most of all. Not measured. Say if that should be made the game's sum too. Also taken simply: a mount that flies or hovers and a wall you slide down (on as you go), a hook or a rope (where you hang), water and honey, a jump begun in the air (PADWorkFlow §16.47, §16.48).
- [ ] **Wings that may not be flown by still glide** — found while the rig was given the mod's own lock on wings (`PSItem`): before the Empress of Light is down a player with wings on cannot fly, but holding jump as they fall they come down at a third of a fall, and go faster sideways through the air than one with none on (5 px a tick against 3.1; 6.3 on wings that are theirs), because the game gives both to anyone who wears wings, time on them or not (`Player.Update`, 1.4.4). That is the mod's rule from before Foigh, as it has always been; not changed. For wings Foigh has cut off it is closed (`PisPlayer.PostUpdateMiscEffects`). If "bounded to the ground" should mean no gliding either, it is the same two lines for the lock (`Player.wingsLogic = 0` where `AllowedWings` is false). Say (PADWorkFlow §16.48).
- [ ] **While Foigh is in his third phase nobody flies, whoever he cut** — asked for: "from now wings are disabled", and by your answer everything that flies, until he is gone. Built as one flag for the world (`Foigh.WingsCut`, which `PisPlayer.AllowedWings` reads), so in multiplayer every player loses flight and has their wings not drawn while he is there, not only the one he stood behind. Rocket boots by themselves, a flying carpet and a jump in the air are left: they are not wings. If it should be only the player he cut, the flag becomes one per player and has to be sent. Say (PADWorkFlow §16.48).
- [ ] **Ultra Bleeding is three buffs, and its first second is 11 life** — asked for as one debuff, "Ultra Bleeding", "Bleeding out. Life does not regenerate". Built as Ultra Bleeding I, II and III (one, two and three wounds), each saying its own life a second (12, 24, 36), as the mod's other debuffs that come in strengths are: which one a player has is what the game already tells every machine. The first second of a wound takes 11, not 12: what was on its way back to you is lost first. Not his knives, nor a shot sent back; the same five seconds in every difficulty. Its three icons are stand-ins (PlaceholderTextures.md). Say if it should be one buff with a number on it (PADWorkFlow §16.48).
- [ ] **Ultra Bleeding spews blood** — asked for: "ultrableeding have blood particle spewing down". Each wound spews from a place of its own on you (the chest, the side behind, the hip), a spurt with every beat of the heart (five ticks of every 36, two drops a tick) and a run between (one tick in three), the wounds a third of a beat apart; so one, two or three spews. Every drop comes down to the ground under you and no further, and some of it lies there a while. Every machine sees it. Mine, all of it but the blood spewing down. Never seen in the game (PADWorkFlow §16.54).
- [ ] **What Foigh holds up after the cut is the wing item's picture** — the small picture of whatever wings you wear, as it is in your inventory, in his bottom hand. Wings as they are worn are drawn by the game pair by pair, each its own way, and that was not written again. With no wings on there is nothing in his hand, and the cut is made all the same. If it should be the wings as worn, or a picture of your own for all of them, say (PADWorkFlow §16.48).
- [ ] **Foigh's three cuts are one move, and each slash is one, aimed** — asked for: "he can also throw a flying slash projectile too for one of the moves but make it a move set tho", and then "remember the slash is multi directional so if ur on the ceiling it also hits you". Built as one move of three cuts, each letting one slash go at you wherever you are. Not built, and each the other way to read it: several moves of his third phase that each throw one; and several slashes at once from each cut, fanned out. Say if either was meant (PADWorkFlow §16.49). Since §16.52 the first of those other readings is as good as built beside it: a cut of his that ends with you out of its reach, the wheel and the sword driven deeper each let slashes go. The three cuts themselves are as they were, and picked as often (PADWorkFlow §16.52).
- [ ] **A flying slash that is parried is gone, and nothing happens to him** — a parry that faces it turns it aside: it ends where it was met, with the parry's sparks (for everybody, with more players than one), and that is all: no blow is dealt to him for it, and he does not reel (it is not his sword in his hand). His knives end the same way. If a parried slash should go back at him, as a shot of yours that he sends back comes at you, or should count toward staggering him, say (PADWorkFlow §16.49).
- [ ] **One who is behind Foigh as he lets a slash go is not cut at** — each goes at you anywhere from straight down, round through ahead of him and straight up, to 45 degrees past straight up over his own head. Further round than that (behind him, and not over him) it goes level the way he faces. He looks for you before each of the three cuts and comes round, so it takes getting behind him in the third to half of a second between his look and the cut. If a slash should be sent behind him as well, say (PADWorkFlow §16.49).
- [ ] **Half the sword's blow and a wound, three times in 2.7 seconds** — mine, all of it: a slash is half his sword's blow through armour and leaves one wound, so one who stands through all three takes a sword and a half and carries all three wounds (36 life a second for five seconds). Each can be jumped, parried, or outrun (it thins away after 59 tiles; the third after 56). Not played: whether that is too much or too little for something thrown is yours to say (PADWorkFlow §16.49).
- [ ] **Foigh's posture has no bar** — by request ("keep posture ui hidden"). His posture is there (what he turns aside wears at it, his own sword parried most; it breaks him for three seconds), and nothing on the screen says how worn it is: it is seen when he goes down. What would draw a bar over his head is kept in `Foigh.Draw.cs` (`DrawPosture`) behind one switch, `PostureBarShown`, which is off. If a sound, or a look of him as it nears breaking, is wanted in its place, say (PADWorkFlow §16.50).
- [ ] **How "parrying goes on for more than 2.5 secs" is counted** — as two and a half seconds in which Foigh keeps turning something aside, with no gap of half a second (`PressGap`, 30 ticks). In his second phase one second in three of a stream is let through, and that second ends the count unless he is at the cap (where everything is turned aside anyway): against the Zenith or the Last Prism he runs after the two and a half seconds; against something weak that he only holds off two times in three, it can be a good deal longer. The other reading is two and a half seconds of being shot at, whatever he does about it: one number (`PressGap` set to 75 or so). Say which (PADWorkFlow §16.50).
- [ ] **At the cap about two blows a second get through Foigh's guard** — with nothing more that can be taken from him he turns everything aside until half a second's worth can be taken again, and then the next hit lands for that. So what hits far harder than the cap lands twice a second, for 600 in Classic, 960 in Expert, 1,440 in Master each, and everything between is sparks. Mine: the other way to do it was to let every hit land for whatever had come back since the last, which is a hit every tick for 20 or so. Whether two a second reads as his guard or as your weapon failing is for the game to show (PADWorkFlow §16.50).
- [ ] **Under a stream held on him at arm's length Foigh does not wait between attacks** — up close, by request, he waits a second or two between attacks (§16.44). The run that answers two and a half seconds of holding something off is not held to that: against one who never lets up from beside him, the run's cut ends, he is still held at it, two and a half seconds have gone by in the cut itself, and he runs (a step) and cuts again. Every one of those cuts can be parried, and letting up for half a second ends it. Mine: say if the wait should hold there too (PADWorkFlow §16.50).
- [ ] **A stream of bullets sends one back a second** — bullets, arrows and knives one at a time are struck out of the air and half of those go back at you, as before. Four a second or more are a stream, held off a second at a time: of each second held, one goes back (100 through armour, broken by a parry or the first tile), while his sword is free for it. A spell, a beam or a blade is never sent back. Mine (PADWorkFlow §16.50).
- [ ] **A Last Prism raised near Foigh is rolled for once, as a spell seen coming** — the crystal in your hand is a thing of the game's that hurts, with a speed that is only where it is aimed. Raised within about 26 tiles of him while he walks, it is taken once for something on its way: two times in three (four in five in his third phase) he flash-steps in on you. From before §16.50, and left: the second delivery is to give him something meant for a beam being held (PADWorkFlow §16.50). Since §16.51, from his second phase it is not: what is held out in the hand is read as a thing held on him (three quarters of a second of it from 200 px or further, told once, and he answers that as he answers anything); in his first phase it is as above (PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **The sparks of a stream turned aside are seen by whoever's stream it is** — what Foigh turns aside is worked out on the machine of whoever shot it, and that machine shows the sparks and plays the steel. With more players than one the others see his guard go up and see him break, not each spark (PADWorkFlow §16.50).
- [ ] **The parry's stagger no longer ends at the look of a hit** — found while the cap was tested, and it is every enemy the parry staggers, not Foigh alone: the game calls an enemy's hit effect for the look of a hit as well as for a hit (a Daybreak or Bone Javelin stuck in it does so every half second), and the stagger woke at any such call, with the riposte's flash and no riposte. `PADStaggerNPC` now takes it for the riposte only if life was taken from the enemy, or it was killed outright. Not seen in the game (PADWorkFlow §16.50).

Nothing was drawn for him. If his is to be a hook of its own it is two files and their two names (PlaceholderTextures.md; PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **Any hit of Foigh's lets go a grapple and shuts wings, not the sword's alone** — asked for as "a hit cuts your grapple" and "a hit in the air grounds you". Mine: say if it should be the sword only, or everything but the knives (PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **What two seconds without wings shuts, and what it leaves** — shut: wings (gliding with them too), rocket boots, the Soaring Insignia, a flying carpet; a mount that flies or hovers is left and cannot be mounted again until it is over. Left alone: a jump in the air, whatever only slows a fall, a grapple (after its own second), everything in the hands. What the wings and boots had left as they were shut is theirs again when it is over, if they are still in the air. Over a long drop that is a fall for two seconds; in the hall it is a fall to the floor. The numbers are yours (a second, two seconds); what is shut besides wings and rocket boots is mine (PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **Pulled in on Foigh's hook, the keys are not yours** — bitten off the ground, you are pulled in to him at 15 px a tick: left, right, up, down, jump, the hook's key and the mount's are taken for as long as he pulls (70 ticks at the most; the whole length of his chain is 53), your own hooks are let go and a mount is left. What is in your hands is still yours: the blow that waits can be parried, and that is the way out of it. Whether that reads as his doing or as the game taking the keys away is for the game to show (PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **"He can jump super high" is the leap he had, raised** — from his second phase the leap onto you tops out a tile and a half over your feet wherever you are, up to forty-six tiles over where he left the ground (it was fourteen), going up at 15.5 px a tick for as long as it takes. The other reading is a jump of its own, straight up, to do something from up there: the third delivery's leap, turn and throw is to be that. Say if this one should not also be so high (PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **The fan of knives goes over one who stands** — five knives at once, twelve degrees apart. At one off the ground the middle knife is the one aimed. Mine (PADWorkFlow §16.51).
- [ ] **Foigh reads a swing from fifteen tiles** — a swing or a thrust of anything in the hand that touches nothing of him, from within 240 px, is told to him, and two times in three (four in five in his third phase) he is in front of you with the sword. 240 px is further than most swords reach: one swung at air a long way from him is read too. One number (`WhiffWithin` in `Foigh.Reach.cs`), if it should be nearer. A parry begun is not a swing (PADWorkFlow §16.51).

- [ ] **A hit of Foigh's goes on counting for a thirtieth of a second after he is gone** — a player's machine works a hit out before his own tick comes, so it is told he is there past his first phase by a mark he leaves each tick, good for two updates of the game. And two of him at once, one in his first phase and one past it, would each let go a grapple: he is not met twice (PADWorkFlow §16.51).

That is his tells seen and answered inside a third of a second. It is one number (`ThirdTempo` in `Foigh.Wild.cs`, 1.5); the waits between moves are two (`ThirdWaitLeast`, `ThirdWaitMost`). Only playing it can say (PADWorkFlow §16.52). Since §16.53 a parry already begun when one of his flying cuts or straight cuts reaches you turns it aside, its wind-up and all (they could not be answered by eye: PADWorkFlow §16.53); his sword in his hand is as it was.

In his first move, his third, his fourth's turn, the cut after the step behind, the hand's two, the blow out of the mill, the cut up out of the ground and the hook's blow. The half circle of §16.52 is gone. The shortest shown is a fifth of a second: only playing it can say whether the hair is seen soon enough to step out of, against the hall's wall (PADWorkFlow §16.53).

Nothing of it is shown, kept after the fight, or sent (PADWorkFlow §16.52).

The same of the straight cuts: from the tick one opens to 13 before. Mine: that his sword in his hand, his knives (17.5 px a tick) and his hook keep the old rule, the wind-up first. - [ ] **The ball answers too much from what fights for you in three seconds** — asked for: "if he takes too much minion damage within a time frame, he curl up into a ball and explode into 20 tile radius slashes aoe". Mine: "too much" is half of all the cap lets him lose in three seconds (1,800 of his 150,000 in a Classic world), counted on your own machine; no oftener than every ten seconds; in all three phases; as soon as he is free (on his feet, walking or with his sword up); nothing lands on him while he is curled up; one owed as a passage begins is forgotten. A summoner further than twenty tiles off is not reached by it at all (PADWorkFlow §16.53). Since §16.54 what fights for you has a cap of its own, half of the cap, and all of that in three seconds is what made the ball before: "too much" is now nine tenths of it (1,620 in a Classic world). Mine. Worked out apart from the rig: a summoner pressing that cap is answered every twelve to fifteen seconds, a long Stardust Dragon in his third phase about every forty, minions landing under half of it never (PADWorkFlow §16.54). Since §16.55 its reach is by phase: a summoner further than 18, 23 or 27 tiles from him (his first phase, his second, his third) is not reached by it (PADWorkFlow §16.55).

Built: what fights for you (minions, sentries, what those shoot) is turned aside two times in three, four in five in his third phase, in all three phases and in whatever move he is in, but not while he answers nothing; a minion turned aside is not hurt; it never wears his posture or sets him running; what lands of it takes no more than half of what the cap allows in any sixty ticks (600 in a Classic world), counted for each player; the ball's cuts cut down what they go through of it four times in nine, the Stardust Dragon whole. The fourth answer, one cooldown for the whole dragon, was already the game's own: §16.53 said each segment hits on its own, and was wrong; what was wrong was his turning a segment aside, which now makes the whole of it wait. Mine: the first phase and every move; sentries; the posture. Say if his first phase should keep its own rate, or sentries be spared (PADWorkFlow §16.54). Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.68): (1) it was so: the level dash held a dasher at `velocity.Y` 0, and the game takes a player not falling at all for one on the ground, so in the air it gave back every jump in the air, the wings' flight and the rocket boots' time, and let them jump as if off the ground; (2) a hair of a fall left to a dash in the air, 0.002 px a tick (`LevelDash.Hair`), a pixel in about eight seconds; (3) mine: within a step of the ground (less than 17 px, where the game's own step down could set them on it) the fall is still taken whole, as before, so that a dash along the ground, up and down slopes and stairs and over platforms goes exactly as it went (the same paths in the rig to the thousandth of a pixel). - [ ] **The level dash in the air: what came with it** — read in the game's code and measured in the rig (PADWorkFlow §16.68): a dash in the air is now slowed as the game slows one in the air, as its own dash is, and goes further: Tabi's 485 px along in 48 ticks where it went 343 in 35, the Shield of Cthulhu's 15 ticks where 13. It no longer counts as a landing for the player at the keyboard (no fall damage in the air in the middle of a fall; the fall counted from where it began, as for any fall). And a jump or flight used in a dash in the air is spent and lifts nothing till the dash is spent (a fart left and pressed: spent; wings held 31 ticks of a dash: 51 of their time down to 20; Rocket Boots held 25: 6 down to 3; all level); before, a jump off the ground was begun instead and all of it given back. Not asked, and not changed. Decide: keep these; or keep the old reach in the air; or have a dash keep the jump key till it is spent (nothing begun, nothing spent); or let a jump or flight begun in it lift, as the game's own dash does.

Mine: the name; 30 contact damage, 10 defence, a quarter of knockback, 4 silver; it hears any player through anything, 50 tiles across and 25 up or down; it gathers 0.15 a tick, runs on 8 tiles past, skids 0.4 a tick; it leaps down as far as 15 tiles, runs into holes up to 8 deep with nothing it can reach, and stands at the edge of deeper ones; it wanders at 1.2; the Pigron's sounds, lower; the bestiary line; its look, and its picture (a stand-in, PlaceholderTextures).

If player has bezoar in inventory, then there will be a button "The Bezoar" with its icon. clicking it will have the dryad thanking you and giving you the blessing." Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.75): (1) one chat in four, until the player has brought her one; (2) the line while they carry one and have not; (3) she takes the Bezoar (mine); (4) once for each player, saved with the player, so each in a world of several can (mine); (5) her two lines (mine).

Its Any Gold Bar and Any Copper Bar are new groups in `PADRecipes`. Say if it should be commented out as the others were (PADWorkFlow §16.75).
- [ ] **The pre-hardmode checklist: its targets and proposals are mine** — `PAD_Prehardmode_Checklist.md`, for "Create check list to rival theirs": the stages, the targets (Calamity's numbers less its rogue class) and every line marked (mine) are proposals, to keep, change or strike out (PADWorkFlow §16.75). Since §16.77 every count is re-run: Calamity's by where each item comes from, not by rarity alone, and what counts as before the Wall is my reading (section 1 of the checklist; its Light Red items one by one); ours with the Handswitch, which was missed (PADWorkFlow §16.77). Since §16.78 ranged are four, the Meat Hook counted as you count it, and the Worminator is in; the Vulture Staff is with what nothing gives yet (PADWorkFlow §16.78).
- [ ] **Worminator: readings to confirm** — asked for: "Create new Summoner Weapon: Worminator prehardmode dropped by giant worm 1/5 chance Summons a worm, base 3 body segment.(+1 head 1 tail) each additional minion adds 1 segment", "Currently port vanilla textures onto the proj folder so i can fix and use.", "Same deal with the stardust dragon but this one only in the ground, so AI like giant worm, 2x speed.", "8 damage all around." Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.78 has each): (1) the first summon makes the head, three segments and the tail and takes one minion slot (two of the three take none), each summon after it one more segment and one more slot; (2) the Stardust Dragon's deal: one projectile a segment, the head and the tail never let go to make room for another minion, a body let go from the middle closing the worm up; (3) only in the ground: the Giant Worm's own moving, steering in the ground and falling out of it, and drawn behind the blocks as it is; (4) 2x speed: two of the Giant Worm's moves a tick, so that it goes the Giant Worm's way at twice its pace; (5) 8 damage all around: every segment hits for 8, each on its own; (6) the textures: the Giant Worm's head, body and tail copied as they are into `Content/Projectiles/Summoner`, the item the game's Worm (the bait); (7) one in 5 from the Giant Worm, in every mode. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **Worminator: mine, to keep or change** — knockback 2, Blue, 1 gold, the staff's use (as Hoard of Undead's); one move a tick, not two, with nothing to fight and within 480 of its owner; what it goes for: the enemy marked within 1,200 of its owner, else the nearest within 700, kept while it stays within 700; with nothing to fight, circling about 9 blocks under the ground below its owner, swinging 6 to each side; more than 2,400 from its owner, put back in the ground under them; the same enemy again after 12 ticks (the Stardust Dragon's segments: 7), an enemy knocked away from the segment that hit it; its digging sound at a quarter of the Giant Worm's loudness; a spray of the block's dust where it breaks out of the ground and goes back in; coming up under the cursor, or under its owner with no ground within 40 blocks under the cursor; the two segments that take no slot drawn as bodies; the buff's name, "Worminator", the tooltips, and the flavour line 'It is always under you.' (PADWorkFlow §16.78).

I guess like finch staff but you can use your creativity to make it more interesting." Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.78): (1) one vulture a summon, one minion slot each; (2) "modify vultures textures": the game's Vulture's picture copied as it is, for you to change, as you asked for the worm's; (3) the shoulders and the towers: the first on the shoulder the owner faces with, the second on the other, the third on the first's back, and so on, both towers behind the owner; (4) like the Finch Staff: placed on its owner as the Baby Finch is, and brought back as it is past 2,000. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **Vulture Staff: mine, to keep or change** — all its numbers: 12 damage (18 since your edit, seen at §16.79), knockback 3, Blue, 1 gold; the towers 32 apart at the shoulders and 6 further apart for each vulture up, each vulture 24 above the one under it; the hop across when its owner turns, the balancing flaps, the stretches and the lean; what they go for: of the enemies within 640 of their owner and in their sight, the one with the least share of its life left, kept while it has no more than a twentieth more than that, the marked one within 1,000 before any; the circle over it (220 by 77, 150 above it, a turn every 3 seconds), the dives in turn (48 ticks of circling between dives, 18 apart at the first take-off, 1.1 faster a tick to 15, 40 ticks at most), the climb after (18 ticks) and the streak behind a dive; 25% more against an enemy under half its life; the frenzy (a kill halves the circling between dives for 3 seconds, the streaks red); the sounds (the wings' `Item32` taking off, the Vulture's own hurt sound higher for a screech); the same enemy again after 20 ticks; the names "Vulture Staff" and "Vultures", the tooltips, and the flavour line 'They are very good at waiting.' (PADWorkFlow §16.78).

- [ ] **The Vast Ocean: readings to confirm** — asked for: "a subworld where it is  just the ocean,  take the thrice as deep as the deepest place of the ocean in normal world, witha  platform on the surface, water un buckettable, and sendiments are sand, shelfishes and the [ambient objects]" and "All functions that can be done at the beach can be done here". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.79): (1) "the deepest place": the deepest the game's beach pass can dig, 149 rows with every roll at its deepest, so 447 (the deepest seen in 20,000 rolled worlds was 119), and since §16.81, asked again, 1788 (twelve times 149) and eighteen times as wide; (2) "un buckettable": the Empty Bucket and the Super and Ultra Absorbant Sponges cannot be used; (3) "ambient objects": of the wiki's list the ones a sea would hold (stones, bones and skulls, wood, marble and gold), with the game's shells, starfish, coral and seaweed; (4) the beach's functions: the game takes every column of it for the sea (since §16.81 by the mod's edits to it, ExtraLargeOcean and SubworldSea). Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean: mine, to keep or change** — 13662 by 3160, the water 2688 rows deep at its deepest in twelve sections of 224 (§16.85; 2260 high, 1788 of water in sections of 149, in §16.83), 358 rows of sky over the water (§16.83; 2081 high with 179 of sky in §16.81, 759 by 740 before); the surface line 70 rows under the water (the sea's fishing; where Duke Fishron rages); dunes 24 high, sand 14 deep, hardened sand to 32; 14 to 19 drifts of Shell Pile to every 759 columns; the floor's odds on level ground (large piles 4 in 100, small 6 and 10, shells 14, coral 11, seaweed 15, grown 2 to 12 tall); the platform (a deck 42 long, its body, four piers 34, 52, 46 and 38 rows down, the dais and two broken columns), kept whole; the creatures put down in open water by depth (the surface, 179 rows, 420 rows, deeper: four fifths of a section and fifteen eighths; 120 and 280 before §16.85) and their odds, no gulls (§16.83), dolphins in pods of three to five, sharks only in the 2nd and 3rd sections, one spawn in six there, one to three at a time within 8 columns and 4 rows of each other (§16.84); "The sea will not be carried."; the clock carried both ways; no meteor in any subworld (PADWorkFlow §16.79, §16.83, §16.84, §16.85).
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean's look: mine** — the water's colour by depth as it is to be seen through the game's own water over it (10 stops, the surface to the end of the 6th: dark navy at the end of the 4th, black at the end of the 6th) and how much of the game's water behind it shows near the surface; the haze on the horizon; the rays, in front of everything (§16.83): the sun's 60 at most and the moon's 28, gathered under the sun or the moon (§16.85: all lit right under it, falling away over about a quarter of the screen to one in seven far off; 26 and 12, even, before), thin (9 to 39 pixels, nearly one in five broad and soft), slanting from where the sun or moon is in the sky and bent by the water, drawing together 11 degrees at 960 pixels out, each lit 1.6 to 4.4 seconds (one turn in four dark), brighter in drifting patches (0.3 to 1.3 times), a soft glow 3.5 times as wide at 0.18, 0.6 brighter over their first 40 to 90 rows, up to three sparkles in each, none under the platform, the sun's through three sections and the moon's through one, as bright as the moon is full, two and a half times as bright to an eye at the end of the 3rd; glints along the surface (by day a fifth of its 18 pixel cells lit at a time, by night fewer and pale; §16.85: under the sun six in ten falling to one in seven, a fifth of the screen to either side, under the moon one in two falling to one in twenty-three, an eighth to either side, with a soft band of its light on the surface under it, the moon's path); the snow, and the specks that glow from the 2nd section to the 6th; the shadows going by (a shark two passes in three, about one in eight of those the Duke, 134 to 358 rows down, 0.6 to 1.6 sections; 90 to 240 before §16.85); the waves (in a calm a swell every 12 seconds for 4, one in four flat, crests 14 tiles apart; §16.85: rougher with the wind and the rain, to every 5 seconds, none flat, 2.3 times as high, crests 9 tiles apart running with the wind); the shaders (deep blue from a fifth at the surface to near three quarters by the end of the 6th, old gold over the water by day, the waver under it; from the 7th the murk, to 0.82, and the silt) (PADWorkFlow §16.79, §16.82, §16.83, §16.85).
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean's depths: readings to confirm** — asked for: "divide the depth into 12 section, after section 2, any lightsource spread is limited and is decreased by half, after section 4 any lightsource only light itslef and not surrounding it (lightsticks and hooks and projectiles), it is pitch dark and after section 6 put on some shader that makes it very hard to see, sound is also distorted and the music is distorted too like how it is realistic to a dive. After section 7 its pure quiet and pitch black, and below this nothing spawns" and "below section 6 to section 10 is a new biome: Forevernight zone. (no NPC spawn), from section 10-12, it is new biome: Abyssal Trench. After section 7, every block you place will shakes and break within 1.3 seconds." Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.82): (1) twelve sections from the water's surface row, the floor in the 12th (149 rows each; 224 since §16.85); (2) "after section N" as from section N + 1: light halved from the 3rd, a light's own cell only from the 5th, the murk and the dive's sound from the 7th, silence and the breaking from the 8th; (3) nothing spawns from the 7th down, invasions too; (4) the Forevernight Zone the 7th to the 9th, the Abyssal Trench the 10th to the 12th (as asked they share the 10th); (5) "every block you place": every tile a player puts down (platforms, torches, furniture too), broken as if mined so its item comes back, walls left; (6) the sun's rays through the 1st to the 3rd, the moon's a third of that. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean's depths: mine, to keep or change** — the dive in the 7th: every sound 0.4 of an octave down, at 0.6, muffled to about 350 Hz; the game's music at 0.45 swelling and sinking by 0.15 (a track played from a file also 0.45 of an octave down and muffled); the ambient loops at half; the breaking: 78 ticks (1.3 s), grit shed more as it goes, a whole block shaking 0.5 to 3 pixels; the 7th's last specks and the void that passes over them (three screens long, every 41 seconds, 7 times in 10); the faintest light given to the water between the surface and the surface line (PADWorkFlow §16.82).
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean, §16.83: readings to confirm** — asked for: "its taking a real ass long time to traverse the gateway bruh", "can you also make the rays directional to the sun? and actually realisitc? thinner rays would work better and shinnings too. little sparkles and the rays disappear and reappear like reali life snorkeling. There is also seaguls spawning too.", "and raise the roof by double so we can have some head space for a boss fight", "optimize it" and "but not at the expense of the beauty". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.83): (1) "raise the roof by double": the sky over the water doubled, 179 rows to 358, the water and all under it as it was; (2) "optimize it": the trip made short (the game's settling of the sea's water, minutes on every trip in, is not run there), nothing taken from the look; (3) "seaguls spawning": the gulls put on the water no longer come, and the game's flocks far off in the sky behind (pictures, not creatures) are left; (4) "directional to the sun": slanting from where the sun or moon is in the game's sky, bent by the water. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **§16.84: readings to confirm** — asked for: "slimy saddle raise speed is exponential and keep that potential until he pops out of the water", "applies for all water", "same with gelatinous pillion", "dolphins are spawn in groups of 3-5" and "sharks spawns in group of 1-3 in section 2-3". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.84): (1) the Slimy Saddle's slime and the Gelatinous Pillion's come up through water faster by a share of their speed each tick, and keep at the surface what they have; (2) "all water": water in every world, the Vast Ocean or not; honey, lava and shimmer as the game has them; (3) dolphins in pods in the Vast Ocean, where the mod puts them down; the game's own seas keep its single dolphins; (4) sharks in the 2nd and 3rd sections only, none in the 1st or from the 4th down. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The slime mounts' rise: mine, to keep or change** — twice as fast every 20 ticks (2 to the twentieth root a tick), from the game's 4 to 32 a tick, and no faster (the fastest the game moves a player through water without passing a block); a jump under way ended at the surface so it does not hold the speed down; from 27 rows of water or more, the full speed, and by the game's own rules after it some 113 rows up with jump not held, 162 with it held (PADWorkFlow §16.84).
- [ ] **§16.85: readings to confirm** — asked for: "1, added a 5 min long surf sample and 4 min long wind, please dissect it and retune it and play randomly a piece from that for the ambiance with variations, diff distances, Yes duke's long, low roar can be good. Added whale hums too, theres a 10 min long piece do the same. 3, nice add this 4, Yes you can make it animate better. 5, excellent", "also anything below the sun or moon shines more rays than the rest is sparse" and "Also make the ocean 1.5x as deep, keep the 12 sections tho". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.85): (1) "dissect": the recordings cut where each is quietest between one wave, gust or song and the next, 68 pieces; (2) "retune": cleaned and levelled, and each played at a pitch of its own, not changed in key; (3) "diff distances": the surf from points along the surface near, further or far, the wind from upwind going by, the whales mostly far off; (4) "duke's long, low roar": the game's own roar of Duke Fishron an octave down, three over each other, every few minutes while he is not about; (5) "3": bubbles from players, from creatures and in columns from the dark; (6) "4": the sea in a storm: the swells by the weather, rain on the water, whitecaps and spray, lightning through the water; (7) "5": the glowing wake where the water is dark, and the moon's path; (8) the rays and the glints gathered under the sun or the moon, few elsewhere; (9) "1.5x as deep": 2688 rows, twelve sections of 224 (half again is 2682, 223.5 a section; in whole rows six deeper). Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean's sounds: mine, to keep or change** — the cuts (surf 6 to 14 seconds, wind 12 to 22, songs 8 to 18; 18 songs kept of the long recording), the cleaning (the surf under 35 Hz off and over 5 kHz 3 dB down, the wind under 30 Hz off, the songs 25 Hz to 3.5 kHz) and the levels (each surf and wind as loud again as half the difference it had); the surf two at once (three when rough), 200 to 650, 800 to 1400 or 1600 to 2300 pixels off (35, 40 and 25 in 100), 0.08 of an octave down to 0.06 up, heard to 160 rows over the water and 120 under; the wind one at once (two when strong), 250 to 700 pixels upwind, 0.12 of an octave higher in the strongest, giving way a third to the game's own; the whales from 6 rows down, every 14 to 40 seconds, one in four a call, 55, 35 and 10 in 100 far, nearer and near, to 0.3 of an octave lower by the end of the 6th, one in three answered; the Duke every 2.5 to 6 minutes, 300 to 900 pixels aside and 1300 to 1800 below, three roars over 1.5 seconds an octave down (PADWorkFlow §16.85).

Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.85): (1) sold by the Mechanic for 20 gold, before her mood moves the price; (2) "Resistence" spelled "Resistance" in the game; (3) "Anchored on the shoulder": an accessory, its lamp drawn on the wearer's front shoulder, turning to its aim; (4) mode 1, the glow, 6.1 tiles in air and 3.7 in water; mode 2, the beam, 20 degrees across (10 either side of the aim), 23 tiles in air and 13 in water, with the glow about the lamp down to 1.2; (5) "dim": the glow 0.7 of full light at the lamp, falling off; (6) "tiles in front will hinder scan rays": the light in straight rays, each stopped by the first solid block, which it lights, shadow behind; platforms and actuated blocks let it by; (7) "Use light physics": falling off as light spreads, taken away exponentially along its way, water taking the red first; (8) "the only actual light source in the vast ocean": laid in after the game spreads its lights, so the depths' rules on light do not hold it back; (9) "dim it the more it goes out": to the least that can be seen at the end of its reach, fading out over the last of it; (10) "Both": the key (L to begin with) and a right click on it in its slot, or on another in the inventory while one is worn; not worn, a right click puts it on as usual. Say which of these meant something else.

- [ ] **§16.87: readings to confirm** — asked for: "Maybe deepen the sendiment too, add ocasional rocks and ice pillars, pointy and curl in clusters", "there should also be mountains and hills in the sendiments and abnormalties and a deep trench hole with caverns", "mountains can rise from section 12-11 to section 10 or 9", "with clear path cave ways", "even smaller rocks boulders too", "can use mud as floor too", "and clay too", "geyser field too with its equivelent ground, actually do research and improve the ocean floor", "skeletal remains are good additions", "and obsidians flow too ig? DO YOUR RESEARCH!!!!", "You know you can also create ambiance tiles for extra ambiances, like smoke/gas emitting things and Coral reefs and sponges and other tiles that is needed", "Just make it look beautiful, and ofc everything is engulfed in shadow" and "be sure to also create particles for those ambience tiles too". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.87): (1) "deepen the sendiment": 520 rows of ground under the dunes' lowest (73 before), sediment in beds over basalt; (2) "ice pillars": clusters of spires on the open floor, ice and rock by turns, curling outward, the middle one tallest (no ice forms on a real deep floor; laid as asked); (3) "abnormalties": things not of the sea as known (moss gardens, geodes, standing stones, sinkholes, a skeleton bigger than any whale); (4) "mountains": five ranges of seamounts, feet in the 12th or 11th section, summits in the 9th or 10th; (5) "clear path cave ways": ways through each range and off the trench, open at both ends, swum end to end; (6) "mud", "clay": red clay and brown mud by stretch under the depth where shells dissolve (half way down the 11th section), pale sand over it; (7) "geyser field": the sea has none (water does not boil at that pressure); its own are hydrothermal vents: black smokers on a sulfide mound, white carbonate chimneys on green mantle rock, vents in the tallest summit's crater, and cold seeps; (8) "obsidians flow": undersea lava is nearly all basalt, so obsidian as its glass (the pillows' skins, the sheets' crusts) and one silicic dome with an obsidian flow (as at Havre, 2012); (9) "ambiance tiles": fifteen tiles of the deep's own life and vents, scenery, not items; (10) "engulfed in shadow": no light added but the black smokers' dull red mouths and the octocorals' glow where touched; (11) "particles": smoke, shimmer, gas bubbles, flecks, specks, plankton, glowing motes. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The Vast Ocean's floor (§16.87): mine, to keep or change** — the compensation depth half way down the 11th section; sand 6 rows, hardened sand to 28, sandstone; clay and mud beds to 28 rows, then mudstone; basalt from 150 rows down; hills 16 to 23, 30 to 170 rows high; ranges at 0.11, 0.29, 0.45, 0.60 and 0.86 of the width, the tallest's summit 8.3 to 8.55 sections down, the rest 8.6 to 9.7; one crater (16 to 21 deep), one guyot; caves 2.8 to 3.4 in radius; the trench at 0.69 of the width, 200 to 230 across, 430 rows deep, caverns at five depths; one field of black smokers (5 to 8 chimneys, the tallest 26 to 41), one of white chimneys (7 to 11, the tallest 42 to 55), two seeps, three mud volcanoes, three pockmark fields, four nodule fields, three pillow piles, up to two lava tongues, one obsidian dome; sixteen spire clusters (the middle spire 50 to 75), a rock every 60 columns tried, a boulder every 14; three whale falls, one great skeleton, two fossil reptiles; six moss gardens, three geodes, two rows of standing stones, three sinkholes; the life (312 to 356 a world) where each kind lives, its numbers and its light; no seaweed or beach coral; starfish only under the compensation depth (PADWorkFlow §16.87).
- [ ] **The sea life's pictures (§16.87)** — the fifteen tile sheets (`Content/Tiles/SeaFloor/`) and the five particles (`Content/Particles/VentSmoke.png`, `SeaFloc.png`, `SeaPlankton.png`, `SeaGlow.png`, `VentShimmer.png`) are mine, drawn by code; redraw any on the same frames (PlaceholderTextures.md; `SeaLifeCatalog_1687.png` shows them all).
- [ ] **§16.88: readings to confirm** — asked for: "1000- 1300 tiles to the left is an island floating in the sea, around 300 tile wide, having a large light house on the rock like this", "I like the jagged rocks", "2nd pic from reddit looks beautiful" (chosen: "Floats", "Red & white, rooms"), "why is this gradiant golden thing side way bruh", "i prefer once in a while", "it can be a bit more navy, its too pristine now", "can you make feather more accumulated at the surface?", "maybe by half height?", "theres shines on the surface u can clearly see a seperation of gray and purple dark", "use bricks colored white wall inside is ok, make it elevated like this with pillar statues and other statues like this, with marble and other tiles painted white or cream. create the stair case like that. windows like that too", "paint mixing and hammering" and "make sure the stone pillar here is walled and leave a space to squeeze through". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.88): (1) the island's rock from 1312 to 982 columns west of the deck's middle, 331 wide, a sea stack off it on a ledge to 1330, floating, two chains down to anchors on the floor; (2) "jagged rocks": a headland of crags with teeth along their ridges and six pinnacles; (3) "2nd pic": the lighthouse in section, every storey fitted out, a stair up the middle; (4) "side way": the fan of light under the sun sheared, not turned, its top along the surface at any hour; (5) "once in a while": a set of three to five waves about once a minute in calm weather, every 15 seconds at the roughest, left to die down; (6) "more navy": the water at the end of the 6th section (2, 5, 22) and from the Abyssal Trench's top (3, 8, 34), not black; (7) "feather", "seperation of gray and purple dark": the light on the surface (the glints, the moon's path, the whitecaps) drawn in the water's top pixels so the game's waves carry it, nothing of the sea's own drawn over the line; "maybe by half height?" taken as how far down the light sits, the waves left as high as they were; (8) the tower: bands of red brick and of brick painted white, each brick's paint varied; inside, whitewashed brick and red brick in stepped patches; raised on a white plinth with a colonnade of pillar statues and Medusa's and a hoplite's statues, all painted white; a stair turning up a shaft of dark boards; windows of glass blocks over an iron lattice painted brown in white frames; (9) "walled": the stack, its shelf and the arch's west leg walled behind with their own stone, and a passage three high cut through the leg into the arch and the cave behind it. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The lighthouse island (§16.88): mine, to keep or change** — its place (1312 to 982 columns west of the deck's middle) and its shape; the plateau 30 rows over the sea; the lighthouse 90 rows from the plateau to its vane: a plinth 8 high, five storeys of 12 rows, 31 wide narrowing to 29 and 27; red bands on the ground floor, the third and the watch room; the brick's paint (one red brick in twelve deep red, one in twenty brown; one white one in ten unpainted, one in twenty grey); the rooms and what is in them (the hall and the store, the kitchen and the dining room, the keeper's room and his assistant's, the library and the study, the watch room); the lights (gold chandeliers, brass lanterns, golden lamps, plain candles; carriage lanterns in the oil house), none boreal (the game lights those cyan); the stair of pale wood platforms in a shaft of dark boards; the statues (four pillars, Medusa, a hoplite); the oil house; the fisherman's house with its blue roof, the pier, the skiff and the sloop; the lens that cannot be broken; the beams (two, a turn in 12 seconds, a flash every 6, lit from half an hour before dusk to half an hour after dawn); 11 chests and what they hold (PADWorkFlow §16.88).
- [ ] **The lighthouse's pictures (§16.88)** — `Content/Tiles/Lighthouse/LighthouseLens.png` (the lens, eight frames to its half turn) and `LighthouseBeam.png` (a beam) are mine, drawn by code; redraw either on the same frames (PlaceholderTextures.md; `LighthouseTowerNight_1688.png` shows both).

Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.89): (1) the M.U.P.R.A.L's beam to 15 tiles through air and 11 through water, the glow (6.1, 3.7) and the little glow left with the beam (1.2) as they were; (2) 30 degrees across, 15 either side of the aim; (3) "super fine": rays an eighth of a degree apart across the beam (241), a degree apart all about for the little glow (360), half a degree apart for the glow (720), each followed exactly through every tile it crosses; (4) "dont light it block by block, any thing passed by the ray is lit up": what the rays pass is lit, to the pixel, the edges of the beam and of every shadow where the rays are, the light falling off smoothly as they lay it; (5) "only the part within the path of the ray": a ray goes into the first solid block it meets, lights the part of it in its path, dimming evenly to nothing a tile deep, and goes no further; a half block or a slope only where it crosses the solid part. - [ ] **The water's weight, the incantation, the beam and the shafts (§16.91): mine, to keep or change** — the warning 40 rows (80 feet) over where it begins, told once each way down; the screen's corners darkening, deep blue going to a dark red, pressing on each beat; the heart, 62 to 150 beats a minute; the Depth Meter amber, then red; the three death lines and the two for the crush, its crunch and shake; the heart and the crunch heard through the silence; none of it under Journey's godmode. Water! Resuscitate Me!: Abjuration, the new element Water (`#0e3a52` outlined in `#3fb8d6`), 30 mana, use time 30, Blue; cast again only in its last 30 seconds; one in 20 Wooden, 10 Iron, 5 Golden, 4 Ocean Crates and their hard mode kinds. The trench: a song every 28 to 70 seconds, 50 to 140 tiles under, a knock every 2.5 to 10 seconds, one in four doubled. The beam: its light ending a quarter past the cursor, about a quarter of full at the cursor, at most 100 tiles, 0.15 of full left at its end once it reaches half again as far as before. The shafts: a degree either way and half a degree of wavering; four tiles both ways to stop the light; 48 tiles looked back toward it over the water. A creature within 20 tiles of a subworld's edge taken away (PADWorkFlow §16.91).

- [ ] **The water's weight on creatures, the caves and the Crevice Eel (§16.92): mine, to keep or change** — who is immune and how deep each creature is safe; a pressed creature darkening toward red. The eel: 2400 life, 16 armour, 85 from the head and 55 from the body, 50 silver and no drops; it strikes within 7 tiles, hunts within 60 by the way through the caves (90 if you bleed), lunges within 10, loses you past 100; 9, 5 and 10.5 pixels a tick striking, hunting and lunging; its bite 5 to 10 seconds of Bleeding; two at most within 150 tiles of a player, one put every 8 to 20 seconds within 110 tiles and out of sight; its name (PADWorkFlow §16.92).

Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The Crevice Eel's hunting, its dens and the caves' maze (§16.96): mine, to keep or change** — watching from 22 tiles, its jaws 26 pixels out of the crevice, turning up to 49 degrees; drawn back from 11 tiles; the strike's coil 8 ticks, the strike 12.5 pixels a tick (9 before); waiting 1.5 to 5 seconds before it hunts (a third of a second to one if they bleed); the seize half a second, let go 2.5 tiles from its crevice, torn free beyond 6; stalking at 3.4 pixels a tick (4.8 far off), creeping at 2.2 within 18 tiles, a stop of 40 ticks in every 150; the lunge's coil 12 ticks, the lunge 13 pixels a tick (10.5 before) for 18 ticks at the most; shaking 16 ticks; backing off 30 ticks at 2.8; holding off at 13 tiles; waking others within 48 tiles; the body's waves 5 pixels at the tail; its bite 85 and its body's 55, armour 16, as before. The caves: down to 225 rows under the sediment's top; a room for every 5000 cells of rock, 16 to 64 in a range; a knot for every 2600, 20 to 110; four in ten of each one's four nearest joined besides the tree; a bend for every 22 cells of a way, out up to a sixth of it; dead ends off three rooms in five and a third of the knots, one in three forking; four to six mouths. Dens: rooms whose half width times half height is 300 or more; four great caverns in five, two halls and domes in five; three to six crevices tried for, within 22 cells of the first; groups from crevices 40 tiles apart or less, two to four eels. Floating rocks: four to eight in a den's room, two to five in three big rooms in five, half obsidian, four cells clear about each, nine from where a way comes in, ten from a crevice's mouth (PADWorkFlow §16.96).
- [ ] **§16.97: readings to confirm** — asked for: "for ambiance: need more seagulls to fly in patches prob patches of 5- 13 across the sea or at the light house". Read as (PADWorkFlow §16.97): (1) for the looks only: flocks drawn over the sea, not the game's gull creatures (nothing to hit or catch; the gulls the sea's spawner put on the water stay out, as since §16.83), each player's own; (2) in patches of five to thirteen; (3) across the sea: now and then a flock crossing the screen over the open water, behind the world's tiles; (4) at the lighthouse: a flock wheeling round the tower, in front of it on the near side and behind it on the far, now and then two, breaking off after a few minutes and another coming; (5) when the game has its gulls: by day, not in the rain or an eclipse; with the game's own gull cry now and then. Say which of these meant something else.
- [ ] **The gulls (§16.97): mine, to keep or change** — the game's picture of its flocks' gull, near half as big again (six flocks in ten), far as it is; across the sea a flock every 6 to 21 seconds while the sky over the water is on the screen (longer in the first and last hour of the day), three at a time at most, three in four with the wind at 2.2 to 4.2 pixels a tick, one in four against it at 1.4 to 2.6, far ones three quarters as fast, between 3 and 170 tiles over the water; a patch's places in an oval 26 pixels and 10 more a gull either way across and 8 and 4 more a gull up and down, 24 apart at least (half as much again near); at the lighthouse within 320 columns of it, from half an hour after dawn, a circle 26 to 40 tiles out, its middle from 16 tiles over the lantern to 30 under it, tilted 8 to 20 hundredths of its width, 2.2 to 2.8 pixels a tick, for 90 to 240 seconds, the next 20 to 60 seconds after; a second flock two times in five, looked for every 40 to 120 seconds, 14 tiles above or below at least; gone from 880 ticks before dusk, none gathering in the last half hour; the far half of a circle down to 86 hundredths of the light; wings a picture every 3 to 5 ticks, gliding 40 to 150 ticks between 2 to 5 wingbeats going across, 120 to 420 between 1 or 2 at the lighthouse; a cry one gull in 3600 a tick, half a second apart at least (PADWorkFlow §16.97).

Mycelium, Finish Them: 14 damage, 24 mana, 40 ticks and 10 between casts, Blue, 40 silver; the ring 10 seconds, 7 tiles either side and 6 up, a hurt every 30 ticks, 9 caps; the caps where something died: 6 at most, a spore every 48 ticks at an enemy within 12 tiles, 12 seconds; one in 8 from anything killed in the mushroom caves underground (by request); the element Fungal (`#1b1554` filled, `#6fb6ff` stroked). The Defacer: 36 damage, knockback 7, 42 ticks to lift all the way, 14 to bring down, 55% to 220% by the lift, its wielder at 70% speed while it is lifted, Defaced for 8 seconds (10 defense ignored), Green, 60 silver; up to six dressers in a world, the first with a Defacer, one in four of the rest. The Parade Sabre: 20 damage, 22 ticks, knockback 4.5, Blue, 1 gold 50 silver; 120 hits of gold leaf; the Plain Sabre 28 damage, 20 ticks, knockback 5, Green, 60 silver; in the first armoury chest Grace lays, one in four after it. The Signal Pistol: 14 damage, 24 ticks, knockback 2, one round and 150 ticks to reload, Blue, 80 silver; the flare 6 seconds, stuck in an enemy 60% of the damage every 30 ticks, what swims within 30 tiles drawn to it, Darkness on its owner while it burns.
