## Known rough edges

- [ ] `QuickSpawnClonedItem` obsolete warnings.
- [ ] `AshDissolve.cs:75` fade bug.
- [ ] `Content/Projectiles/Ranged/ChlorophyteBullet.cs` / `.png` — was marked for deletion.
- [ ] `Content/NPCs/Legends/SlimeMass.cs`, `Content/Sounds/AbomanationWarn.ogg`, the `Content/Tiles/SealGlass.cs` stub — to delete.
- [ ] `AquaticFirearmsBattery.cs` doc comments still say damage ×2/3; the code is gun + ball.
- [ ] Placeholder art — every row in `PlaceholderTextures.md`.
- [ ] `description.txt` is still the template placeholder. `build.txt` version is `1.0` — set it honestly.
- [ ] **'Dame' Quilt's bar after her fall** — `DonaQuinn.cs` sets `NPC.boss = fighting`, meaning the bar to go at the fall, but nothing that draws the bar reads that flag: vanilla tracks any active NPC with a boss head, and `BossPlate.Track` holds the bar while she is active with life over 0. Read off the code, not seen in play: check whether an empty bar stays up through the plea and the talk (PADWorkFlow §16.22).
- [ ] **The Megashark's other drops** — its comment says the kill is worth one gun and nothing else, but it is a boss with no `BossLoot` override, so vanilla adds 5 to 15 Lesser Healing Potions and 5 to 9 hearts. Keep them or cut them (PADWorkFlow §16.22).
- [ ] **Amalgaermis's voice has not been heard** — `Content/Sounds/Abomanation*.ogg`, 20 files besides the screech, made by synthesis and only measured (level, where their energy sits). Listen to each; any that is wrong can be remade.
- [ ] **Amalgaermis's old rush misses** — the rush that climbs over you passes a player standing still about nine tiles to one side (measured in the simulator; it always did). Kept as the rarer of the two. Fix it, keep it as a feint, or cut it (`Abomanation.ThroughChance`).
- [ ] **`Content/Particles/DustFog.png` is not premultiplied** — white with the shape only in its alpha, drawn in the premultiplied pass, so the colour does not fall away with the alpha. King Slime's slam, the Queen Slime, Cold Call and now Amalgaermis's ground all use it as it is. Premultiplying the file changes all of them at once: look before and after.
- [ ] `PieceOfPaper.cs`: the comment over the class describes the Grand map as it was before you rewrote its lines (it still names the graveyard, and quotes "the vast jungle and iceland").
- [ ] **The cleanup strips a long way down** — `GraceCleanupPass.Strip` takes every framed tile from `worldSurface - 400` to `worldSurface + 40`, and under the Cleared Ground, the Mile and the plain the surface is about 150 rows over that line. In Far Tract of Potions there is no pot, chest, heart or stalactite between y 700 and 845 under the Cleared Ground, and 590 pots and 4 hearts in the 150 rows below. Meant, or should its reach follow the ground?
- [ ] **The snow is not where `Layout` puts it** — in Far Tract of Potions the surface snow runs x 6332 to 7306, 29 tiles from spawn; `SnowStart` and `SnowEnd` say 6825 to 7475. Seen while testing, not looked into.
- [ ] **Boss Active does not stop everything** — it stops blocks and walls being placed, the pick, the axe, the hammer, the shovel, buckets and the drill (vanilla's `noBuilding`), and the box stops explosions. Not stopped: a tile put down by a projectile (a dirt bomb, the Sandgun), liquid from a bomb or a rocket, wire, paint, a weapon cutting grass or breaking pots. Vanilla has no one place those go through (PADWorkFlow §16.25).
- [ ] **The flattest place, in a world made before the islands came off** — it is looked for from the ground under the first player to stand on the Cleared Ground. In a world that still has its floating islands (Far Tract of Potions), a player standing on one of them starts the walk up there, and the place found is another, shorter one. `/dona` says where it is; `/dona reset` forgets it. New worlds have no islands there.
- [ ] **The Iron Flow's and the parry's sounds have not been heard** — the cuts and the parry's sweep are `SoundID.Item1` at four pitches; a parry landed is `Item37` and `NPCHit4` pitched up; a stagger is `NPCHit4` pitched down with `DD2_MonkStaffGroundImpact`; a riposte is `Item71` with `DD2_MonkStaffGroundImpact`; Triunfo thrown back is `Dig` pitched down. A Golem fist landing is `DD2_MonkStaffGroundImpact` and `Item14`, both pitched down. Picked by what they are, not by ear.
- [ ] **The parry has never been run with two machines** — the packet, the server ending the arrow and turning the weapon, the spark on the other screens, and another player's sword (each machine runs it for itself from three numbers) are checked outside the game only (PADWorkFlow §16.26). So are the push, the stagger and whether it is held, which go with the enemy; the riposte, worked out on the machine that lands it; and the late word from the server that is not believed for 20 ticks (§16.30). On the machine that parried, the enemy (a flying weapon too, since §16.31) is turned only when the server says so, a few ticks after the clash is seen (it cannot touch that player meanwhile), and 'Dame' Quilt and a Golem fist the same: say if it should be shown at once. The fist's landing is struck by the server and shown by each machine off its own copy of the fist getting there (§16.33). §16.42: the Iron Flow's three numbers and a parried lance of 'Dame' Quilt's were in fact never sent: both asked with `netUpdate` from outside the projectile's own update, which the game forgets (the entry below on that). Sent now; still not run.
- [ ] **Under reversed gravity Visus's, the Flesh Maiden's and Cold Call's arm points the wrong way** — `VisusGrip.ArmRotation` and `ColdCallHold.ArmRotation` multiply the arm's rotation by `gravDir`, and vanilla's `Player.SetCompositeArmFront` turns it over again, so it is only right pointing straight up or down. Read off the IL, not seen. The Iron Flow passes it the right way up. The fix is to take `* player.gravDir` out of both; not done, not asked for (PADWorkFlow §16.26).
- [ ] **An idle holdout is ended and made again every tick its owner is frozen, stoned or webbed** — `BaseIdleHoldoutProjectile.AI` ends it then and `PADHoldoutPlayer` makes it again: the Greatbow's, the Iron Flow's, and now the Meat Hook's (whose hook is ended with it, at once, and its wait begun: `MeatHook_Hook.AI` asks `CCed` itself, PADWorkFlow §16.41). Nothing to see alone; two packets a tick with a server. One line in `CheckForEveryHoldout` (not while `CCed`) stops it.
- [ ] **A right click during a cut is lost** — vanilla only asks for an item's alternate use on a fresh press with nothing in use and the left button up, so the parry cannot be pressed ahead of the end of a cut. Say if it should be remembered for a few ticks.
- [ ] **`MeleeDecider.cs` is gone and its words are not** — the file is no longer in `Content/Items/LoreItem`; `Items.MeleeDecider` is still in the `.hjson`, a name and an empty tooltip for an item that does not exist. Three of the build's 13 warnings were that file's: the baseline is 10 now (PADWorkFlow §16.26).
- [ ] **The Run of Bladders has no Sealed Pocket** — the log of its making says so: "The Sealed Pocket found nowhere in the cavern under the Cleared Ground that was not already spoken for." Eighty places are tried, each a box two or three hundred tiles square that nothing in the structure map may touch. Seen while reading the log for the shrine, not looked into (PADWorkFlow §16.27).
- [ ] **A tile of ore over her ground, once in a long while** — vanilla's Surface Ore and Stone runs after her ground is made, and `OrePatch` can lay one tile of ore a row or two over ground that falls three rows in three columns. Vanilla's own, on her ground as anywhere; nothing stops it (PADWorkFlow §16.28).
- [ ] **Subworlds are single player only** — with others in the world the door stays shut and says so. Nothing of a subworld is sent to anybody (PADWorkFlow §16.35).
- [ ] **What comes through a door, and what does not** — since §16.45 a place has its world's flags, and what is done in one is done in the world (yours: "Subworld uses the same flags as main world"; that it goes both ways is my reading of "the same"). Both ways: every boss, invasion and moon beaten and the Old One's Army's tiers; who has been saved, bought or let in; the books used; an orb smashed; and the mod's own (Legends, the Megashark and the Abomanation beaten, the Shimmer, Grace's seal, the Guide's word on Legends, where Mors's, Pugream's and the Dame's stories have got to, the count of deaths, whose name you have and what you have asked). Into a place they go with you. Back they go by a file the place leaves beside its own whenever it is saved (`Worlds/PisAndDag_Subworlds/[the world's id]/Flags.dat`), which the world takes up whenever it is loaded: so Save and Exit from inside a place loses nothing. Mine, and yours to change: what comes back only ever adds to the world (a flag set, a count gone up, a name learnt), so that nothing that goes wrong with that file can cost a world what it has done; to put a flag out by hand, do it in the world. Hard mode and the world's evil go into a place and are not brought back (hard mode is the world changed, not a flag set; nothing of the game's begins it in a place). The offerings on the Mile and the Guide's first words being owed go in and are not brought back (only the world's own ground changes them). **Not carried**: where anything is; the world's secret seed (next entry); the clock, the weather and whatever is going on; the bestiary and the kill counts (banners); Journey's per-world powers; other mods' flags. The world's clock does not move while its player is in a place. Minions and shots are gone on the other side and what lay on the ground is lost, as on Save and Exit. Mors, Pugream and the Guide's first words stand still in a place (PADWorkFlow §16.45).
- [ ] **A place is never of its world's secret seed** — the game puts all eight out when it clears a world to make a place's file in (`WorldGen.clearWorld`), so in a For the Worthy world the hall is not, and whatever is fought there is as it would be without it. Not carried with the flags: each seed changes the game in a great many places (For the Worthy is named in 81 of the game's methods, the upside down one in 134), in a place nothing of the kind was laid out for, and none of it can be looked at from here. Say if a place should have its world's seed (PADWorkFlow §16.45).
- [ ] **Nobody leaves a grave in a place** — yours ("player dont drop graves in sub worlds"): the game is not let throw the stone, in any place, with its rules lifted or not; the coins fall and the death is told as anywhere. Mine: the death is one of the world's (the count is one of the flags), so the line after a world's first death comes there and Mors is at the graveyard after; it is also counted apart (`GraveyardZone.Elsewhere`), and the graveyard's one burial goes to the first death that leaves a stone, so a first death in the hall does not use it up. Say if either should be otherwise (PADWorkFlow §16.45).
- [ ] **The graveyard's burial with others in the world** — found in §16.45: a death's stone is thrown by the server as its own player (255), and the burial looked for the dead in the stone's owner, so by the assembly nobody would ever have been buried on a server. It reads who died from the stone now (its first ai slot, where the game puts it). Run in the rig as a server throws it; never tried with two machines.
- [ ] **A game left from inside a subworld** comes back in the world at its spawn, not at the door: the world a place was come into from, and the door, are kept in memory only.
- [ ] **Subworld files outlive their world** — deleting a world from the game's list leaves its folder in `Worlds/PisAndDag_Subworlds`. And the game's daily backup names its zip by the file's name, so the same place of two worlds shares one zip a day (`Worlds/Backups`).
- [ ] **The door can be used in a fight** — nothing stops a player leaving through it with a boss alive; the boss is gone when they come back.
- [ ] **Not stopped in a subworld** — paint and its scraping off, wire, and what turns one kind of tile into another: none goes through one place, and none breaks anything. A dig that is refused is refused silently: no sound, no dust.
- [ ] **A subworld's top: an NPC's weight, the sky, and water** — the game makes things lighter and the sky darker near a world's top, by rows counted from its surface line, which a place puts near its bottom. A player's weight is put back since §16.44 (`SubworldRulesPlayer.PreUpdateBuffs`: in the hall as it is now the top twelve rows were in that band, and a player under the ceiling weighed 0.59 of what they do on the floor). Not dealt with: an NPC's, which the game thins the same way (`NPC.UpdateNPC_UpdateGravity`) unless its `GravityIgnoresSpace` is set or it has no gravity at all: nothing spawns in a place and Foigh carries his own weight, but an NPC ever put in one with the game's gravity wants that field set (items and projectiles are not thinned); the sky's darkening, which nothing in the hall can see, there being a wall behind every cell of it; and water laid into a place would boil off, as the game has it do in the Underworld. Each wants dealing with before a place has what it touches.
- [ ] **`DustFog.png` is drawn flat where puffs of it overlap** — the mod's particles are drawn in two passes; in the first a picture has to be stored already multiplied by its own alpha (`FlameSmoke`, `Ember`, `SoftLine` and `Smear` are), and `DustFog.png` is stored plain white, so each puff covers what is under it more than it should and many together read as flat discs in full light. Known since the pass was written (PADWorkFlow, "Found on the way, not fixed"); seen again in the film of Foigh's third phase, where his floor dust is the only thing that reads so. Left: storing it multiplied would also change King Slime's, Queen Slime's and Cold Call's dust, which have been seen and passed as they are. Say if it should be done (PADWorkFlow §16.48).
- [ ] **The game's own menu sounds under the black** — its save and its entering a world make three (close, open, close), between the door's creak and its shutting. Read off the assembly, not heard: say if they should be silenced.
- [ ] **E turns the tip, and nothing else can** — the key is fixed (`SubworldLoading.NextTip`), not a keybind, and the corner says "E" whatever is in the hand: on a gamepad nothing turns the tip. E is also the game's grapple: still held when the black lifts, it throws a hook.
- [ ] **Aseprite sources are packed into the mod** — the two in `Content/Subworlds/LoadingArts` (456 KB) go into the `.tmod` as they are: nothing in `build.txt` leaves them out. `*.aseprite` in its `buildIgnore` would. Not done: say. Since §16.43 two more are in `Content/NPCs/Foigh` (`SwordsmanBoss.aseprite`, `SwordsmanBluntSide.aseprite`), and beside them `SwordsmanBossRef.png`, the picture of the whole of him next to a player's height, which nothing asks for and which is packed as a texture like any other.
- [ ] **A loading picture stays in memory once it has been loaded** — about 8 MB each at 1920 by 1080, until the mod is unloaded; and one is loaded ahead of its passage, the first as the first world is entered (single player). Nothing with seven in the folder; worth a thought with fifty.
- [ ] **A loading picture that will not load** — is struck off until the mod is loaded again, with a line in client.log, and another is drawn in its place at once. tModLoader logs it as well and, as for any asset of a mod's that is missing or unreadable once mods are loaded, puts up its own "Failed to load asset" box, which holds the game until it is answered. A picture is turned into raw pixels when the mod is built, so it would mean a damaged `.tmod`. Never seen.
- [ ] **Old halls are left beside the new one** — a hall made before its ground was deepened or the place made taller stays in its world's folder as `EmptyHall.wld` or `EmptyHall.2.wld` (each with its `.twld` and `.bak`), unread; so do `EmptyHall.3.wld`, the hall under open sky, and `EmptyHall.4.wld`, the hall 307 tiles long and 161 high; the hall is `EmptyHall.5.wld` now (PADWorkFlow §16.38, §16.43, §16.44). The mod deletes nothing. The same will be true of any place whose `Layout` is raised.
- [ ] **The loading screen's words are sized as the game's menus, not as the interface** — the window's height over 900, never under 1 (`Main.PreDrawMenu`), so the interface scale setting does not move them. In a window much narrower than 16 by 9 a long tip wraps to more lines and is moved up, where the frame is lighter. Not seen at any size but in a sketch.
- [ ] **The garbage collector stops the game while it saves a great world** — in the rig the longest wait for a frame while a Grand world was saved was 22 ms in some runs and up to 245 ms in others, the collector's own doing (the game and tModLoader write the world to memory, read it back and zip it). On the loading screen that is the bar and a turning tip held for a moment. Not measured in the game: the passage's line in client.log has it. If it is long there, the runtime can be asked to put its full collections off for a passage (`GCSettings.LatencyMode`), which took the long waits away in the rig (PADWorkFlow §16.39). **Offered, not done.**
- [ ] **tModLoader collects all garbage whenever a world is entered** — on the main thread, as the player is put down (`Interface.ResetData`): 60 to 100 ms in the rig with a Grand world loaded. It falls before the black lifts. Not the mod's to change. The mod's own readying of the loading screen is on the same call, in single player: the tips found, and a picture read and handed to the graphics card if none is held. In the rig that is 10 to 15 ms at the first world entered after the mod is loaded, without the picture (it has no card to give one to), and under a millisecond at later ones, where a picture is held already. What the picture adds is not measured.
- [ ] **With Frame Skip on, the door's coming back up may be seen in fewer frames** — its 15 ticks of black and 30 of fade are ticks, and with Frame Skip on the game runs the updates a long frame cost before it draws again. With it off or subtle a tick is a frame. The passage's line says how many frames it was drawn in (44 is all of them). Not seen.
- [ ] **A door tried while an autosave is backing the player up** — the autosave saves the player on a thread of its own (`WorldGen.saveToonWhilePlaying`): the map under a lock the passage waits for (`MapHelper.IOLock`), then tModLoader's backup zip and the player's file under none. For those few seconds every ten minutes a passage's own save of the player can run beside the autosave's. Read no further than this: if the writing of the player throws, `Player.SavePlayer` shows its error and gives up. The game's own Save and Exit has the same gap. Not seen; to be looked into before release (the "while the game is autosaving" step below is the test of it).
- [ ] **tModLoader's maker of backups is detoured, found by name** — `BackupIO.RunArchiving` and `BackupIO.Player.PlayerBackupDir`, neither of them public (the entry on the player's backup, above). The detour is put on by tModLoader's own `MonoModHooks.Add` and comes off with the mod. Before the mod is published: say in its description that a door's passage does not rewrite the day's backup of the player.
- [ ] **Two of the game's private locks are found by name** — `WorldFile.IOLock` and `MapHelper.IOLock`, so that a door used during an autosave waits for it. If a later tModLoader moves them, client.log says so at load and the wait is skipped.
- [ ] **The Meat Hook's sounds have not been heard** — the shot is `SoundID.Item36` (the Shotgun's) pitched down with `Item38` (the Tactical Shotgun's) lower under it, and `Item14` under a shot fired while the hook holds; the throw is `Item10`, the Harpoon's own, as asked; the bite is `Item153` (a whip's crack) pitched down; `Tink` low on a tile and high as the hook seats on the gun; `MaxMana` pitched down when the wait ends. Picked by what they are, not by ear.
- [ ] **The Meat Hook has never been run with two machines** — what the hook holds and which way the drag goes are sent with it, and every machine does the dragging from its own copy of the hook; the world sends a dragged enemy every 6 ticks; the wait is counted on every machine from the hook's end; the gun's aim is sent when it has turned 2 degrees or for 8 ticks. Reasoned, not run. On a slow line a dragged enemy will jump once as the world's word of it catches up. The gun broken open for a reload is seen by its wielder only: nobody else has the magazine's count (PADWorkFlow §16.41). §16.42: a second right click letting go was not sent either (the gun's update tells the hook, and the hook had asked with `netUpdate`): the enemy would have stayed held for everyone until the hook was home. Sent now; still not run.
- [ ] **PSItem's flash and `MeatHook.FlashReach`** — PSItem puts its flash 0.85 of an item's sprite out from the point the shot is given (`DefaultMuzzle`), and the Meat Hook moves that point so the sum lands on the muzzle of the gun as it is held. If `PSItem.MuzzleOverrides` is ever given an entry for the Meat Hook, `MeatHook.FlashReach` has to follow it.
- [ ] **The Meat Hook under reversed gravity** — not seen. The gun, the arms and the chain go by the Iron Flow's sums (the arms passed the right way up: the entry on Visus's arm, above). The bar and "x2" are placed as the magazine's count is, by the screen: the world is drawn turned over and they are not.
- [ ] **The hook's wait is not saved** — it is kept on the player for the sitting; leaving the world ends it.
- [ ] **The hook on the gun is made from the two sprites** — the hook as it lies under the barrels is the item's sprite less every pixel of `MeatHookHoldOut_WithoutHook.png`, worked out once when it is first drawn (184 pixels now). Repaint either and the other has to stay the same gun, pixel for pixel, or pixels of the gun will glow and go dark with the hook. If the two are ever not the same size, the item's whole sprite is drawn while the hook is home, and nothing glows or goes dark on the gun.
- [ ] **A parried hand's and a returned core's looks and sounds have not been seen or heard** — a hand turned: `NPCHit2` pitched up with `DD2_SkeletonHurt` pitched down under it, a steel ring, 18 bone dust thrown the way the hand is; the core on its way back: `Item7` pitched up every 6 ticks, the parry's steel drawn over its red, spinning the other way; into her: `Shatter`, `Item27` and `DD2_MonkStaffGroundImpact`, a ring, a red bloom, shards, 18 globs of her gel out of her far side, her crown jumping, the screen shaken. Picked by what they are, not by eye or ear (PADWorkFlow §16.42).
- [ ] **Skeletron's hands and the Queen Slime's core have never been run with two machines** — the fifth and the tenth are taken by the server and sent as a strike; a hand's turned state goes with the hand, the core's with the core. Run in pieces in the rig, never with two machines. What to expect: on the machine that parried, a hand goes on as it was until the server's word comes (as every enemy's push does), and in a smash the hands may touch the ground there, silently, before they are thrown back up; the core turns at once there and gets into her a little before she loses the tenth; another player's machine may still show a clap's bang if the word of the parry reaches it after its own hands have met; a core parried so near her that it is in her the same tick is shown going in only on the machine that parried it. The Golem's fist still has the weakness the hands had: a server refuses the word of a parry if the punch is over by the time it comes (§16.42, section 8).
- [ ] **A projectile's `netUpdate` set outside its own update is never sent** — `Projectile.Update` clears it before the AI and looks at it only at the end (1.4.4 assembly; an NPC's is not cleared so). From an item's `Shoot`, a player's update, a packet or another projectile's update, set `netUpdate2`: the game keeps that one until it has been sent, and sets it so itself for a fishing bobber it reels in. Five places did it wrong and are put right (the Queen Slime's core turned, the Iron Flow's move, Cold Call's staff held on, 'Dame' Quilt's lance knocked away, the Meat Hook let go); the other nineteen ask from inside the projectile's own update, or in `OnSpawn`, before it is first sent. Anything new has to mind it (PADWorkFlow §16.42).
- [ ] **The way back through a door held one frame for 3.7 seconds** — client.log of 10/6, the first with the door's lines in it: to the hall 8.55 s (the save 7.00 s, drawn 60 times a second, no frame over 28 ms; the picture and the tip up in 0 ms); back to the world 10.80 s, the load 5.59 s and drawn through it, and then as the Grand world came back up one frame of 3678 ms (the collector's stops in that stage 213 ms, so it is not that). Not looked into. Five seconds after it you saved, left the world and loaded it again: say if something was wrong on the screen (PADWorkFlow §16.42, section 1).

Nothing of him has been on a screen. Never run at all: the passage's three lines in the dialogue's box, the light up the blade (a strip of `SoftLine`), and the sharp side coming in over the blunt one as the stone goes along it. The stone along the steel has sparks and no sound of its own (PADWorkFlow §16.44). Never run at all: the camera coming in while he holds you. Its sounds, picked the same way: `Item71` pitched down as the sword goes up and again as it comes down, with the ground's `DD2_MonkStaffGroundImpact` and `Dig` under the blow; `Item71` at the cut up; `Dig` pitched down as he goes onto the knee; `DD2_MonkStaffSwing` as he leaves it; `Item1` pitched down as the hand closes and as the sword comes over his shoulder; `Dig` as he lands; `DD2_GhastlyGlaivePierce` with `NPCHit1`, both pitched down, as the point goes in; `Item71` pitched down as it is raised (PADWorkFlow §16.46). Never on a screen: whether the black of the smoke and of the cuts shows against the hall's wall (against a dark one only its own red shows it); the bloodied sword; the wings' picture in his hand; the blood, which is held against the floor in the rig (none under it) but not against a slope or a wall; the camera on him as the picture comes back; and the panel with his four lines. Never on a screen: how the crescent reads at the game's zoom and in the hall's light (against a dark wall soot is nothing: what shows is the red about it and its edge); him coming round between cuts; three of them in 2.7 seconds. Never on a screen: his guard at the cap, with two blows a second getting through; how often he is broken against what you fight him with; how often he runs and breaks out; him broken, for three seconds. - [ ] **Foigh has never been run with two machines** — written for it: the server moves him from move to move; every machine runs the move he is in from a few numbers sent with him; each machine works his sword, his knives and his hand against its own player; the machine of whoever shot or swung decides what he does about it and tells the server (one packet, `PADPacket.Foigh`). None of that has had a second machine. The hall is a subworld, and subworlds are single player, so it will not first be met there. The passage into his second phase (§16.44) is the same shape and as untried: the server decides when and moves him through it; the machine of the player he fights, and no other, moves its own player (telling the server where they now stand, as the road's walk up does), shows the scene, and sends the second answer and that the third line was read; anyone else sees him do it, cannot hurt him, and is free. A machine that hears of the knee more than a quarter of a second late finds the picture already back, and leaves its player where they stand. His second phase (§16.46) adds nothing of a new shape: the server says the hand has someone, as it does for the one who drank, and their own machine carries them, takes the blow and is shown the camera; the quicker clock of a move is a number the game already sends with him. As untried as the rest. The four moves of §16.47 add three things, none of them run with a second machine: a player's machine tells the server that his cut up threw them into the air (one more word in the same packet), and the server sends him up after them; each machine cuts its own player with the late cut's line, as with his sword; and the server works out where a player on another machine will be as his sword comes down from what the game last sent of them, which for one just thrown as a ragdoll is their own machine's doing a moment before: if that is late or rough he may be a little off beside them. The passage of §16.48 adds, none of it run with a second machine: three more words a player's machine sends the server (the first line answered, the two that go by themselves read), with the server's own waits if they never come; the wound, which each machine puts on its own player; flight, which each machine shuts for itself from seeing him with the cut made (true for two updates after he was last seen so); the sword left standing behind the player, drawn where each machine saw him stand for the cut; and where he is put back under the black, which the server says. §16.49 adds, not run with a second machine either: what he lets go of is the server's projectile, as his knives are; each machine flies its own copy of it by the same numbers, hurts its own player by it, and counts its own ticks of it; and one player's parry of it ends it for everybody, as with his knives (the machine that parried tells the server, which ends its own and says so to the rest). §16.50 adds, not run with a second machine either: the cap is worked out on the machine of whoever's hit it is, from what that machine has seen strikes take from him, and the server gives back to his life whatever was taken beyond it (two players' hits landing in the same tick are each held to the whole of what could be taken, and the second one's is given back a tick later: its number is shown and the life comes back); his posture is the server's and is sent with him; the roll for a stream is made on the shooter's machine, which tells the server what it has turned aside (a new word, no oftener than every 8 ticks); the run and the break-out are the server's. §16.51 adds, not run with a second machine either: his hook is the server's, as his knives are, and each machine tries it against its own player; that machine works out the bite, looks at where its player is, and tells the server (a new word, with whether they stood on the ground), and from then who is on the hook is sent with him (two bytes more: 50 of his own now); one who is pulled in is moved by their own machine, asked every tick by his; what a hit of his does to a grapple and to wings is done on the machine of whoever is hit; and what he reads of a player is seen by that player's machine and told to the server (a second new word, no oftener than every two and a half seconds), which answers it or not. §16.52 adds, not run with a second machine either: one byte more with him (51 of his own now), how long the sword lingers in the move he is in, or that it is a feint; the move's clock then stands still on every machine by itself; whether it lingers or is a feint, a cut let go, the wheel's cuts and the cuts out of the ground are the server's, made as his other cuts are; what he learns is the server's and is not sent. §16.53 adds, not run with a second machine either: a straight cut's own ticks shown and hanging go with it to every machine (written into the projectile's own message as it is made, before the game sends it: `OnSpawn`, `SendExtraAI`); each machine cuts its own player with it, and one player's parry is through with that player alone; what a player's minions take from him is counted on their own machine, which tells the server when it is too much (a new word, `Swarmed`, no oftener than every second), and the ball is the server's. §16.54 adds, not run with a second machine either: each machine counts its own player's minions against a cap of their own and cuts down its own player's; the ball's mark goes with each of its lines (a byte after its ticks); a minion cut down is killed by its own machine, which tells every other as the game does. §16.55 adds, not run with a second machine either: his end is the server's (its parts, the count of his coughs, how long it waits), and the player he fights is held, put down, walked and asked by their own machine, which tells the server where they have got to and what they answered (a new word, `Last`, with the part it is for); every machine draws that player's arms and the sword in their hands; the ball's cut carries whether it is white and how often it cuts down (two bytes more). The hall is a place for one player: its flames are put out and lit with no message.
- [ ] **Foigh stands on flat ground only** — asked for, for now. He carries his own weight (`noGravity`, with a fall of his own), and the game skips its slope handling for anything that does (1.4.4); nothing of his steps him up a block either. His flash steps look for level ground to stand on, and his walk, his leap and his run after the sword take the ground as level. Only ever run on level ground.
- [ ] **Foigh's sword takes no notice of tiles** — the blade is drawn over whatever it crosses, and a cut lands on a player it reaches whether or not anything solid is between them: nothing asks. In the hall there is nothing to stand behind. A player in his hand beside a wall is held nearer him, out of the wall (run in the rig with the player in each corner). To be looked at before he fights anywhere with cover. Since §16.47: the late cut's line lies through a wall as through the air and cuts whoever is on it, and the step along it goes to where there is ground to stand on through whatever is between. The step up into the air does look for room beside you (nothing solid where he would be), and the blow there for the ground under him.

What the game does with such an item afterwards was not looked into, and it has not been seen in play. Hers was not changed. - [ ] **The M.U.P.R.A.L to the pixel: what it cannot do** — the game makes its pictures of the blocks, what stands on them, the walls, the water and the backdrop again only once in four frames, each on a frame of its own, and the lamp's light to the pixel is laid over each as it is made: swinging the beam fast, its edge on the blocks moves in steps, 15 a second, and on the blocks and on the walls can be a frame or three apart (the Vast Ocean's water lit follows every frame). When the lamp starts moving or the beam starts turning all at once, the light given to the game's map can be a frame or two behind, and the edge it is going toward dim for that moment. Creatures, players and things are lit by the tile they are in, as the game lights everything, so one just outside the beam's edge can be lit. A tile the game draws at full brightness whatever its light (glowing paint) is darkened about the beam. Under the game's Retro and Trippy lighting it is as before, tile by tile (PADWorkFlow §16.89).

Nothing of it has run in a world.
- [ ] **Skeletron's rework has never been run with two machines (§17.11)** — the server decides each move, its aim and who is caught, and sends them with its clock; every machine plays the move from them. What to expect: a move's start can reach another machine a few ticks late (its first sounds a moment after the server's); a client guesses a move's aim until the server's arrives and never after; a caught player is held on every machine and thrown by their own, so another player may see them jump a little as they are let go.
- [ ] **Dancing Sand never run in the game (§17.19)**: its numbers are worked out, not measured: 574 a second within 5 tiles, 150 at 20, 100 at 30, to no defence, at 67 a hit. Within 5 tiles it is the strongest early weapon there is: say if the near end wants a floor (say every 12 ticks, not 7).
- [ ] **§17.13 never run in the game** — compiled against tModLoader 1.4.4.9 and read through by an independent review (its four findings fixed); the swimming seen only through a Python stand-in with the same rules and numbers (`Claude outputs\VastOcean_schools_day_1025.gif`, `VastOcean_schools_night_1025.gif`). The colours made at load have not been seen in the game's light, nor the cost measured there.
- [ ] **The Harenaeum set never run in the game (§17.20)** — compiled against tModLoader 1.4.4.9 and read through by an independent review (its 13 findings fixed); the motion seen only through Python stand-ins (`Claude outputs\*_1028.gif`), worked out from the code's numbers. Never tried with a second player: the throws (`PADPacket.Launch`), the tornado's carrying (the server's), the ballista's place and the tails' strikes are the parts to watch. The fists pose the player's arm by redrawing the game's own arm pieces (`FistArmLayers`): a body armour or glove whose arm pictures are laid out unlike the game's, or another mod drawing arms its own way, may not follow the pose.

## Last

- [ ] Recompile every `.fx` after any shader edit; a stale `.xnb` throws on `Parameters[...]` at draw time, not at load.
- [ ] Fresh world, fresh character, no commands: die once, walk to the graveyard, talk to him.
- [ ] Amalgaermis: `/abomanation`, then each of `rush`, `lunge`, `puke`, `ash`, `vent`, `spasm`, `whip` (the vent gives it nothing back until it is under 40%). Then a whole fight with no commands, and one with a second player.
- [ ] A new Grand world, on foot from the jungle to the Mile: no land floating over the Cleared Ground and no leaf wall standing on it; its lumps, ponds and cracks where vanilla put them, and the middle 280 tiles flat (no step over a block but into a few small holes, none deeper than 5; no water; no living tree), with hills, ponds and cracks again from there out, lower and shallower for the first 70 tiles; every tree on it a stump; the four signs on the ground before the ash. If a living tree landed on it: its stump, and the room under it with its chest. Then the Blood Shrine's chest: the Orb, 5 Life Crystals, 20 to 25 bones and six other things.
- [ ] 'Dame' Quilt's box. `/dona` for her ground and her place, the middle of it (in a world made before: for where the flattest place is); walk at it across the Cleared Ground (or `/dona summon` within eighty tiles of it): she is put down on it, off the screen. Boss Active is on the buff bar from then until she is thrown. Run at each wall: the camera stops, then you do. Inside it, try a pickaxe, a block and a bomb. Let the swing throw you at a wall. Take her to one life with your back to a wall and watch the camera come away as she falls. Take the fight off the flat to an end of the box, where the hills and the water are now. Then with a second player who starts outside the box and walks in.
- [ ] The log of a new Grand world's making (`client.log`): the `Cleared Ground:` line gives her ground's x, the flat's x and its level, the columns cut and filled, the holes left on the flat, the easing's columns with water on them and the water taken; the `Blood Shrine:` line gives the shrine's x and how far it is from the spawn. Go to that x: the shrine is on the ground.
- [ ] The Iron Flow. Make one (12 Iron or Lead Bars, an anvil). Held: it hangs by the leg, facing both ways, walking, jumping, and in the inventory's picture of you. Left click held: down, up, round, each a step forward (1, half and 1.5 blocks standing still; nothing added at a run). Right click at a Goblin Archer's or a Skeleton Archer's arrow: early, on time, late, and with your back to it. The same at an Enchanted Sword, a Cursed Hammer or a Crimson Axe: thrown back, spinning 2 seconds, not touching you on its way out. Then with a second player watching, and under a Gravitation Potion.
- [ ] The parry, on everything. A Zombie: every parry strikes it for the sword's damage and pushes it back six blocks; the third staggers it 2 seconds (glints going round its head), it touches nobody, and the first hit on it does three times its damage, throws it, is never a crit, and wakes it; then the count starts over. A Paladin or a Yeti: the same count. An Ice Golem: your blow lands and so does its hit. A Demon Eye or an Enchanted Sword: the second parry (the eye is knocked out of the air, and gets up). A Skeleton's bone, a Goblin Archer's arrow, a Pirate Deadeye's bullet: broken. A Demon's scythe or a Fire Imp's ball: not. `/parry` with the cursor on each says the same. 'Dame' Quilt (`/dona summon`): her charge and Triunfo running you down, parried three times between them for the stagger and the riposte; her thrown lance knocked away and fetched; her swing not parried; once her lance has hit you, a parry later in that same charge does nothing (PADWorkFlow §16.32). Amalgaermis's bones and skulls (`/abomanation`, `puke`). The Golem: parry a punch; the fist turns about and flies back, and when it reaches the Golem the fist loses 1428 and the Golem 714 (ordinary world), head on or off; it is never staggered; the seventh breaks it; `/parry` on a fist in flight says so, and on one at the Golem's side says not now After a fist has been sent back with the head still on, the Golem and its fists are no faster than before (§16.34). (PADWorkFlow §16.33). Then with a second player watching, and landing the riposte.
- [ ] Subworlds, single player. `/subworld door`, put the Unmarked Door down on flat ground, right click it: a creak, and from that frame, with no hitch, the world goes down in under half a second into one of the pictures in `Content/Subworlds/LoadingArts` (another one each time) under the frame's blemished black edges, with a tip low on the right and "E :Next" under it, all of it there by the time the screen is dark and no plain black at any point; then, the world left, E turns to another tip (about one in five is the game's own, under "Common Knowledge") and the bar, a seventh of the window wide low on the left and empty until then, fills at an even pace, never going back or standing for seconds (the first passage each way after starting the game goes by a reckoning, the next by what that one took); then the hall, with no music: Gray Brick all round (the floor, a wall at each end 171 tiles apart, a ceiling 48 rows up, which a screen does not reach from the floor), stone wall behind it with its pilasters and its one course half way up, torches up the wall and a Tiki Torch in every bay, no dark corner, the door against the left wall and you in front of it. In a world that had a hall before, this one is new (three rows became twenty-six, then forty in a place 320 high, then walls, a roof and lights: §16.43; then fourteen bays by two panels: §16.44). Get up under the ceiling by whatever you have (a hook does it) and let go: you fall as fast from there as from half way up, where the game by itself would have had you fall slower (§16.44). A Teleportation Potion puts you somewhere on the floor. There: a pick, a hammer, a bomb, a block, a bucket, the Sandgun, a dirt bomb and an actuator all leave it as it was; die there: your coins fall and no tombstone does (§16.45); nothing spawns; a watch stands at 8:15. The map, full screen too: brick and the wall behind it, with no Underworld behind them (every cell of the hall has one or the other now, so there is no sky on it to look for). `/subworld` says where its file is. Right click its door: back in front of the first one. Then again: with an item on the cursor and one in the Goblin's slot (both come through); in Journey with godmode on (still on); at 150% interface scale (nothing of the menu at the black's right or bottom); from a Grand world; with a chest open; while the game is autosaving. A pick and a bomb at the door in the world: nothing. `/subworld undoor` with the cursor on it takes it up. With a second player in the world the door says it does not open (PADWorkFlow §16.35). Afterwards, in client.log: one `Subworlds: through the door to ...` line for each passage, and no `could not grow the world arrays`; and the Grand world's map, full screen, whole after coming back (PADWorkFlow §16.39). And for §16.40: at the mod's load, `Hook Terraria.ModLoader.BackupIO::RunArchiving(...) added by PisAndDag`, and no `backup of a player is not where it was`; each passage's line ending `tModLoader's backup of the player was left out of the saving.`, with a few ms at most for the frame the door was tried on; `Backing up player file` followed within a second by `Saving world data`, and each passage about 4 s shorter than §16.39's table has it; and, after a passage and then an autosave or Save and Exit, the day's zip of the player in `Players\Backups` with the time of that later save. The flags, since §16.45: in a hard mode world with bosses beaten, the hall has them (Boss Checklist's log shows the same bosses beaten there as in the world: it asks the game's flags each time, though a mark made in it by hand is its own and is not carried; and `/pugream state` gives the same stage and the same "eye beaten" or "eye not beaten" there, with "sea none", the shore being the world's). Beat something in the hall that the world has not (any boss, by Cheat Sheet) and go back through the door: beaten in the world. Again with another, and this time Save and Exit from inside the hall, then open the world: beaten there too. In a world with no death yet and not yet in hard mode, die in the hall: "Something should have remembered that." comes there; back in the world Mors is at the graveyard when you come to it; and the next death in the world is the one buried in the open grave. With Mors's story at his monument (hard mode), stand in the hall a minute: nothing in client.log.
- [ ] The Meat Hook. Cheat Sheet one, and Musket Balls. Held: the gun in both hands, pointed at the cursor, facing both ways, walking, jumping, and in the inventory's picture of you; the hook under the barrels glowing red. Left click: a shell of 8, the gun kicking, the flash at the muzzle; the second shell, then "Reloading" and the gun broken open for a little over a second. Right click at a Zombie 20 tiles off: the harpoon's sound, the chain out, the bite, the Zombie dragged to you and hung there under a second, "x2" beside you and a shell into it doing double; then the hook home, the bar filling for 3 seconds and the hook dark on the gun until it is full. At a Demon Eye: dragged down out of the air. At a Paladin or a Target Dummy: you are dragged to it and stop short. At a boss: the same. At nothing: back from 50 tiles, 5 seconds. At a wall: back at once, 5 seconds. Through platforms. A second right click, and a jump while dragged: let go. The gun put away with the hook out: the hook gone, the wait still running when the gun comes back. A throw in the middle of a reload, and with no bullets. Right click on a chest, a door and somebody to talk to with the gun out: no hook. Then under a Gravitation Potion, and with a second player watching both drags (PADWorkFlow §16.41).
- [ ] Skeletron's hands and the Queen Slime's core. An Iron Flow. Skeletron, at night: parry a hand as it comes down at you, and as it sweeps in from his side: your blow, then a fifth of its full life (312 of 1560 in Expert), bone knocked off it, the hand thrown back about nine tiles and harmless for under a second. Walk into a hand hanging by him with a parry up: not parried, and it hurts. `/parry` with the cursor on a hand says which. The fifth parry of one hand breaks it. In a Legends world: his clap and his smash, each parried facing left and facing right: nothing lands, both hands are thrown back, one loses a fifth, and there is no bang where they would have met (let one land, to hear it). The Queen Slime in a Legends world, before her wings: her core parried on its way out, and on its way back: the parry's steel over its red, straight back into her, a tenth of her full life gone as it goes in (3456 of 34560 in Expert), then she catches it. With her a little over half: her wings come after it. With under a tenth left: her death begins. Then with a second player: each watching the other parry a hand and the core; the other's Iron Flow seen cutting and parrying; a lance of 'Dame' Quilt's parried and seen knocked away on both screens; the Meat Hook let go by a second right click and the enemy dropped at once on both (PADWorkFlow §16.42).
- [ ] Foigh, in the hall. Through an Unmarked Door; walk a little way in, face down the hall, `/foigh`: "Somebody is standing a way off, with his back to you." Then touch nothing: the picture fades and you are 28 tiles behind him, walking to 20; the camera goes to him; he looks back over his shoulder and turns round; three lines, "..." under each; he takes the sword out of the ground behind him, up over his head and down behind him; the fourth line, and "Ready"; his name under him, then the bar; black, and back to you. No music until then, the fight's from there. The fight: let him walk to you (the sword dragged, sparks off its point). Each of the four sword moves, taken once and got out of the way of once: down, up, the thrust and the leap back; the leap onto you (missed, the sword is in the ground two seconds and he pulls at it; landed, he goes straight on); the blow down (twice the damage), the turn and the knife; the jump, the thrown sword with its yellow star (it throws you down for a second), his run to it and twice round. From far off: one knife, three knives, the step behind you with half a second before the cut up, and the step back. While he walks: bullets, arrows and thrown knives (2 in 5 struck out of the air, half of those coming back at you); a spell, a sword's light, a rocket (3 in 5 he is not there: in front of you with the sword, or over it and down on you); a minion (always lands); a sword, a spear and a whip swung at him (3 in 5 met, and you thrown back). The same in the middle of a move of his: everything lands. A healing potion drunk near him: standing (the hand, up, the sword through you, flung), at a run, with a jump as he comes (those two get out of it), and in the middle of his first move. The Iron Flow: parry three cuts (reel, reel, a knee for two seconds) and land the riposte; parry a knife and a shot he sent back; try the thrown sword and the hand (neither can be). Die once to each of the sword, a knife, the thrown sword, the potion's blow and a shot sent back, for its line. Take him to nothing (a knee, a fade, nothing dropped), and once let him kill you with nobody else there (he stands, and goes). `/foigh gone` at any point. In a Legends world his life is 225,000 and the sword 150 (Classic). The leap, since §16.44: from beside him as high as it was (under five tiles); from thirty tiles off, three times that, and longer in the air (it lands less often than it did: see the entry on that). Then his second phase: `/foigh second`, or take him to two thirds. He stops whatever he is at and bows, the bars come in and the picture goes black; it comes back with you 70 tiles from him, facing him; the camera goes over to him, on one knee before the sword, which stands upright in the ground with only its handle and the top quarter of its blade over it, both his hands on the handle and his head bowed over it (§16.45); blood four times, a second, 0.4 s and 0.3 s apart, each bending him; he looks up; "Your skills are... exceedingly well honed. I can feel it!" with "..." under it; "You have earned my sharp side of the blade...!" with "..."; his head goes down to it, his hands tighten and he heaves, coming up off his knee with the sword rising a little; then he tears it out forward, the floor's tiles heaving up one after another along its way and the screen shaking as the point breaks out, and it is up at the sky; down before him, a stone from his belt, the sword drawn back through it with sparks and the blade pale behind the stone; a light up the blade, twice; the sword over and down behind him; "Let this foolish old man's flame rage once more", which goes by itself; black, and back on you, and he walks from 70 tiles off. Nothing lands on him or on you from the first of it to the last. Bring it on from the middle of each of his moves, from the top of a leap, with you in his hand (a potion), and with his sword thrown and standing in the ground. Once near each end of the hall: with no room for 70 tiles on your side you are put on his other side. After it: the pale sword in his hand, thrown, and in the ground, and 150 a blow (Classic). It happens once; at half his life nothing more. Then the fight of his second phase (§16.46). From far off, stand and count what he does: mostly he keeps walking at you; the leap, from as far as 40 tiles; now and then the step behind you or a knife. Shoot him while he walks (two bullets in three struck out of the air, two spells in three stepped out of the way of) and swing at him (two in three met). Up close: after each of his moves a second or two of walking before the next, and a swing of yours that he meets in it does not bring the next one on. His first move, his third and his fourth, each quicker than before the passage, and the thrust reaching further. Then the hand: the sword up over his head, the run at you, the blow down with the floor heaved, the cut up, down on a knee wearing the yellow star, and the leap with the hand out. Once stand still through all of it (thrown seven tiles by the blow you are still inside the leap): by the throat, down, the camera in, pulled against him, the sword over his shoulder and slowly through you (one blow), raised with you on it, and flung off. Once run from the knee on (he comes down empty and is a moment on his knee); once jump; once get behind him while he is down (he does not turn). Parry the blow down (he reels; the hand cannot be parried). Die on the slow sword once, for its line. All of it once with your back to each end wall. Then the four of §16.47, each waited for (he picks them himself; up close the mill and the hand come most often). The late cut: he sinks low with the sword at his hip, a pale line from him through you, and is gone along it, standing past you with his back to you; the line opens a third of a second later. Once stand on it (cut, and thrown toward him); once jump as it opens; once run on the way he went; parry it as it opens, facing either way; once with your back to an end wall. The mill: the sword over and levelled, three turns coming on, the blow down with the floor heaved; stand ahead of it, get behind it, parry a turn. The cut up out of the ground: the run with the sword dragged in sparks and the yellow star, the blade under the ground and out ahead of him with the floor's tiles heaving; stand in it once: thrown about 12 tiles up, he is beside you up there at once and strikes you down, though you could not have been hurt (two blows of the sword, 300 in Classic); then get out of it: run from the moment he sets off, or jump over him at a run. In the air: get off the ground near him and wait (in the hall a jump, or a hook in an end wall, its ceiling being out of a hook's reach; outside it, `/foigh` on flat ground with a rope or a platform put up beforehand, or under something a hook will hold in): the step up beside you, the sword going up while he hangs, the blow, and you sent down; from a jump he is beside where you land and the blow ends in the floor; hanging from a hook in a ceiling he comes in beside you a little lower; once with a Featherfall Potion, falling past him. Try to parry the cut up and the blow in the air (neither can be). Die once to the late cut and once sent down, for their lines. Then his third phase (§16.48): `/foigh third`, with wings on in a world where the Empress is down (and once with none on; once in a world where she is not). The fade, and the picture back on him bent over the sword, shaking; two coughs; his line and your "..."; the sword up at the sky, taken by the blade, through him; out, and the blood off the swing; your line over the bloodied sword; "It is fairer so."; him behind you, the pull, the cut, the sword left standing and your wings in his hand; "Futile devices"; the fade back on a burning sword. Then try to fly, to glide down with jump held, a flying mount, and the booster with rocket boots: none. Take a blow (Ultra Bleeding I), a second inside five seconds (II), a third (III), and wait it out; die of it once for its line. Watch a swing, a blow that lands and the thrown sword for the black and red. Kill him or `/foigh gone`: flight is back at once and the wings are drawn again. Once stand with a wall right behind you as he steps behind; once let every line wait. Then his third phase's own move (§16.49): after `/foigh third`, stand a little way off him (about 20 tiles) and wait: about one move in six he puts the sword up over his head, the blade flares, and he cuts three times where he stands, down, up, and down in both hands; each lets go a crescent of black and red that comes at you. Stand through all three once (half the sword each, and Ultra Bleeding I, II, III). Jump each. Parry each (it is gone; he does not reel). Stand in his reach for the blade's own three cuts, and parry the blade (he reels, and nothing more is let go of). Get behind him after the first: he comes round before the second. Then hang from the ceiling by a hook, near enough over him (within about 20 tiles to the side): about one move in four it is these, and each goes up at you there. Send one into a wall. `/foigh gone` with one in the air: it thins away. Then what keeps him standing (§16.50), with the Zenith and the Last Prism. In his first phase: the first hits land for a great deal (two seconds' worth at once: 3,840 in Expert), then he has his sword up and nearly all of it is sparks, with a hit getting through about twice a second; his life goes down by no more than 1,920 a second (Expert) whatever you use; keep at it and about once in half a minute he is down on a knee for three seconds. `/foigh second`: he turns a whole second of it aside two times in three, in the middle of his own moves too; hold it on him and after two and a half seconds he runs at you behind his sword (nothing lands on him while he does) and cuts; hit him hard enough and he is gone, behind you a moment later, with a cut up that a parry does not turn. Parry his sword four times running: he breaks (down for three seconds, the first blow on him three times its weight, and more can be taken from him then than at any other time). `/foigh third`: the same at four in five, and your hits are a little smaller (defence 80). No bar shows his posture: by request. Then what reaches you (§16.51). In the passage into his second phase, as his third line is up: his bottom hand goes back and comes forward with a hook on a chain, and nothing is said of it. `/foigh second` and stand a way off (ten to forty tiles): sooner or later the hand goes back and the hook comes; it bites for 50 and he is pulled along its chain to you with the sword coming down as he arrives (parry that, or be elsewhere). Hang from a grapple, or fly: bitten there, your own hook is let go and you are pulled in to him, with none of the keys yours, and the blow waits (it can be parried). Parry the hook itself: it is knocked away and he takes the chain in, open. Let anything of his hit you while you hang from a grapple: it lets go, and the key does nothing for a second. Let anything of his hit you in the air: wings, rocket boots, the Soaring Insignia and a carpet do nothing for two seconds and a flying mount is left; what the wings had left comes back if you are still in the air after. Stand far off: two cuts where he stands, each sending a pale cut at you (half his sword's blow, no wound; each can be parried, and there is time between them to parry both); and knives, now and then five at once: stood on the ground the lowest is at you and the rest go over your head; in the air the middle one is at you. Get well above him, up to forty-six tiles: the leap comes that high. Swing a sword at air within fifteen tiles of him, hold a beam or a yoyo on him from a way off, reload, or throw a grapple: two times in three he is in front of you with the sword (or, with you off the ground, the hook comes). In his first phase none of this happens. Then his third phase, wilder (§16.52): `/foigh third`. Everything of the sword's on his feet is half as fast again, and he waits half a second to a second between moves. Stand a way off as he cuts: a crescent leaves the cut and comes at you. Stand off, or hang from the ceiling: sooner or later he goes down as for his fourth move and up off the ground, turning, and three great cuts come at you one after another; then he falls. Get out from under the leap: he comes up over the stuck sword and drives it in, and cuts go out of the ground all round as far as ten tiles (stand further, jump it, or parry the one that comes at you). Watch the blade as he begins his first move, his third, or the hand's: if it flares, the sword will stay up longer than it should (it flares again, with his eye, a third of a second before it comes down) or he will be gone as it should have come down, and behind you. Parry him a dozen times running and both come far oftener; keep in the air and the wheel, his hook and the blow in the air come oftener; keep off and he walks less. Since §16.53: let one of his slashes come at you and press the parry late, as it is about to touch you: it is turned aside. In his third phase get out from under the leap: straight cuts open across the ground about the blade one after another, each shown a moment before (step out of the one through you, or parry it as it opens). Bring twenty minions or a long Stardust Dragon: within a few seconds he curls up in the air, nothing landing on him, and bursts into straight cuts about him, the first through you as he bursts. And going through a door, press E on the loading screen: the next tip is there at once. Since §16.54: call up minions or a Stardust Dragon and set them on him: two in three of their hits are turned aside with a spark (four in five in his third phase), and they take no more than about 600 a second off him in a Classic world; let him curl up and burst with them about him: what his cuts go through is cut down four times in nine, a dragon whole. And take a wound in his third phase: blood spews down out of you, more with each wound. Since §16.55: set minions on him in each phase: in his first two the ball is white (the air drawn in, a white flash, white cuts) and in his third red and black, across 18, 23 and 27 tiles; before it bursts a roar comes in to him from the edges of the screen. Then his end: `/foigh end` (or take his life). He is gone to the fourth pilaster from the far end; the picture fades and you are twenty tiles in front of him, and the camera goes over to him (§16.66); he roars, and the hall goes dark from him outward but for the torches over him; he goes down on his knees and his hands, the sword falling before him; you walk up; he looks up, laughs and coughs blood; answer the question one way, and another time the other; then Finish (the sword taken up, the thrust, "Thank you", the ash, and Foigh's Rust Side Sword in your inventory, the hall lit again) and, another time, Spare (the throw, the walk away, ten seconds, his line, and out of the hall to the loading screen). Leave a line unanswered for a minute and a half: he goes on, and the plea's choice becomes Spare. Look hardest at what has never been on a screen: the lines in the dialogue's box, the trail behind the blade, him narrowing as he turns, the star's outline, the light up the blade, the camera coming in while he holds you, the late cut's line and the light along it as it opens, him behind the tiles while the blade is under the ground, the whole of the passage into his third phase and the black and red of his sword, the crescents of his three cuts, his guard against a stream and him broken, the hook in his hand and its chain, a cut of wind, the wheel, the straight cuts, the ball, minions turned aside and cut down, the blood out of a wound, the white ball and the roar drawn in, all of his end, the blade's flare, and every sound (PADWorkFlow §16.43 to §16.55).
- [ ] Foigh's Rust Side Sword. Finish him (or Cheat Sheet one). Held: on the shoulder, facing both ways, walking, jumping. Left click held at a Zombie: up, down, round, the smash, the thrust, and again; standing still each carries you a tile, half a tile, none, a tile and a half, two tiles; the smash shakes the ground. At a Skeleton: 600 a cut, give or take, its defense no matter. Right click held: up, a roll and a slash at the cursor, twice more (the third slash great), then down; nothing more until the ground. A slash through a wall into something behind it. Enter held 3 seconds standing (the chat must not open): the sword up before the face, the red up the blade, then the roar and Blood Drenched Sword on the bar; the sword red and burning, its slashes red; your most life three fifths, defense up, running and jumping faster. Left click held near enemies: up, down, the smash, the leap at the nearest, and held, into the ground and twelve red cuts out of it; from a leap that ends in the air, down onto the point first. Poisoned and on fire before the focus: both gone after it, Potion Sickness kept. Two minutes on, Dried Blood for two more; Enter then opens the chat. Under the blood, any other weapon used faster. Since §16.58: a leap at a Demon Eye, in the air: down onto the point, and the sword in as you land, its straight cuts out to fifteen tiles. Under the blood every enemy you hit bleeds: 200 over it once a second, blood spewing out of it, five seconds after the last blow. Under a boss over you (the Eye of Cthulhu low over you), left held: you stay where you are, every cut hits it, the thrust goes straight up; drenched, the leap goes straight up into it and back down, and the sword goes in under it. With the cursor ahead and an enemy close behind you, the chain turns to it. Since §16.59: Shift held over it shows its three verses and "Lore Item"; it will not stay in the trash or a chest, no shop takes it, the Goblin Tinkerer will not take it, and a second (finish him again) is gone as it comes. The Zenith's tooltip says 150 damage; held at the Target Dummy with the cursor on it, about a fifth less a second than before. Since §16.61: hover it, with the tooltip's box on and off: its name's letters dark, from a brown red at their top through a dark purple to a crimson at their bottom, outlined in red that is a deep red at the top, a pinkish red half way and crimson at the bottom, breathing with the lines under it; the other lines as they were. Shift held: the same name over its verses. As it comes into your hands (Finish, or Cheat Sheet), the name that rises from it is pinkish red (#d13d62).
- [ ] The way down to the Ball. A new Grand world: `client.log` has a line `The Way Down: made: the mouth at ...` with where its mouth, its hall and its door are. Go to the mouth (between the beach and the Dungeon, at least 50 tiles in from the sand) and walk down with nothing but your feet: a narrow ragged cave, a torch on its floor now and then, dirt wall behind it near the surface and under that mostly no wall, with patches of rock wall; no fall that hurts while you walk off them. Partway down it comes out level into a hall of Gray Brick, its top corners stepped in, its back wall in registers with a torch on each of its four pilasters, and the Unmarked Door in the middle of its floor: a pick, a hammer and a bomb do nothing to it or to the brick it stands on, and right clicked it takes you to the Empty Hall and back to it. On down: near the Ball the wall behind the cave is its evil's and so are the torches, and the way ends at the top of the Ball's shaft (the drop into it is the Ball's own). Nothing on the way is cut through: no chest, pot, tree, house, water or Dungeon brick. Then in The Run of Bladders: `/struct update` says "The Way Down: placed." and the log has its line; the same walk; `/struct update` again: "The Way Down: present."
- [ ] The minion count. In a world, small, to the left of your hearts and level with them, two lines: "Minion" over "0.0/1". Summon minions: it counts them as they come and go, two Imps "2.0" before the slash and a minion that takes half a slot a half; the room after the slash goes up with what you wear. Change the life display in the settings (Classic, Fancy, the bars, with and without their text): it keeps beside the hearts or the bar, and beside the bars clear of their text and their mana bar. Change the interface's scale: it keeps beside them. The full map, and Hide UI: gone. Then the Rust Side Sword's name (§16.62): hover it and watch the colours move, the letters' down and the outline's up, a turn every two seconds, never jumping.
- [ ] Hoard of Undead. Kill zombies at night until one drops it (one in 50; Cheat Sheet one to start). Summon one: it comes up at the cursor, one of the eight zombies of the wiki's page, see-through, a little greyed, its outline in the four colours, see-through too, going round it counter clockwise. With nothing about, it walks to you and stands behind you; summon more: each a little further back; turn round: they go round to the other side. Walk away: they shamble after you and fall behind; go 75 tiles off and they are at your feet, from a fight too (leave them at a dummy and run). With an enemy within 60 tiles (not past it), they walk to it as zombies walk to you, step up a block, jump walls up to six high (not seven), drop through platforms to what is under them, and hurt it 30 or so a touch, each again only every 35 ticks (yours); mark one with a right click: they go for it first. A Torch Zombie lights its way. Cancel the buff: they go. One slot each: summon past your room and one of them goes. In Expert, they jump at what is above them. In water they move faster than on land (yours), and never into a block.
- [ ] The way round about, in Errand Boy. `/struct update`: the game stands still a while, then "The Way Down: placed."; `client.log` has a line `The Way Down: no way down keeping 40 off the Dungeon (...); made round about it, 10 off it at the least: the mouth at ...` with where its mouth, its hall and its door are. Go to the mouth and walk down with nothing but your feet and your jumps: down to the hall and its door, then on, seaward over the Dungeon's halls and never into them, down past their seaward end under the beach, back along under them and into the top of the Ball's shaft. No fall that hurts while you walk off them (a jump off a ledge may). Nothing of the Dungeon's broken or opened into; no chest, pot, tree or water cut through. `/struct update` again: "The Way Down: present."
- [ ] The Triple Fart In A Horse Shoe Adorned Bottle. Cheat Sheet one (its recipe is commented out) and wear it: a jump and three more in the air, each with the Fart in a Jar's sound, its green cloud and a rush across; on the ground again, all three back. With a Cloud in a Bottle as well: the three, then the cloud. Drop from high up: no damage, and none with wings on as well. Its name, its tooltip and its picture in the inventory.
- [ ] The way into the ball's hollow, in Flacid. `/struct update`: the game stands still a while, then "The Way Down: placed."; `client.log` has a line `The Way Down: no way down keeping 40 off the Dungeon (...); nor round about it into the top of the ball's shaft (... it is shut in ...); made round about it into the ball's hollow from the side, 10 off it at the least: the mouth at ...` with where its mouth, its hall and its door are, and where it comes into the hollow. Go to the mouth and walk down with nothing but your feet and your jumps: down to the hall and its door, then on down, seaward of the Dungeon's halls and never into them, into the ball's shell over its seaward top, down through it, and out onto the bank in the hollow. No fall that hurts while you walk off them (a jump off a ledge may). Nothing of the Dungeon's broken or opened into, its walls in the hollow too; no chest, pot, tree or water cut through; the hollow's side not opened over the bank. `/struct update` again: "The Way Down: present."
- [ ] The level dash in the air. Wear Tabi (or the Shield of Cthulhu, or the Master Ninja Gear) and the Triple Fart bottle: jump, use the three farts, dash: none comes back while you dash; land: all three back. A Cloud in a Bottle, Rocket Boots, and wings after the Empress the same: nothing back till you land. The dash in the air still goes level, a little further than it did. Along the ground, up and down a hill's slope and stairs, over platforms: as it went. With a fart left, press jump in a dash in the air: it is spent and does not lift you (PADWorkFlow §16.68; decide).
- [ ] Bone Squad. Cheat Sheet one (its recipe is commented out), with room for six minions (with five or fewer it cannot be used). Use it: six come up at the cursor, the game's own Bone Lee, two different Armored Bones, a Ragged Caster, a Necromancer or a Diabolist, a Skeleton Archer and a Giant Cursed Skull, each a little greyed and see-through, an outline in the four greens going round it; the minion count up by 5.5. With nothing about: the walkers come and stand behind you, each a little further back; the caster comes to you in a puff now and then; the skull floats behind and over you. Use it again: the six go and six more come, picked anew, and nothing else of yours goes; with other minions out, as many of them go as for any staff. At enemies within 60 tiles (not a target dummy): Bone Lee runs at them and leaps, the Armored Bones walk at them at their own speeds, the archer stops and shoots flaming arrows over them, the caster appears near them and casts (lost souls that bend to them, a shadow beam off the walls, or an inferno that bursts into fire), the skull flies at them weaving and spits shadowflame; the arrow, the flames and the spells' dust greyed and outlined too. Mark one with a right click: they go for it first. Run 75 tiles off: they are at your feet (the skull at your middle). Cancel the buff: they go. The staff in the inventory, on the ground and in hand: the Skull, the green outline going round it. With a second player: each sees the same squad.
- [ ] Bone Squad's jumps and bringing back. The squad out, nothing about: stand on a ledge 10 blocks high, facing out over its edge so that their places behind you are up there with you: Bone Lee and the Armored Bones jump up to you, the archer does not (7 at most). On a ledge 11 high: none of them gets up; 5 seconds after, Bone Lee and the Armored Bones are at your feet, the archer stays below. Walk behind a wall 15 high, or shut a door on them: 5 seconds after, the three are at your feet. Run off fast: the slow Armored Bones fall behind and are at your feet every 5 or 6 seconds, Bone Lee keeps up. With an enemy about, none is brought back while it has it to fight. Stop beside a wall: none jumps at it.
- [ ] §16.75. The Blind Tusker: in a desert by day, in a sandstorm before the Eye, under the desert (or from Cheat Sheet): it finds you, charges, runs past and turns; up walls to 15 and not 16; over a wide gap; short of a wide deep pit; standing, running, in the air and skidding, facing each way; the bestiary page; the Iron Flow off it. The Dryad: the wound one chat in four, "The Bezoar" with one on you, her thanks and the clover, and neither again for that player. The Trumpet's recipe at an anvil with each kind of bar. Amalgaermis's health in each mode, and in Legends.
- [ ] §16.76. Betsy's Flames (Cheat Sheet one): its tooltip says Type: Transmutation and Element: Draconic, the word in `#b0a158` outlined in `#db184c`; picked as the incantation up, its name on a seal's "Up:" line in the same colours.
- [ ] §16.78. The Worminator: kill Giant Worms underground until one drops it (one in 5), or Cheat Sheet one. Summon at the cursor over ground: the Giant Worm's sound, dirt thrown up, the worm leaping out and diving back in; the buff "Worminator". With nothing about: its dirt and its quiet digging under you, now and then the worm breaking out; walk and run: it keeps up. At a Zombie: it comes up through it and loops back, 8 a segment; summon again: one segment longer each time, one more slot each (the minion count). With every slot full, summon an Imp: the worm one segment shorter, not gone; with one slot: the worm gone. A Hunter Potion: the worm seen in the ground. Cancel the buff: it goes.
- [ ] §16.78. The Vulture Staff (Cheat Sheet one): summon four: each flies from the cursor to your shoulders, two towers of two behind you, facing your way; turn round: they hop across; jump and land: they flap; stand a while: a stretch; run: the top two lean back. Walk at two Zombies: they take off one after another, circle over the one with the least life, dive in turn with a screech and a streak, and pull up; a kill: they dive more often for 3 seconds, the streaks red; nothing left: they fly home and sit. Mark another enemy: they go for it.
- [ ] §16.79. The waygate and the Vast Ocean. A new world, and one made before (loaded once): `client.log` has `Waygate: put down on the left (or right) beach, its top left tile at ...`, and `/subworld gate` says where it is from you. At the shrine: the light, its rays, rings and motes; nearer, brighter; the cursor on it, brighter still. Right click: the band, "Traverse the mysterious gateway?", Yes and No, the one under the cursor lit; walk off: it goes; No: it goes. Yes: the black and a picture, then the Vast Ocean, standing in its waygate on the platform, the teleport sound low. The haze on the horizon (does the game's painted sea sit right against the water?), the shafts, the snow; dive: deeper blue, the screen wavering, a shark's shadow far off now and then; at the floor, 447 down: sand, shell, coral, seaweed, piles. Break the deck: nothing; dig the floor: it digs. An Empty Bucket: "The sea will not be carried." Fish from the deck: the sea's catch, and the Angler's fish counts; a Truffle Worm: Duke Fishron; go 70 rows down with him up: he rages. Sharks, squid, jellyfish, seahorses, turtles and dolphins about (no gulls since §16.83). A night there: night at home too. Back through its waygate to the beach's.
- [ ] §16.79. Pugream's fire. Once he stands on the shore: a Campfire up the sand from him, six blocks clear between, nothing lying between them; break it: it does not come back. Neither he nor it on the shrine.
- [ ] §16.80. The shrine on Huh's beach. Build & Reload, then load Huh: `client.log` has `Waygate: put down on the right beach, its top left tile at 12024, 539.` (that place for the beach as Huh was saved), and the shrine stands in the middle of the beach, 89 tiles in from the water, the cactus that stood there gone whole and none left hanging. Then a new Grand world: its shrine too, and no "no beach" line (PADWorkFlow §16.80).
- [ ] §16.81. The Vast Ocean, new. Build & Reload, go through Huh's waygate: a new place is made (a few seconds; `VastOcean.2.wld` beside the old one). The sea stays full: watch the surface by the deck, then dive to the floor (2688 rows since §16.85) and back: no dark hollow over the floor, the surface where it was. From the middle of the deck: the sea's fish; a Truffle Worm brings up Duke Fishron; fought near the surface he does not rage, gone down past the surface line he does. Swim a few hundred columns either way from the platform: the sea's backdrop behind the water all the way, the sea's music, the beach biome (the bestiary, the Angler's quest fish) (PADWorkFlow §16.81).
