- [ ] §16.82. The depths. Build & Reload, through the waygate (a Gills Potion helps; the depth meter reads two feet a row, Underground from the surface line). By day at the deck: the water bright under the surface and darkening as you go down, sun rays slanting into it (one way in the morning, the other in the evening), and now and then a swell running along the surface. Dive: the blue darkens section by section, with no black band anywhere near the top; the rays fade out by the end of the 3rd (about 756' Underground is the 3rd's top, 1204' its end, since §16.85); a torch held: as far as on land in the 1st and 2nd, half as far from the 3rd, only its own cell from the 5th (1652'), pitch dark; dark navy at the end of the 4th, black by the end of the 6th. The 7th (2548'): the Forevernight Zone (the bestiary or a biome display), the screen murky with silt drifting, sounds low and muffled, the music quieter and swelling, nothing spawning, a few faint specks that go out now and then where something passes; a block put down stays. The 8th (2996'): silence, no music; put down a block, a platform, a torch and a table: each shakes and breaks in 1.3 seconds and comes back as its item. The 10th (3892'): the Abyssal Trench. Back up: sound and music as before. At night: moon rays, a third as long (PADWorkFlow §16.82).
- [ ] §16.83. The trip, the light, the roof. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.3.wld` beside the old files in a few seconds, with no long "Settling liquids" (`client.log`); back, and in again, the same. On the deck: twice the sky over the water; fly up: open sky for some 134 rows, then the sky going black, the top space. Under the surface by day: thin rays slanting away from the sun (to the right in the morning, straight down at noon, to the left toward evening), each lit for a moment and gone and another lit, brighter in patches that drift, a soft glow about each, sparkles in them, glints along the surface; none under the platform; at night the moon's, fewer and pale. No gulls on the water (PADWorkFlow §16.83).
- [ ] §16.84. Slime mounts, dolphins, sharks. Build & Reload. Anywhere, on the Slimy Saddle in water a few rows deep: up faster than before, and out with a little hop; from 30 rows down or more: faster and faster to a row a tick, and out of the surface into the air, some 110 rows up (some 160 with jump held); the same on the Gelatinous Pillion; in honey or lava, as before. In the Vast Ocean: dolphins three to five together at the surface, none under the platform; sharks only from about 308' to 1204' Underground (the 2nd and 3rd sections; since §16.85), one to three together, none near the surface or deeper down (PADWorkFlow §16.84).
- [ ] §16.85. The Vast Ocean heard; its bubbles, storms and night; half as deep again; the M.U.P.R.A.L. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.4.wld`. On the deck: the surf from both sides, near and far, two or three pieces at once running into each other, never the same one twice running; a breeze from upwind going by; louder and rougher with wind and rain; fly up: the surf fades, the wind grows. Dive: the surf muffled at once, gone by some 120 rows down; from about 6 rows down, whales now and then, mostly far, now and then one answering another from the other side; in the first three minutes or so, a long low roar from far below (not while Duke Fishron is about). Bubbles: a breath every three or four seconds, more swimming fast, from fish too; half a section down, columns rising from under the screen; each bursts at the surface or under a block. At night near the surface, a glowing wake behind you and the fish; the moon's light gathered in a path on the water under it; by day the rays thick under the sun and sparse to the sides. In a storm: the swells higher, oftener and with the wind, rain rings, whitecaps and spray, the water flashing with the lightning. The depth meter: the 5th section from about 1652' Underground, the 7th 2548', the 8th 2996', the floor about 5236'. The M.U.P.R.A.L: buy it from the Mechanic (20 gold) and wear it: a lamp on your shoulder; its tooltip says which light it gives and how to switch. In the 5th section (a torch lights only its own tile there): the glow about 3.7 tiles, a wall nearby lit on its face and dark behind; press L (or right click it in its slot): the lamp turns to the cursor, a beam of 20 degrees to about 13 tiles, a block in its way casting a shadow down it, the glow about you 1.2 tiles; at the surface, in air, the glow 6.1 tiles and the beam 23; in the inventory, not worn, a right click puts it on. In multiplayer, in a world (the waygate is single player only): another player's lamp turns as they aim, and their switching is seen (PADWorkFlow §16.85).
- [ ] §16.86. The Vast Ocean's look and the lantern in the depths. Build & Reload (first delete `Claude outputs\VastOceanAmbience_1685.png` if it is still there), through Huh's waygate. By day at the deck and just under: the water a clear turquoise at the surface going cerulean, a net of light moving just under the surface, under the sun a fan of gold opening down into the water with gold shafts fanning out from over it and motes glittering in it; to the sides fewer shafts and the water bluer. Morning and evening: the fan and the shafts slanting with the sun, a deeper gold. Watch the waterline for a minute through a swell or two: no thin line floating over the water, no doubled foam. Dive: sapphire by the end of the 1st section, a royal blue by the 3rd, navy by the 4th, black by the 6th; what is under the water clearer than before. At night: silver. With the M.U.P.R.A.L worn, in the 5th section and in the 12th: the glow and the beam light the water and what they reach, warm by the lamp and blue at the end, shadows behind blocks; in the 7th and under, the murk eases as it comes on (in a third of a second) and back as it goes off (most of a second); no lamp, as dark as ever. Leave for Huh: its water as before. No chat error "minValue must be less than maxValue" however long you stay (PADWorkFlow §16.86).
- [ ] §16.87. The Vast Ocean's floor and its life. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.5.wld` (the old files can go: Known rough edges). Take Gills and the M.U.P.R.A.L. Dive from the deck: a range of mountains not far west of under the deck (some 1400' West, give or take 700), its summit some 3600' to 4200' Underground, jagged, crags along its ridges; corals and sponges on its ledges; brush past the bubblegum coral, the bamboo coral and the sea pens: they light blue-green, a wave running along them, motes drifting off. Into its caves: a tunnel low through it from side to side, a cavern in its heart, a tunnel higher up into it, a shaft down from near the summit, grottos in its sides; swim each through, none blocked. On the plains (from some 4560' Underground the sand gives way to red clay or brown mud): sea pens, xenophyophores, glass and cup sponges, anemones, sea lilies, specks of plankton drifting by them and now and then one blinking blue; pebbles and boulders; now and then a cluster of spires of ice or rock curling outward. Some 5200' East: the trench, down to some 6100' Underground, ledges on its walls, caverns off them, silt in its bowl. Elsewhere along the floor (the map helps): on a range's side a mound of black smokers, black smoke rising from their dull red mouths, giant tube worms about them, the ground stained rust and white; the tallest summit's crater with a few more; a terrace of green rock with tall white chimneys shimmering at their tops; cold seeps (white and orange mats, white mounds of hydrate, bushes of seep worms, bubbles in fits from cracks); cones of grey mud; fields of pits; dark lumps on the red clay; heaps of pillow lava, tongues of lava, a pale dome spiny with obsidian and its flow; three whale skeletons, a much longer skeleton in coils, two fossil reptiles half out of the sediment; gardens of glowing moss, geodes, standing stones, sinkholes; stalactites in the caves. The map names each thing of the life (Giant Tube Worms, Black Smoker, Sea Pen...). Break one: nothing drops. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.87).
- [ ] §16.88. The lighthouse island, and the sea's surface. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.6.wld` (the old files can go: Known rough edges). West from the deck some 2350' (the compass): the island floating, spikes of rock under it, two chains going down from it into the dark. From the east: the sloop at anchor, the skiff at the pier, the fisherman's house on stilts with its blue roof and its chimney smoking, palms on the beach, kelp and coral on the shelf under the water, the stair up the rock to the plateau. The lighthouse on its white plinth: the colonnade, its statues and brass lanterns; up either stair to the deck and in by a door; up the stair in the shaft through every storey (the hall and the store, the kitchen and the dining room, the two bedrooms, the library and the study, the watch room), every room lit warm, none a house (the housing query in each says it is not); the chests hold something; up the rung to the gallery, the lens turning in the lantern. At dusk: two beams sweeping over the sea, the lens flaring every six seconds; out half an hour after dawn. East of the plinth the oil house and its loft. West: the crags, the arch, the cave behind it, a chest on its ledge; from the shelf at the stack's foot squeeze through the passage in the arch's west leg into the arch; no sky through the stack. On the sea: with the sun low, its fan of gold lying along the surface, none of it over the water; a set of waves about once a minute in calm weather, oftener in rough, dying down after; at night in rain the glints and whitecaps riding the waves, no grey streak over a dark gap. In the Abyssal Trench the water a little navy, not black. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.88).
- [ ] §16.89. The M.U.P.R.A.L's fine rays. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate, Gills and the M.U.P.R.A.L on. In the 5th section or deeper, the beam on (L): a cone of 30 degrees, its edges clean, to about 11 tiles; swing it over the floor: the floor's top lit only inside the cone, a thin band where the light grazes it, no step a tile along its edges; a block's corner casting a clean shadow down the beam; a coral or sea pen half in the beam lit only on the half that is in it; the water lit in the cone and nowhere else, the motes glittering only in it. Swing it fast: the light on the blocks may step a little (say if it bothers). The glow (L): a smooth circle about you, a wall or block in the way casting its shadow. At the surface, in air: the beam to about 15 tiles. In a world at night, in a house: the beam on the walls and furniture the same, clean-edged. The tooltip: "a 30 degree beam to the cursor, 15 tiles in air, 11 in water". Settings, Lighting Retro, then Trippy: the old light, tile by tile, no errors; back to Color. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.89).
- [ ] §16.90. The Depth Meter in the Vast Ocean. Build & Reload, a Depth Meter (or a Cell Phone) on, through Huh's waygate. On the deck: "4' Surface"; in the water at the top: a few feet, "Vast Ocean"; dive: the feet climbing, 448 a section; past about 2690 (the murk): "Forevernight Zone"; past about 4030: "Abyssal Trench"; at the trench's foot about 6236, "Abyssal Trench", not the Underworld. High in the sky: "Space" as before. Back in the world: the meter as it always was. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.90).
- [ ] §16.91. The water's weight, the Gills Potion, Water! Resuscitate Me!, the trench, the dark, the beam, the shafts. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate. By day at the deck and just under: every shaft leaning the same way, away from the sun (down to the right in the morning, to the left in the evening), rooted along the surface, most under the sun; none through the island's rock or past the platform's, the pier's posts and deck letting them through; the fan of light cut off along the line of the light where rock stops it; by night the moon's the same. A Gills Potion: its tooltip's new line; drink and dive: the buff's time draining faster, its tooltip saying how fast (8 times from 2240'). The M.U.P.R.A.L on, the beam (L): from about 1900' down the water black, only what the beam lights seen, no rock standing black against the water; move the cursor away: the beam reaching past it, bright to it, up to 100 tiles; a second player sees it reach as far. Dive on: from about 2160', "The water grows heavy" over you, the corners darkening, a slow heart, the Depth Meter amber; past 2240', The Water's Weight among your debuffs, "Losing 10 life a second", more the deeper, the dark going red and the heart faster; die of it: one of the three lines. Water! Resuscitate Me! (fish crates, or Cheat Sheet), through a seal: bubbles drawn in, a splash, Resuscitated for 5 minutes; dive: nothing taken to 3584', then as before; cast again at once: nothing, in its last 30 seconds: back to 5 minutes. From 3136' down (the silence): long low songs from far under you, knocks and clacks, more toward 4032'. At 4928': crushed at once, a crunch and a shake. Swim to the sea's far east and west ends where the turtles are: no error.
- [ ] §16.92. The caves, the Crevice Eel, the water's weight on creatures, nobody moving in. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.7.wld` (the old files can go: Known rough edges). On the deck and the island: nobody of the town about, and none arriving over a whole day and night; no old man anywhere. Dive with the M.U.P.R.A.L (and a way to live past the 8th: a Journey character's godmode, which the water's weight leaves alone): lead a shark down past 2240': it darkens toward red, numbers rise off it and it dies; a blue jellyfish in the 6th untouched until the 7th. In a range (one some 1400' west of the deck), find a mouth on its side and go in: walls behind every cave (grey-blue cobbles, darker patches, rock walls where the stone is); dripstone of the rock hanging and standing, pairs all but meeting in the halls, the game's own small dripstone thick on the ceilings; halls, domes, long galleries, pockets, dead ends, a great cavern down at the range's root, a moss garden or two lit in the dark. Crevices off the hollows (straight burrows two tiles wide): an eel's head at a mouth, its eyes faintly lit, its mouth working; come within 7 tiles in front of it: it strikes, snaps, and goes back in tail first. Deeper in the caves, under rock: an eel comes out of a crevice out of sight and through the caves to you, its body waving after it, never through rock; it lunges when near; its bite leaves you bleeding (godmode off for that, and be quick: the water's weight there is 100 a second and more); hit it anywhere along it; killed, it bleeds along its length. Go up out of the caves for a few seconds: it goes home, into its crevice, and is gone. Its bestiary entry shows the whole eel. Its sounds through the silence.
- [ ] §16.93. The implosion, the H.E.A.T.R, the beam, the Hole Dweller, the lighthouse's rock. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate (no new layout: `VastOcean.7.wld` as it is). With nothing on and no Journey godmode (under it the water's weight does nothing): dive past 2240': life taken as before, more the deeper; from about 3424': "Any deeper and the sea closes in" over you, the dark closing in red, the heart at its fastest, the Depth Meter flashing red and white; at 3584': imploded at once, the sea rushing in, a flash, bubbles, the crunch, a shake, one of the three lines. With Water! Resuscitate Me!: nothing taken to 3584', then the same. A shark led down implodes at 3584' too. The H.E.A.T.R (Cheat Sheet): its tooltip's top speed with and without, in mph (a Stopwatch agrees: on foot 15 to 13, Hermes Boots 31 to 26); jump: lower; on a mount: slower too. Worn: breathing in water, the bubbles not going down; dive to the floor and the trench: nothing taken, nothing implodes. The lamp on the shoulder: a glow all about, some 30 tiles through water, and a 45 degree beam to the cursor at once; L does nothing. The field: a faint crimson line about you two pixels out, fading pink inward, hexagons rising in it; walk, jump, swim, turn, a tall hat, a mask with a nose, wings and a held sword (left out), a mount (left out); Shimmer or an Invisibility Potion: it fades with you; hide its slot's picture: no field, no lamp drawn. In a world For the Worthy: breath full in water and in air. The M.U.P.R.A.L alone: the beam's reach the same with the cursor near you or far, about 50 tiles in water. The Hole Dweller: the new look, bigger; hit it anywhere along it, the head at its jaws; its head turning about its neck in the bends; the bestiary showing it swimming. Under the lighthouse by day and by night, with the lamp and without: the rock under the island black where nothing lights it, no blue blocks, nothing flickering; lit only where a light falls (PADWorkFlow §16.93).
- [ ] §16.94. The H.E.A.T.R and the M.U.P.R.A.L, light to run. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate, the H.E.A.T.R on, the frame rate shown as in the picture. In a sea cave like the picture's: 60 frames a second (or what you have with it off); the light as before: the beam's edges clean, the glow round you now to about 20 tiles in water (it was 30) and 33 in air (49.5), the rock's rims lit where the rays reach, nothing lit through rock. Move, swing the beam, rise through the water's surface, run along the floor: no flicker, no lines or specks in the light, the motes in it as before. The field over you as before, its hexagons rising; stand still and walk. The M.U.P.R.A.L's beam and glow as before. Two players with lamps near each other: both lights added where they cross. The tooltip: "Its lamp glows 20 tiles around you in water, 33 in air".
- [ ] §16.95. The sea's light, the Depth Meter, the floor. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate, to the fisherman's house by day as in the picture. Under the water: the shadows in the light about the rays straight lines along the light, no steps; the light ending on the blocks' tops and falling past their corners onto their sides; walk and swim along: nothing in it jumping, the shadows going by with the world; stand still: the thin rays staying where they are in the water (each sliding a little with the waves, coming and going). Near the island's shore and the platform: the light about the rays not pumping brighter and dimmer as you move. The Depth Meter on, going down: 0' Vast Ocean in white; from 448' Schooler Zone (#26e9f0); from 896' Hummer's Sea (#4d5abd); from 1792' Light Party (#b853b1); from 2688' Forevernight Zone in red; from 4032' Abyssal Trench, its colour slowly going round three dark purples; the whole line in the colour; where the water takes your life, the line flashing red and white. Below 3136': mine the floor, a mountain, a cave's wall block: nothing breaks; a coral or a pile on it, a wall: breaks; put a block down there: it shakes and crumbles as before, and can be mined before it does.
- [ ] §16.96. The Crevice Eel, its dens, the caves. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.8.wld`, a new Vast Ocean (what was built in the old one is not in it; the old files can go: Known rough edges). Dive into a range with the M.U.P.R.A.L and a way to live past the 8th section (the H.E.A.T.R, or a Journey character's godmode): its caves going on down under the sea floor about it; ways branching, looping and winding, knots where they meet, dead ends, some forking. In a big room: here and there single blocks of obsidian or rock floating, none near the ways in and out; in some, a den: two to five crevices close together in a wall. Come within 22 tiles of a crevice an eel is in, in its sight: its head slides out and turns after you, its mouth working faster; within 11 tiles it draws back; within 7 it draws in a moment, then strikes; bitten: held half a second and dragged back toward its hole, let go short of it, bleeding. Stay down there: a second to five later it comes out after you, slowly, stopping now and then, creeping when it sees you near, drawing back, then lunging; bitten, shaken; it backs off tail first and comes again. In a den: the others come out too, one going in at a time, the rest circling you some 13 tiles off. Hit an eel on its head, then on its body: one health bar, over its head only; its name Crevice Eel; its life 9500 to 11,900 (twice that in Expert). Go up out of the caves: they go home into their crevices and are gone. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.96).
- [ ] §16.97. The gulls. Build & Reload, through Huh's waygate (no new layout: `VastOcean.8.wld` as it is), by day, not raining. On the deck, looking about: now and then a flock of five to thirteen gulls comes in from off one side of the screen and crosses the sky over the sea behind the platform and you, most going the way the wind blows, some smaller and slower (far off), each beating its wings and gliding by turns; now and then a gull's cry. Go to the lighthouse (some 1180 tiles to the left of the platform) and up to its gallery: a flock wheels round the tower, passing in front of it on one side and going behind it on the other, none jumping in front of or behind anything as it changes side; sometimes a second at another height; after a few minutes it breaks off and goes away over the sea, and later another flies in from off the side of the screen. When the rain comes, or near dusk, the lighthouse's flock goes and no more come; at night none. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.97).
- [ ] §16.98. The five weapons. Build & Reload. **The Mantrap** (Cheat Sheet one, or a goblin army): shoot at a Zombie: the jaws fly open, turning, shut on it with a clack, sparks and a flash, and it stands held a second (its arms still hurt), then the jaws drop off and fade; at a bigger enemy they bite and drop at once. Three shots, then the reload. Shoot at the floor: the jaws land and lie open, a click and a glint after a moment; a Zombie walking in is shut on for more; a fourth on the floor shuts the oldest on nothing; walk into one yourself: bitten (the death line if it kills: "stepped where they had set it"), held in place a moment; standing on one as it sets, it waits for you to step off. **Mycelium, Finish Them** (Cheat Sheet one; a seal with it up): cast at the ground: nine glowing caps burst up from the middle out, threads of light under the ground pulsing outward, spores; enemies in it hurt every half second; one that dies in it: a cap comes up where it fell and puffs spores at the next enemy; its pieces on the ground sink into it; a second cast withers the first; cast at the sky with no ground near: nothing, mana back. Its tooltip: Conjuration, Fungal in dark blue edged in light blue. **The Defacer** (Cheat Sheet one): hold: lifted up and back in both hands, slowly, grit off it, you walk slower; at the top a glint and a tick of stone and it glows faintly; let go: down, the toothed face first, a grey smear behind it; on the ground a crack, chips and dust, and lifted all the way the ground heaves; a Zombie hit lifted all the way: chips, its name under the cursor scratched with slashes, and it takes more from every hit for 8 seconds. Facing both ways, walking, jumping. **The Parade Sabre** (Cheat Sheet one): swings with a gold arc; hits on enemies flake gold leaf off it (its tooltip counts down from 120); at 0, at the end of the swing, a shower of leaf and a bell: the Plain Sabre, a steel arc, its prefix kept. **The Signal Pistol** (Cheat Sheet one): a pop and a red flash; the flare arcs, sparks and pink smoke, lighting red; in an enemy it burns it; on a wall it sticks; in water it sinks slowly, bubbling, and fish come to it; your sight dims while it burns. **Where they are**: in Huh, `/struct update`: "The Drawers: placed, 6." (or fewer) and "The Fairy Ring: placed."; a dresser by a cabin's chest underground with a Defacer in its drawers; a mushroom chest in the mushroom caves with the incantation, small glowing mushrooms round it. Through Huh's waygate: the first trip makes `VastOcean.9.wld`, a new Vast Ocean (what was built in the old one is not in it; the old files can go); the lighthouse's watch room, east sea chest: the Signal Pistol. A new Grand world: an armoury chest at the start of an armoury's row of stands, the first holding a Parade Sabre. Nothing in `client.log` (PADWorkFlow §16.98).
- [ ] §16.100. The glows. Build & Reload. The Signal Pistol at dusk or underground: round the flare a soft red light with no edge anywhere, an orange glow and a white-hot point in it, two faint glints turning; in water wider and softer. Mycelium, Finish Them: a soft blue light along the ground and about every cap, the spores soft balls of light; no disc anywhere. (PADWorkFlow §16.100.)
- [ ] §17.2. The Empress of Light. Build & Reload. In the Hallow at night, kill a Prismatic Lacewing (Cheat Sheet one). You cannot move; bars come in; the music and the world's sounds go; the light flickers (sky pitch and back, the moon dark and thrown bright), then her darker sky stays, she not darker; across the top of it soft ribbons of her colours, wavering and drifting, no end of one on the screen and no hard edge. She comes over you as vanilla's does, flashes down onto you, a hexagon of light shuts round you both; you are dragged to halfway between the ground and the top of the sky; ten stars spin out of her and round her for a second into a ring, then shoot out, the camera going with the one going right; at the corner the border drawn out of the stars, what lies outside coming in her colours, no seam anywhere in it as you look about later. The cage breaks; you fall; you are caught (Favor of an Empress on the bar); she rises 500 px over you and screams, "Elin" in pink and "Empress Of Light" in pale blue coming up under her with it; as they go down to the bar the bars go, the camera walks back to you and the music is back (a second and a half from the scream). Fly: no end to it, at the solar wings' speed with any wings or none. Fly at a wall: put back. A Magic Mirror inside the arena, or a Rod of Discord past the wall: outside, Visual Intoxication, 350 a second, and if it kills, "saw what lies outside". Die in the fight: the favour gone; come back and fly up to her: given again near the arena. Win: she slows over you, you hang; the camera goes to her; beams out of her eyes and her first line; cracks with light and her second line; the border closes on her and you for three seconds, shaking; it is thrown out and gone; she shrinks into a purple butterfly (catch it); her loot where she was; no gore. As the border goes the favour goes from the bar: you float down slowly (down held, fast), no fall damage. A second player: both see it all; one within 3600 px is caged and dragged too.
- [ ] §17.3. Build & Reload. **The Wall** in a new Grand world (the Ball in it): kill it for the first time; you can move all the while; the V comes as it always has, and a few seconds later the Ball's arms are there round it (`client.log`: "The Ball spread at ..., ... tiles turned, ... framed."). With a second player: both keep moving, and both see the arms after. A world in hardmode from before the Ball could spread: kill a Wall again; the same, no stop. **Her sky**: in her fight look at the top of the sky: soft ribbons, no end of one on the screen, no hard edge; fly side to side: they slide a little the other way; wait a few minutes: they drift on, never jumping; the dark under them with no bands in it. **Her plate**: she rises and screams; her name comes up under her with the scream, sits half a second, and as it starts down to the bar the bars go, the interface comes back, the camera walks to you and the music comes back; the name lands as the camera arrives. **Her favour**: win; as the border bursts and goes, the favour goes from the bar; you float down slowly, down held to drop fast; land from high up: no harm. **The Defacer**: bring it down on a Zombie three tiles off (where it used to miss): hit; let one come right up against you: hit, by the haft; on flat ground the blow lands about four tiles in front of you, and a full lift heaves the ground there; by a wall and under a low ceiling (it stops where its face meets the tiles); in the inventory a little smaller (a 68 by 68 picture fitted to the slot) (PADWorkFlow §17.3).
- [ ] §17.4. Elin in Legends. Build & Reload. A **Legends** world, at night, in the Hallow: kill a Prismatic Lacewing and let her come (her arrival as in §17.2). Fight her first half (above half her life) for a few minutes: every other move is one of her own, the move between vanilla's; no act comes again until three others have been played. **The grab**: let her dash hit you: she stops dead on you, a ring of light; you are in her hand, held out (you cannot move or use anything); the camera leans in on the two of you, the bars in; her other hand draws up and back away from you and a ball of light fills it, flashing; it comes over the top and through into you: a blast, rings, glass, a roar, about 200 off you in a normal world; you are thrown clear, away from her. A dash that misses goes by as vanilla's. **The mirror crack**: she hangs above you and raises a hand; a glint where you stand; the world cracks out round you to 22 tiles like a struck mirror, the cracks glistening the rainbow, stars winking where they cross; for two seconds the shards shift and tip and tremble, the world in them out of true (a grass line broken at every crack); the pink ring at the edge brightens: get out of it. It shatters: the shards fly out turning, the world in them, dark where they were; inside, you are hurt; they fly back and the world is whole. (Options > Lighting Retro: the cracks only, the world in them unmoved.) **The laser pillars**: she raises the hand on your side, it fills with light, she drags it down; from beside her toward you a row of thin coloured lines, then beams down the whole arena, one every 0.2 seconds, 6 tiles apart, red, orange, yellow, green, blue, indigo, violet and round again; a beam hurts only while whole. **The prism**: a glass prism in her hand, thrown turning to the arena's middle where it stands; she flies to the far side of it from you; two seconds of a white ray flickering on into it; then seven rays out of its far side toward you, fanned, sweeping as she goes slowly round; the prism bursts. **The light chainsaw**: she holds out both hands, a white blade grows from them with a rainbow edge and teeth of light running round it; she comes at you slowly behind it while her bolts, lances, sun dance and rainbow go on, never dashing or darting; after two of her moves (or eleven seconds) the teeth fly off and it goes out. **The mirage**: a flash, three of her round you; two cast bolts at you, one never does and glints at the crown: that one is her; all three come through where you were; if she meets you, the grab. **The eclipse**: she rises over you and the world goes dark; she goes dark in front of a ring of light with streamers; a notch opens in it, two faint lines across the arena from it: stand in line with the notch; the ring is thrown out over the whole arena; it passes you by inside the notch, hurts anywhere else; the light comes back. **The aurora curtains**: two dashed green lines where you are; curtains of light sweep in from both sides, green feet wavering, blue to violet above, rays flickering, a white line at each inner edge; for four seconds the gap drifts and narrows; touching a curtain anywhere, top to bottom, hurts; they draw back and go. **After each act**: everything she brought is gone (dark, copies, curtains, cracks, saw), and vanilla's next move comes. **At half her life**: her change, and from there her moves and dashes are vanilla's. **By day** (or `/time` to day mid-fight): every act and the blast kill. **Kill her in the middle of an act**: her death as in §17.2, nothing of the act left (the sky light again, nobody held). **A second player**: both see every act; the crack opens round each of you near her; the one held is held in the same hand on both screens; the prism's rays, the saw and the curtains go on hurting as long as they show; the other is not caught by the blast unless in it. **Town folk** under her arena: none hurt by her rays, rings or curtains; a player outside her arena is not hurt by them either. **The Inner Tube**: make a new world and open its surface chests, or spawn a few (Cheat Sheet): each is pink, cyan, neon green, maroon or solar orange, about evenly; the name says the colour ("Cyan Inner Tube"; a pink one is plain "Inner Tube"); in the inventory, dropped on the ground, and worn in water (round the waist) all the same colour; save and quit, back in: the same; reforge it: the same; a second player sees your tube's colour; dye it: the dye takes, as on the pink. (PADWorkFlow §17.4.)
- [ ] §17.5. Build & Reload; a Legends world, her fight at night. **Her name once**: from her arrival into the fight, the black bars go as her name goes down to the bar, and do not come back; no second plate. **Her voice**: she screams as each of her acts begins (a ring of pink off her), and at the height of each; at half her life, her loudest. **The sounds**: each act heard high and low together, nothing cutting another off (PADWorkFlow §17.5 item 2 lists them); the mirage's real one heard by a tink at her crown. **The prism**: the seven rays fanned wide (96 degrees). **The saw**: it grows from her hands to 50 tiles over two seconds as she comes, faster than before; the tip never whips round faster than you could step out of; a chainsaw's motor all the while in two notes, a rev now and then, her scream as it reaches its length, winding down as it goes. **The eclipse**: the notch shown for a second and two thirds, ticking toward the burst. **The pillars**: from the screen's edge on her side to the arena's edge. **The crack**: 32 tiles; she hangs above it to one side, not in it. (PADWorkFlow §17.5.)
- [ ] §17.6. Build & Reload; a Legends world, her fight at night, the prism: once the seven rays are out she goes round the middle slowly, then faster and faster (three times as fast by the end), the rays sweeping with her and their hum quickening and climbing; the firing lasts about seven and a half seconds before the prism bursts. (PADWorkFlow §17.6.)
- [ ] §17.7. Build & Reload. Her arena’s size, from her middle to a corner (the stars at its corners): a normal world 125 tiles, an expert one 115, a master one 100; in a Legends world 15 fewer (110, 100, 85). The stars fly out to the smaller decagon and the camera with one; the walls hold at it; what lies outside comes in at it. (PADWorkFlow §17.7.)
- [ ] §17.8. Build & Reload. **Her numbers**: in a normal world your hits on her do about 6 less than before (her defense 50 to 63), about 11 less in Legends (72); more in expert and master. A Legends world: her life on the bar 1.65 times what it was (115,500 in a normal world, was 70,000); the grab’s blast about 240 in a normal world (was 200); her lances, bolts, sun dance and rainbows a fifth harder than in a world without Legends; by day everything of hers still kills. **Her words** (Legends only, her first half only): 1.5 seconds after her name goes down to the bar, two lines at the foot of the screen, over the bar’s name: cream words, a pink rule under them going blue and to nothing at its ends, drawn out from the middle as they come up; each line stays as long as it takes to read and fades by itself, the second after the first. About one act in three begins with a line of that act’s (exactly one of each three she plays); when she catches you, a line; when you die, a line; fly well over her for three seconds, once: “Thou fliest by my leave.”; at four fifths of her life, once: “A mark on me. It will be corrected.”; at half, as she changes, two lines; after that she says nothing. No line twice running for the same thing. Through the grab’s bars the line stays; F11 hides it. Kill her mid-line: it goes as her death begins. A world without Legends: she says nothing. A second player near her sees the same lines. **Dame Quilt**: her four shouts, when they came before, now on that line at the foot of the screen with a gold rule, not over her head. (PADWorkFlow §17.8.)
- [ ] §17.9. Build & Reload; a Legends world, her fight at night, the game's Waves Quality not Off and Lighting not Retro or Trippy. **The pillars**: each warning line shimmers, and a light falls down it from the top of the screen, a streak with stars on it, reaching the foot as the beam comes; the world beside it kinked sideways as it passes (the clouds, the stars, you); each beam fringed with its neighbouring colours, slips of light running down it, the air either side rippling, a wave of bending thrown out either side as it fires; her raised hand with seven motes spiralling in and the world about her palm drawn in, turning. **Every other act**: the crack (a wave as it cracks and a bigger one as it bursts, flecks of glass glinting inside it), the prism (the world swollen in its glass, two strands round the white ray, glass triangles when it breaks), the saw (faint images of the blade behind it, a glint running out along it, the air shimmering), the mirage (a wave as she splits, the copies fringed red and blue as they peel off), the eclipse (the world drawn in toward her as she gathers, let go before the notch opens: the way out is where it looks), the curtains (the air shimmering at their inner edges), the grab (the world drawn into the ball, the blast a shockwave). Nothing bent over the interface. Under a Blood Moon (or its monolith on), the bent places red like the rest, no clear columns; with a Gravitation Potion, the bends the right way up. **Her sounds**: glassy new sounds over the game's own in every act (tingles, a crinkle as the crack strains, a swell before things fire, a falling star down each pillar line, zaps, whooshes, bells, a hum, shards, shockwaves); none drowning hers, none clipping. Set Waves Quality to Off: no bending anywhere, everything else the same. **The walls**: run, fly and dash (Shield of Cthulhu, Tabi, any other) into each wall of her arena: you stop at it and slide along it, never through and never sent back; land on the bottom edge and the two slopes either side of it: you stand on them, walk up and down them, jump from them, and take no fall damage landing; a Magic Mirror, Cell Phone, Recall Potion or conch can't be used inside ("It finds no way out of her light." over you, nothing used up); a Rod of Discord aimed past a wall puts you at the wall. Her grab still carries you, her cage still pins you. **Her death**: twenty butterflies burst out of her every way (Purple Emperors, Sulphurs, Ulysses, Tree Nymphs, in her colours round the ring), each faintly lit, fluttering out for about three seconds and then flying about as butterflies do; a bug net catches them, each as the kind it is; the camera walks home slowly. A second player sees them fly the same. **Her weapons**: in a Legends world the tooltips of Nightglow, Starlight, Kaleidoscope, Eventide and Terraprisma show 1.15 times the damage they show in another world, and they hit that much harder (Terraprisma's blades too). (PADWorkFlow §17.9.)
- [ ] §17.10. Build & Reload; a Legends world, her fight at night. **Her light chainsaw** (first half): at its full reach 37.5 tiles; the saw shivering; its teeth running round it fast, each streaking; six prism teeth looping out along one side and back along the other, trailing rainbow ribbons; sparks streaming toward its tip. **Her pictures**: `Content/NPCs/Legends/Empress` holds every picture of hers; her shots look as the game's (her homing bolts, lasting rainbows, sun dance, death aurora and the lances' warning line soft, not hard-edged or washed white; her wings' glow the same); paint a mark on Body.png and rebuild: the mark on her in the fight, on her panel's face when she speaks, on the map and in the bestiary; the mod disabled, the game's own back. **Her change**, at half her life, you near her: she stops and cries out, the camera goes to her and leans in, the bars come, you are held; the world bends into her bust and her hips, turning, threads of light spiralling in after it; a flash of white and she is her second half, the bends let go in a wave, her veil's streaks on her at once, solid white, never her body bare, fading slowly to clear over about four seconds; her left hand rises and light spirals into it and is a bow (Eventide's own picture, enlarged, a little over half her height), its string set with a thrum just as the spiralling sound's chord lands; behind her a clock of light, its hands slowing; at two and a half seconds a frozen chord on the very moment the hands stop, a slow ring through the world; her scream; the camera home; about twenty-eight seconds in all, held for her speech (the bending slow, she changes at about 12.5 seconds just as "At me!" is read; the day stops at about 17, the clock held behind her and the bow up while she speaks; her scream at about 27 as her last line goes), the frozen chord still landing on the moment the clock's hands stop and the bow's chord on its string's thrum. Her speech at the foot of the screen, a line at a time, the same every fight: three as the world starts to bend ("Ugh." to "...At me!"), two as the day stops ("Hush now..." and "Fleshy mortal... Our battle rages on."). **The day stopped**: a watch reads the same from that moment until she is dead or gone, then goes on; the same on a second player's. **Her veil** (BodyPhase2.png her body under it, HeadAndShoulderForPhase2.png over it): nine thin streaks at gentle slants (never steep, never all flat), crossing now and then with her body seen between them, slow (a drift half a minute), one always over the red chest bar and one over the red hip square (no red ever seen, at any moment), each with a thinner one parting from it and back, a broken one and a thin one across her waist, one slanting across her thighs, a pair low on her legs; each tipping slowly up and down through a fixed range (about a third of a radian either way, forty to fifty seconds a sway), the red still never seen; her head, hair and shoulders never taken; through the streaks only what is behind her (her streamers, her wings, the sky): no blue ghost of her dress left floating in them; their torn edges glowing softly into her and into the gap, drifting, the hip one's thin partner sliding down over her legs and back every eleven seconds; never over her face, arms, wings or streamers; on her as she dies. **Her bow**: always in the hand on the side she faces, upright beside her between acts, raised and drawn back toward her chest by her other hand when she shoots, a nocked arrow's tip still clear of the bow (the string a thread of rainbow, her fingers on it by a thread of light), its edges never cut off as it turns; her arrows Daybreak's shape in her colours, several nocked together still each an arrow (no white blaze); she turns to face you between acts, not flickering when you cross under her. **Every beam** (a laser, a ray, a rain column, a guideline, an arrow's way, the clock's hands, the blade, her grab's beam) ends soft and rounded, never cut square. **Her acts**, every other move (vanilla's own the others), never one of the last three, her first the moored volley; stand high in the arena so she hovers above its roof: as a move of hers begins she steps inside (a flash where she was and where she is), and the dawnbreak's shadow, the moored arrows and the barrier all work from there. For each act, the warning shown before it can hurt, and its sounds: the moored volley (three to seven arrows, one at a time, the string pulled back to her chest for each, sweeping across you; each one's way and its burst's reach shown as she draws it, stuck ticking in the border, each bursting thirty tiles three seconds after it sticks, so they burst one after another); the negative (the world inverted out of her and its colours going round through her next three vanilla moves, then back; while it is on, every hit on her heals her one and a half times its damage, green numbers, no red ones; a burning debuff heals her too); the cage (her curtains pulled in to a small decagon about you, meeting at its corners with no gaps, wandering five and a half seconds and holding you in; standing on its floor or the slopes beside it never hurts, its other walls hurt once it has closed, and touching them is what hurts, not being near); the constellation (six marks, two near you, guidelines for a second running through each two marks and on to the border both ways, then fifteen lasers border to border); the blinks (five, half a second apart, never on top of you, her screech, six homing bolts each; a second player sees her land each time where you do); the mirror barrier (no sphere seen: a crack in the world stands between her and you, a jagged spine with forked branches, growing outward as it takes hits (and a little with time); its panes hold the world moved, and a smaller copy inside each, a mirror in a mirror; hits on her crack it where they came through and do nothing to her, small numbers in the glass's colour, the game's not shown; at 2500 or ten seconds it comes off her at you, faster and faster,, whole, shoves you to the border and bursts, its panes flying with the world still in them; with Retro or Trippy lighting, the crack's lines alone); the ricochets (two to four, each whole way shown, three bounces, no slowing at corners); the prism at her heart (seven thin lines for 0.7 seconds, then seven rays turning about her, quicker); the lightspeed shot (twice: a line following you, two pings, then the shot, its line burning a moment); the dawnbreak (a sun rising at the border on her far side, her shadow thrown long away from it, soft-edged with its very edges in lilac, everything outside it seared at the end: stand inside the lilac lines and nothing); the refraction volley (a prism between you, seven arrows held about it, each diving down a line to you in turn); the rain of light, one of two kinds by chance: three waves of columns all across the screen about you, unevenly apart (6 to 11 tiles), each shown first the arena's height; or the starfall (nine arrows up, then three waves of three seconds: nine columns of light each slanted its own way (gently, never more than a fifth of a turn off upright), crossing about where you were, as a storm of arrows from all over the sky; each shown first as a thin line border to border, then a pulse of light down the whole of it at once, not falling, one after another at uneven times through one second, a second quiet before the next wave); the blade (a Sun Dance petal from her palm in the Everlasting Rainbow's colours, images of it trailing it wherever it moves (as she flies at you with it, as it swings); three sweeps, each arc shown before it, the world drawn in about her palm as she winds; sweeping, petals fanned behind it and a rainbow smear over the arc with a white edge, the world pushed before it; after, a line of white light along the arc and the world either side of it slid apart, healing in half a second; a shockwave off the blade; stand just outside the shown arc and it misses; she does not lunge through it; the last a whole turn about her, a wheel of petals, a ring of shockwave, the screen shaken). **Her dash** (both halves) takes you a little further out than her body (14 px every way, and where she was half a tick before), in her second half: meeting you she takes you in her free hand, light burns through you and out of your back, 75 a second for four seconds whatever your defence (dead by day), the camera close; then you are thrown limp and turning, bounced off her walls, no control till you lie still or a second and a half is out; killed by it, "<name> was seen through."; a second player sees it but not your numbers falling on their screen. **A second player** sees the same: the arrows stuck where they are, the cage, the marks, her blinks, the barrier's cracks and its bursting, the prism, the sun, the columns, the blade's sweeps. **Her coming** (Legends): as soon as she has faded up over you, before the flash and the cage, her nine lines one after another at the foot of the screen ("I was asleep." to "Unsheathe thy steel, puny one. Cry me thy havoc ballads."), you held, about 49 seconds, her hanging and breathing; the flash down onto you as the last goes; no other line of hers until a while after her name is down. Her lines show during her coming, over the black bars. **E to skip** (single player): "E Skip" in the bottom-right corner through her coming, her change and her fall; a tap does nothing, holding E fills the key and skips: her coming straight to the fight (you in the middle of her arena, her over you, her name), her change straight to her second half (veil on, the day stopped), her fall straight to the butterflies with no last lines; holding E from before a scene starts does not skip it; in multiplayer no prompt. **Her death**: her arrows gone, the bow breaking in her hand; no lines at her eyes or cracks; the border closing over about 15 seconds while she says three lines at the foot of the screen ("Ah... so this is what it feels like..." to "...not killed so easily..."), thrown out as the last goes; she bursts into butterflies, you are let go, and her last three go on ("Take flight, then..." to "We shall meet again."), no panel; forty butterflies, twenty thrown far in a ring and twenty thrown softly, a nearer cloud inside it. **Her words in her second half**: of every eighteen acts of hers, seven begin with a line of that act's at the foot of the screen (no exclamation marks); her hand and a fall still remarked on. With the game's Waves Quality Off: no bending, all the rest the same. **Reload**: Build + Reload twice in a row, and with the mod disabled: no "errors occurred while unloading" (restart tModLoader once first: the build loaded before this one still had the old unload). (PADWorkFlow §17.10.)
- [ ] §17.11. Build & Reload. **His coming** (any world, at night, the old man cursed): the camera goes to the old man and leans in, the interface off, you still able to move and nothing hurting you; he coughs twice, blood coming up, doubled over; his hand bursts in blood and bone and two arms come out of it, hands on them about his size, that take hold of him from either side; they hold him about two seconds, crushing him, the camera closer and shaking harder; he is torn in two down the middle (a splat as it tears), the arms coming out of the tear; the skull pops up out of him, growing, the hands growing with it, the halves thrown aside to lie where they fall, bleeding, and fade; his jaw drops and he roars, his name up with it; the camera home and the fight, the hands swinging in turn as the game's do. About eight seconds. Hold E in the corner: straight to the fight (the halves still thrown). Then with no old man (the Clothier's voodoo doll at night): the ground cracks, bone and dust drawn in, a red glow, two hands burst up out of it, the skull up, the red hat on him, a grey skull and grey hands; `/skeletron red` to see either look in a fight. **His body**: arms of two bones each out from under the skull (a hand far off takes its arm off his body with it), elbows up, forearms and hands hanging, fingers slowly flexing; the hands mirrored left and right, thumbs inward; open as they wind up, fists (fingers slanted in on the knuckles, the thumb wrapped round) as they swing; no gap or jump in an arm. **His hands lost** (kill both): he jerks back, his jaw snaps shut, the jaw drops wide and the skull lifts off it, a roar, a stream of skulls breathed up that slow and fall back as a rain (they hurt only in Legends), his mouth shuts, the camera home. **His death**: the skull's two halves (and the hat) and the hands' bones flying, in his look. **In Legends** (`/skeletron <move>` asks for a move by name, `/skeletron hands` kills his hands): his skull untouchable with a rim of cold light while a hand lives. Each of his hands' moves, its warning before it can hurt and its sounds: Basketball (his own skull palmed off the top, carried down to the floor, dribbled at you three times and crossed over from hand to hand; a fadeaway shot of his skull off the fingertips that comes down on you hard, and he bounces back up), Clap (the hands either side of you, then together, a shockwave going up), Tornado (both middle fingers up, a power chord, the arms spun round him, hurting all the way round for the whole spin), Solo (the middle finger by him, an air guitar, skulls spat in time to three riffs, rock on), Combo (punch, backhand, fist bump: no hand jumping across the screen), Wag (the hand turned to show its palm, the pointer up and the other fingers folded over the palm, wagged no, tsk, your shots turned aside by the finger; a slap), Flick (a finger held back beside you, yellow, its line through you; it throws you even through crowd control), Whip (a handshake, then the one hand up to the other arm's shoulder and the arm torn off there, its two bones swung back over his head, the forearm trailing, then cracked over the top and through you, snapping straight; put back without hurting on its way), Bowling (fingers hooked in his eye socket, his skull swung back and bowled along the ground at you, a strike; jump it; the hands left at the line following through as he rolls off), Seals (five hand signs, smoke round you, skulls out of it that come for you), Swoop (a hand round in a curve; stand still: caught, pinched by the head, thrown up, a punch on the way, no fall damage when you land), Purple (open hands high in purple light, then from both sides; stand still: caught, at his mouth, burned by the Diabolist's fire, held through its burst), Guns (finger guns, pew, the smoke blown off, a twirl), Snap (a snap, bone spikes up in a line to you), Rps (jan, ken, pon; rock comes down on you, paper (the palm shown) a wall of air you can jump, scissors a snip). Parry a hand's blow in each: thrown back as before, both in the clap, the fist bump and the purple grab. With his hands gone, his own: Dive (a circle out, the path marked to what it hits, never along the floor or up out of the ground, a spin, the dive; under it you die; the ground cracks him, a fifteenth of his life, never his last), Breath (six skulls breathed up, each drawing its line, then along it), Chomp (after you, jaws snapping, faster when you are), Inferno (up over you, five bursts of Diabolist fire round you, each bursting), Raise (a roar, skeletons up round you, on platforms too), Blaze (eyes on fire, a breath purple at his mouth going red from its first moment, turned slowly after you, fifty tiles at most), Spin, Volley (three spits of four homing skulls; cut away from one at speed and it loses you; the game's own homing skulls, between his moves, the same), Jaw (a spin, his jaw thrown out past you and back). Nothing left behind after any move (no hand stuck, no player held, no shot still about); after his death, nothing of his left. **Reload**: Build + Reload twice, and with the mod disabled: no errors on unloading (PADWorkFlow §17.11).
- [ ] **§17.12: readings to confirm** — (1) the quest: the Blood Shrine is "the little hut" he asks about; inside is his family's Weathered Ledger; bringing it to him ("The little hut" on the Guide) ends it, for the whole world. (2) The ledger's words and his (Lore Bible §XXVII, none of it named: a face at the foot of every page, his). (3) "Voodoo demons does not spawn": a Voodoo Demon that would come of itself comes as a plain Demon until then; summoned ones are left alone. (4) "whichever mouse is using to change wind": the off hand's key is the wind's test bind kept under its name, so whatever button you set for it carries over; the O default stays. (5) What fits the off hand: weapons, tools and fishing rods. (6) The slot just left of the trash, moving with it (PADWorkFlow §17.12).
- [ ] §17.12. Build & Reload. A new world: the Weathered Ledger in the Blood Shrine's chest, seawards; an old world: the log says where it was put (or that there was no chest). Talk to the Guide: "The little hut" (where it is); bring the ledger: he reads it, "Your other hand is free.", the option gone. Inventory: the off hand slot left of the trash in its bone frame, a faint hand in it; only a weapon or a tool goes in; with a chest open it moves down with the trash. Your Wind key (Controls, Mod Controls: "Use Off Hand"): the off hand's weapon used, its primary only (no right click), the held item back when you let go; scroll the hotbar meanwhile: back at once. Save and quit with it held: nothing lost or doubled. Die with it held: the same. Before the quest: Voodoo Demons never in the Underworld (plain Demons); after: as ever. `/guidequest`, `/guidequest done`, `/guidequest reset`. Multiplayer: one player brings the ledger, everyone gets the slot and the line.
- [ ] §17.13. Build & Reload. The Vast Ocean by day: dive into the first two sections; within seconds schools come in from the sides, little fish half their picture's size in several colours, a school mostly one or two; they gather as loose shapeless crowds, the fish at their own size, the mass churning and bulging and changing shape, never going round and round; more schools in the Schooler Zone than near the surface. Swim into one: it opens round you and closes behind, the fish nearest you darting off faster, flashing silver with a star as they turn; shoot through one: it parts for the shots; a creature going through, the same. Near the surface, scales glittering now and then. None in the rock, none over the surface, none under the Schooler Zone (the 2nd section). By night (or low in the Schooler Zone by day): each fish glowing in its own colours, a wave of it rolling through the school, soft glows about them, small lights trailing behind, the water lit a little about them. Watch the frame rate with five or six schools on screen. Leave the Vast Ocean and come back: no fish left over, new ones soon. Two players: each sees their own fish, and both frighten them. `Build + Reload` twice: no errors on unloading (PADWorkFlow §17.13).
- [ ] §17.19. Build & Reload. `/give` or kill antlions in the desert (one in fifty) for Dancing Sand; put it in the Magic Arsenal, hold a staff and select it. Hold the button: the staff to the mouth, sand blown off it toward the cursor in a narrow cone, turning with the cursor, the player facing it; a low rush, not the chime. Up close an enemy is hit very fast; at 20 tiles about twice a second; past 30, not at all; not through walls. The grains streak, slow, waver and loop where they give out, and lie on the ground they hit. Mana: 12 a second. Let go: it stops at once.
- [ ] §17.20. Build & Reload. A **new** world: the underground desert's sandstone and hardened sand flecked with gold-and-turquoise veins (the log's `Harenaeum:` line counts them); mine one with the Nightmare or Deathbringer Pickaxe (not less), smelt 3 and a Sand Block at a Furnace. `/give` the rest. **Pocket Sandstorm**: out 15 tiles, back after 20 s; a whirl of sand round it, needles flicking through what is in it. **Corosion**: left click held at a Zombie: jab, cross, uppercut (it flies up); your own arm (shirt, armour and all) thrown out long and thin with each and springing back, a faint gold smear behind it, a sand forearm to the fist about 2.75 tiles out; the back arm only out for the cross; you walking a step in and back with each blow (not while running, jumping or flying); try it in armour and with a glove on; standing in it while punching, no touch damage; at a boss, half. Right click: you scatter into sand, appear 10 tiles back, blink half a second, Sand Settling 15 s. **Scorpius Servus**: three summons, three gold tails fanned over your back; walk near a Zombie: they strike over you in turn and poison it. **Jelle's Sable Helper**: a ribbon of sand round you; three enemies near (or a boss): it pours down behind you into a ballista, shoots a bolt a second, far and fast; five seconds of nothing: back to sand. **Sidewinder**: arrows weave; shoot the ground short of a Zombie: a furrow runs to it and a spike throws it up. **The Last Grain**: the glass bottom right, empty; right click: it turns, sand falls; shots with a sand blast; when it runs out, shots of 3 and you walk slower holding it; turn it half-way: half the time. **Clay Turbah** with **Mini Sandstorm** (Magic Arsenal): a small tornado on the ground under the cursor, going to the nearest enemy; slimes and zombies carried up and thrown out, hurt when they land; a bat only hurt; a boss only hurt. Two players: the uppercut's and the spike's throws seen by both; the ballista and tails seen by both.
- [ ] §17.21. The Gilded Knight of Grace. Build & Reload (expect the sounds and pictures under `Content/NPCs/GildedKnight` and `Content/Sounds/Knight`). A **Grand** world with the kingdom (a new one, or any made since Grace): `/knight` says where his post and field are. **His post**: walk up the way onto the plateau toward the Curtain: before you reach him he is already standing on the road thirty tiles out from the wall's foot, swords home, his near hand on a hilt, breathing, facing down the field; nothing hurts him; no bar, the world's own music. Walk away a long way (150 tiles): he is gone; come back: he is there again. **His scene**: walk on up the field to about 44 tiles from him: the picture fades to black and comes back on him far off, the camera leaned in close; no music; he walks at you slowly, a ring of plate at every step (seven); stops; crosses both hands to the hilts at the opposite hips, a breath, draws both together (two rings of steel) and sweeps them up to cross before his face, a star where they cross, a ring, motes; holds; parts them in two great arcs, the Sun over the top and down ahead, the Grace over and down behind; points the Sun at you slowly on a straight arm, a glint up it to its point; his name and "Who Held the Wall" come up under him and go to the bar; the camera goes back to you and out (your feet yours half way); his music; about ten seconds. You cannot move through it. Hold E (single player): straight to the fight, his name up. **The field**: try to walk out either end: held at an invisible wall (toward the Curtain, short of the bore's mouth; outward, where the flat falls away into the way up); a second player who was outside when it went up cannot walk in, nor get in with a Rod of Discord (put back where they stood); nothing of his reaches them and nothing of theirs hurts him; die and respawn: you cannot walk back in. Jump or climb as high as you like inside it: he does not leave. **His first half** (watch each a few times, at range and up close): between moves he stalks you to a few tiles off and turns about through edge-on; the twin cut (three blows, a step with each; or four, the last both blades high, held, and down together into the ground with sparks); the leap (crouch, up over you, both blades down where you will be: the ground struck, a wave of gold running out both ways: jump it); crescents along the ground (one, or one and a rising one off the other blade); the wheel (blades out level, twice round at you, a leap back, a cut that lets a crescent go); the lancet (the Grace drawn back low, a hair of light following you, then still with a flash, the thrust: a lance of light down the line to the first wall); the votive blades (three to five blades of light rising into a row over him, turning to point at you, loosed one after another, each standing in what it strikes); a hop back; a dash. Trails off each blade only where it moves fast; afterimages in the dash, the leap and the wheel. **The parry**: parry any cut: the blade is thrown back, he rocks back a step and a half, gold over all of him and a ring turning about him; hit him during that second: nothing; then his guard and his next move. Parry a crescent or a blade of light: it is gone. **His change** at half his life: rocked back, the blades lowered, raised and crossed before his face; a roar of gold; the Sun on fire and the Grace white (embers off the one, motes off the other from now on); once round with fire and light after the blades; both into the ground and fire bursting up about him outward in turn; nothing lands on him through it. **His second half**: shorter rests; the leap's waves bigger with fire either side; two more lancets beside the first; the great crescent (an arc of fire round his front, held, burst over the whole half circle); the procession (the Sun into the ground, fire running at you along it, him down on a knee behind it); the crown (seven blades in a ring over his head, loosed one after another, then a dash and four cuts); the long blade (light grown out of the raised Grace higher than the screen, brought down ahead: anywhere under it is cut, a pillar of light where its point lands; then a band of light along the ground the length of the field: jump it); the gilded sun (the Sun raised, a sun with two rings forming over it, light streaming up into it, the screen dimming round it; brought down where you stand then: a pillar of fire). No move twice running. **His end**: the blow that would kill him: he rocks, the Sun falls from his hand turning and lies in the grass; he drives the Grace into the ground before him; one knee, the other, both hands on the hilt, head bowed; the camera leans in; quiet; he comes apart into light from his plume down, the Sun in the grass going a little after him; a flash, rays, a ring; the Gilded Spur and Healing Potions where he was. `/knight`: beaten. Walk off and back: his post stands empty. **The spur**: ring it off the field (or right at its ends): "It rings, and nothing answers here." over you, nothing happens (on a client as well, from the server). On the field: the picture goes black and he is at his post under it, and his scene plays (shorter if you are close to his post: he walks fewer steps, never into you). Ring it while he is about: nothing. `/knight reset`: never beaten, his post his again. **A world with no kingdom**: `/give` a Gilded Spur and ring it anywhere on the surface: he comes from off to one side of you, his field about you both. **Numbers** (normal world): his bar 9780 (expert 15648, master 23472; Legends half as much again); his cuts about 46 a blow before your defence. **Boss Checklist** (if you use it): his page after Deerclops, him standing, the spur as what calls him. **Two players**: both see his scene if both are on the field; each is cut by what reaches them; both can hurt him (from the client too); a parry by the client turns him as it does in a world of one (he rocks back, the ward); the spur rung by either works; his moves, shots and death the same on both screens. **Reload**: Build + Reload twice in a row: no errors on unloading. (PADWorkFlow §17.21.)
- [ ] §17.22. The Gilded Knight, slower (this supersedes §17.21's damage numbers and its end). Build & Reload. **Between his moves**: each time he walks at you for one to three seconds, his armour ringing, and stops within reach of his first cut (about seven tiles off) before the next move (only the crown's dash and four cuts follow it at once); after a parry's step back, half a second; after his scene, a second and a half. **Damage** (normal world): a cut about 39 before your defence. **The stun**: parry his cuts three times (across the fight, any cuts): the first two, his step back and the gold ward as before; the third, he is rocked back and goes down on one knee, blades in the turf, head hanging, swaying, stars over him, for five seconds; hit him then: the first hit does three times the damage and he gets up at once; leave him: he gets up after five seconds, slowly, then goes on. **His end**: about sixteen and a half seconds and not a sound: the Sun falls slowly and lies in the grass; the Grace planted at two seconds; both knees by four; head bowed; from seven and a half seconds golden smoke builds on him and his plate warms to gold; at twelve seconds he comes apart into golden grains that hold his shape a moment, then rise and drift off on the wind, the Sun in the grass after him; the Gilded Spur and Healing Potions where he knelt. **The wheel**: its two turns now cut you standing (they passed over your head). **Two players**: the stun and the riposte from either. (PADWorkFlow §17.22.)
